# Stalwart 1.0.1 upgrade execution outcome

Status: Task 1, 2026-10-30. The hook inventory and merge prediction (part B) come from a dry
merge; the interface sheet (part C) was checked against the v1.0.1 source; the reduced
baseline (part A) is `tests/qualification/baseline-0.16.27.json`. The primary's decisions
are under "Task 1 rulings". The real merge's conflict ledger follows below.

Refs:
- Fork tip `main` = `9a7574203e9821a8313239f51a080c0657672be9` (the merge of PR #66).
- Integration branch `stalwart-1-upgrade` = `0ddbbc1a3e8650947f3df94d6ad2ae4674789854`; its
  14 commits over `9a7574203` touch only `docs/superpowers/`.
- Old upstream base `v0.16.27` = `aa47d8ef7bfba7200d3293198ac2592747b20e73` (the merge base).
- Target `v1.0.1` = `23b69755cb3cd8ecd952fe0443c5ac067a506184`, fetched by URL and verified;
  its parent `v1.0.0` = `47df4c0baeb88d83424dfda6aa7cf6040706999e` is present as a commit,
  not as a local tag.
- Dry-merge result tree `df7eab1ff9a13c78e94c828a735c8cf04eee99c9`, from
  `git merge-tree --write-tree --messages --name-only 0ddbbc1a3 v1.0.1` (exit 1).

### Merge prediction

The dry merge reports 134 conflicted paths: 106 content conflicts, 27 modify/delete
conflicts and 1 add/add conflict. It reports no rename conflict. This matches the design's
count for `5b914ddb5`.

Git's rename detection followed two upstream renames of fork-modified files:
`services/src/task_manager/alarm.rs` became `task_manager/tasks/alarm.rs` and merged clean;
`task_manager/maintenance.rs` became `task_manager/tasks/maintenance.rs` and conflicts there.
That conflict is counted with the content conflicts below. The other two renames git reports
(`tests/src/jmap/files/mod.rs` to `crates/sieve/src/runtime/message/mod.rs` and
`tests/src/cluster/mod.rs` to `crates/telemetry/src/lib.rs`) are false pairings. Neither
source is fork-modified, so neither carries a hook.

**Add/add conflict (1):** `crates/groupware/src/calendar/sequence.rs`. The fork's file
holds the SEQUENCE floor and reconciliation (`za_sequence_floor`, `za_reconcile_sequences`).
v1.0.1 adds its own file of that name with new SEQUENCE logic (`ICalendarSequence`:
`has_scheduling_changes`, `increment_sequence`, and a check that sequences are never
lowered). Both are needed, so the fork module must be renamed. The floor also has to be
re-read against upstream's new logic (Task 4).

**Rename conflicts:** none.

**Paths the merge deletes** (dispositions are in "Deleted paths and their disposition"):
- Under `tests/`: 541 of the fork's 599 files. 520 go without a conflict; the 21
  modify/delete conflicts above make up the rest. v1.0.1 ships no `tests/` directory, so
  every upstream-origin test file goes. The 58 that stay are fork-owned.
- Outside `tests/`: 148 paths. 142 go without a conflict; the 6 modify/delete conflicts
  above make up the rest.

The merge also removes `tests` and `crates/migration` from the root workspace `members`
without a conflict, and keeps the fork's `crates/vault` member. Restoring `tests/` does not
restore its membership, so Task 2 must add `tests` back.

**Upstream changes the merge applies cleanly to fork-modified files outside `crates/`**
(`git diff 0ddbbc1a3 df7eab1ff -- .github/ .gitignore`):
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: the Docker image tags gain `type=semver,pattern=v{{major}}`
  (near `:275`, after `v{{major}}.{{minor}}`). That is a release-identity change (C11),
  for **Task 9**: decide whether the fork's images take a major-only tag.
- `.gitignore`: `/target` becomes `target/`. Harmless; no owner needed.

### Hook inventory

A hook is the fork's change to an upstream-owned runtime file, meaning one that exists at
`v0.16.27`. The unit counted is a fork diff hunk at git's default three lines of context
that changes code. Hunks that only change `use` lines are counted separately, beside each
file's category, and are not hooks. Fork-owned new files are not hooks; the module
declarations and calls that reach them from upstream files are.

Under `crates/` there are **485 hooks in 154 files**, plus 81 import-only hunks. 483 are
diff hunks. The other 2 are ruling 28's C3 hooks: the raw Authorization value that
0.16.27 put into two errors of `http/src/auth/authenticate.rs`, which the fork removed. They
sit inside that file's first hunk and get rows of their own (1a and 1b). The root
`Cargo.toml` adds 3 more (Task 2; listed at the end of the file table), for 488 in all.

Categories, per hook:
- **(a)** merges clean and needs no re-read. Two kinds: `a`, the file is unchanged
  upstream (1 hook); and `a*`, the file changed upstream elsewhere but not in the hook's
  enclosing item (41 hooks).
- **(b)** the file changed upstream and merges clean in text, but upstream changed the
  hook's enclosing item, or lines within six of it. Needs a re-read (177 hooks).
- **(c)** a textual conflict, with the hook's upstream item still in place (212 hooks).
- **(d)** the target was deleted, split, moved or renamed (54 hooks):
  - 31 in the 5 modify/delete files;
  - 5 in the 2 renamed files (4 clean, 1 in conflict);
  - 18 in 8 surviving files whose enclosing function or item is gone from that file in
    v1.0.1 (17 of them also conflict, 1 merges clean).

  The "how" note on each (d) row keeps a moved target apart from a textual conflict.

The region test is textual. A hook's region runs from its enclosing item's line to the
next item's line in the 0.16.27 file. An `a*` hook can still break on a signature that
changed elsewhere; the crate-level `cargo check` that closes each of Tasks 2 to 6 is the
backstop for that. Conflict membership was found by mapping all 285 conflict blocks of the
dry merge onto the fork's lines, and every block maps to at least one fork hunk.

Hooks and files by owner (owner from the plan's File ownership table; a shared file is
listed under its first-named owner):

| Task 2 | 17 | 28 | 6 | 7 | 13 | 2 |
| Task 3 | 31 | 118 | 19 | 38 | 52 | 9 |
| Task 4 | 27 | 127 | 8 | 47 | 49 | 23 |
| Task 5 | 67 | 188 | 9 | 80 | 90 | 9 |
| Task 6 | 9 | 21 | 0 | 4 | 6 | 11 |
| Task 9 (identity; compiles in Task 3) | 3 | 3 | 0 | 1 | 2 | 0 |
| **total** | **154** | **485** | **42** | **177** | **212** | **54** |

Files by their worst hook: 1 `a`, 11 `a*`, 44 `b`, 83 `c`, 15 `d`.

Shared files need an explicit handoff, as the plan says. The owner column names both owners
where the fork's hunks serve two tasks: the issue #20 masking against the key-session
masking in the IMAP, POP3 and SMTP session files; the shares route in
`http/src/api/mod.rs`; the upload gate in `http/src/request.rs`; and the calendar refusal in
`jmap/src/api/request.rs`.

#### Per-hook rows for categories (b), (c) and (d)

One row per hook, grouped by file:
- "Fork site" is the hook's first changed line at `9a7574203`.
- "Upstream item" is the nearest enclosing or preceding item in the 0.16.27 file.
- "v1.0.1 site" is that item's line in `v1.0.1-src/`, always an implementation, never a
  trait method's declaration. 179 rows first cited a declaration; all now cite the impl,
  in another file of the crate where the impl lives there. Where the hook's own
  unchanged context line occurs exactly once in that impl, the site adds the insertion
  point: "inserted after :N" (the context line just above the hunk) or "inserted before
  :N" (the one just below). 219 rows carry one. For (d) hooks, and R36's two rows in
  `http/src/auth/oauth/auth.rs`, the site was found by hand.
- The preserve text is the hook's own comment where it has one; for (d) rows and thin
  hunks it was written by hand.

#### Findings for the primary

Each of these hooks has an owner, but its disposition needs a decision or a design note
before a writer ports it.

1. **The UID lease has lost its storage class.**
   - v1.0.1 removed `ValueClass::InMemory` and `InMemoryClass` (compare
     `v1.0.1-src/crates/store/src/write/mod.rs:924` with 0.16.27's `ValueClass`). Its
     default in-memory store is now a process-local `LocalStore`
     (`v1.0.1-src/crates/store/src/build/memory.rs:20`).
   - The fork's lease (`crates/common/src/za/lease.rs`) writes `ValueClass::InMemory` keys
     into the data store, and every fenced fan-out item write asserts them. The prefix
     `ZA_UID_LEASE_PREFIX`, `KV_LOCK_ZA_UID`, the purge skip in `dispatch/lookup.rs` and
     the sweep in `tasks/maintenance.rs` all hang on that class.
   - v1.0.1 adds its own `try_lease`, `renew_lease` and `release_lease`
     (`dispatch/lookup.rs:314-389`), but on the in-memory store, which cannot be asserted
     inside a data-store batch.
   - C7 keeps UID leases, and the design does not say where they live at 1.0 (Tasks 3, 4
     and 2).
2. **SEQUENCE.** Upstream's new `calendar/sequence.rs` takes the fork module's name and
   overlaps its purpose (see the add/add conflict). Rename the fork module and re-read the
   floor against `ICalendarSequence` (Task 4).
3. **DAV sync tokens now page.** The fork's foreign-calendar tombstone authorization and its
   refusal of a truncated change set in `propfind.rs` assume 0.16's `Query::Since` and
   truncation. v1.0.1 uses `SyncToken::{State, ChangesPage}` over `Query::Range(id,
   snapshot)` (`propfind/get.rs:117-137`), and the fork's `vanished_with_change_id`
   (`store/src/query/log.rs`) reads with `Query::Since`. Re-derive both (Task 4).
4. **The PROPFIND unseal has two paths.** 1.0 loads archives in batches
   (`ArchiveLoader::load`, `propfind/load.rs:132`) and answers some properties from the
   resource cache (`is_cached`). The fork's per-item `za_archive_view` must cover both, and
   must run before `calendar_event_view` (`propfind/mod.rs:540`) (Task 4).
5. **IMAP route check versus the cache read.** `sync_view` (`imap/src/core/view.rs:793`)
   replaces `synchronize_messages`, and its callers read `MessageStoreCache` before calling
   it. R34's check sat inside `synchronize_messages`, before any read (Task 5).
6. **A renamed path helper.** `groupware::encode_path_segment` became
   `common::storage::dav::canonical_path_segment`. The fork calls the old one in
   `crates/http/src/api/shares.rs` and `propfind.rs` (Task 4).
7. **Workspace membership.** The root `Cargo.toml` silently loses `tests` (see Merge
   prediction) (Task 2).
8. **SEL-tagged code written by the fork.** `Store::is_read_replica`
   (`crates/store/src/lib.rs:726-738`) contains a `cfg(feature = "enterprise")` arm
   wrapped in an SEL snippet marker, which the fork wrote. It mirrors the arm in
   upstream's `is_sql()`. It needs a licence disposition before it is carried forward. It
   compiles out of the OSS product either way.
9. **No OSS LLM path.** The LLM redaction hook is dropped with its SEL file. The harness
   builds with `enterprise`, so its binary contains the SEL LLM analysis
   (`spam-filter/src/analysis/ai/`) without the fork's redaction. No `za` test configures
   an LLM. Record this as a C3 limit, not a port.
10. **R62's scans.** The design gives R62 and R63 the "subject deleted" disposition because
    `Store::index`'s chunk loop is gone. R62's orphan-key scans (`za_has_email_search_terms`
    and `za_has_calendar_search_terms` over `has_account_search_terms`) are a separate
    piece. Decide whether to keep them over 1.0's search key layout (Task 6).
11. **The move route's payload bound.** `accounted_input_bytes` counted `Params`. v1.0.1's
    `MergeFnc` is a closure with no countable inputs, so the bound must be re-derived
    (Task 6, consumed by Task 4).
12. **Stored traces.** The trace binary serializer (`trc/src/serializers/binary.rs`) is
    gone. Stored traces are now span documents in the search index (`build_span_document`,
    `common/src/telemetry/tracers/store.rs:235`). Confirm that redacted values persist as
    redacted (C3; Tasks 3 and 6).
13. **The ACL refresh.** `common/src/sharing/acl.rs` (`refresh_acls`) is gone; grants now
    come from `common/src/sharing/grants.rs` and the index's `acl_grant` and `acl_revoke`
    keys. C7's own-id permission reads need a re-read (Task 4).
14. **Imports of deleted modules.** Fork-owned files import modules v1.0.1 deleted.
    `groupware/src/calendar/seal/{collection,event,item}.rs` use `DeadProperty`;
    `common/src/auth/vault.rs` and `email/src/message/za/ingest.rs` match on
    `Task::IndexDocument`; `common/src/za/test_hooks.rs` references
    `task_manager::index`. These are not hooks, but their owners will meet them first.

#### Upstream test hooks (for Task 2)

At `v0.16.27`, 63 upstream files hold 144 `feature = "test_mode"` sites: attributes,
`cfg_attr`s and `cfg!` expressions. `v1.0.1` has none. Each one went one of three ways:
- it became `cfg(stalwart_tests)`, `cfg(not(stalwart_tests))`, `cfg(any(test,
  stalwart_tests))` or a variant with `dev_mode`;
- it moved into a private `test_support` slot;
- it went away.

No fork build sets `stalwart_tests`, so after the merge every one is dead: its test branch
never compiles and its production branch always does. An `any(test, stalwart_tests)` site
compiles only in its own crate's unit tests. It never compiles in the `tests` crate's build
of that crate, which is where the integration suites run.

Totals by proposed disposition:
- restore under `test_mode`: 121;
- not needed by the retained suite: 11;
- subject deleted: 10;
- in an SEL-only file: 2.

How the consumers were found: a grep of the fork's `tests/src` for each hook's identifier or
trigger string (`STS_TEST_POLICY`, `badtls`, `dmarc-`, `-debug`, `skip_checks`, `__sleep`,
port `9925`, `shard_index`, `DNS_RECORDS` and so on). A hook with no identifier was matched
by its subject, marked "(by subject)". Part A's baseline holds the per-test identifiers.

The restore rule for Task 2:
- Where the site survives, turn its `stalwart_tests` back into `feature = "test_mode"`
  (keeping `dev_mode` and `test` where 1.0.1 has them). This is a one-line edit in an
  upstream file, as at 0.16.27. Each such edit is a Task 2 hook of its own.
- Where v1.0.1 moved the code into a private slot or removed it, write a fork-owned
  replacement from the 0.16.27 behaviour.

Flags:
- **Silent changes** that a passing suite would not show:
  - registry queries now default to descending id order (`jmap/src/registry/query.rs`);
  - loopback push URLs are no longer exempt (`jmap/src/push/set.rs`);
  - the DNS provider domain has no test override (`common/src/network/dns/records.rs`);
  - ACME retries and backoff lost their test values (`tasks/acme.rs`).
- **The test licence key** sits in an SEL-only file (`common/src/enterprise/config.rs`).
  Restoring it edits SEL code, so it needs a decision: either the harness runs without a
  licence, or the SEL disposition allows the cfg edit.
- **`any(test, stalwart_tests)` in `smtp`** (`badtls`, the DNSSEC mock): these keep
  `cfg(test)`, which hides the loss from `cargo test -p smtp`. The integration suites
  (`smtp::outbound::tls`, `smtp::outbound::dane`) still lose them.

### Moved hook targets: check against the known answer

The brief's call-site counts equal the number of lines that mention `za_` in the fork's
version of each file (`git show 9a7574203:<file> | grep -cE '\bza_'`). That measure
reproduces all four exactly. It undercounts the port surface, so the table also gives the
code hunks.

| 0.16.27 file | Brief | `za_` lines found | Code hunks | Replacement in v1.0.1 | Agrees |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `crates/dav/src/common/propfind.rs` | 15 | 15 | 19 | `crates/dav/src/common/propfind/{data,dead,get,item,load,mod}.rs` | yes |
| `crates/imap/src/op/fetch.rs` | 7 | 7 | 5 | `crates/imap/src/op/fetch/{mod,section,source,structure}.rs` | yes |
| `crates/imap/src/core/message.rs` | 1 | 1 | 1 | `crates/imap/src/core/view.rs` (`sync_view`, :793) | yes |
| `crates/spam-filter/src/analysis/llm.rs` | 1 | 1 | 1 | `crates/spam-filter/src/analysis/ai/` (SEL), which calls `crates/common/src/enterprise/ai/` (SEL) | yes, with the analysis now in `spam-filter/src/analysis/ai/` |

Notes on the four:
- `propfind.rs` also carries hunks with no `za_` token. These are the sync-token tombstone
  helpers (`authorize_calendar_tombstones`, `foreign_calendar_contexts`,
  `invalid_calendar_sync_token`), the subtree filtering, the truncation refusal, the
  multiget collection check and the `CalendarQueryHandler` id parameters.
- The 0.16.27 `llm.rs` was already SEL-only (`LicenseRef-SEL`, behind
  `cfg(feature = "enterprise")`), so the fork's hook sat in SEL code. Its disposition is
  "SEL-only on both sides, not ported".

|---|---|---|---|---|
| `crates/services/src/task_manager/index.rs` (modify/delete; 4 `za_` lines) | 5 | deleted; the index tasks became the index queue | `crates/services/src/index_queue/{document,index}.rs` | Task 6 |

### Deleted paths and their disposition

**Under `tests/`:** the counts agree with the brief. Upstream deletes 541 of the fork's 599
files: 520 without a conflict and 21 as modify/delete conflicts. These are every
upstream-origin file at 0.16.27; v1.0.1 has no `tests/`. The fork-side checkout
(`git checkout HEAD -- tests/ .github/workflows/test.yml`) restores all of them.
- Restoring `tests/` does not restore the root workspace member; see the Merge prediction.
- Ruling 3 commits `tests/qualification/baseline-0.16.27.json` before the merge, so HEAD's
  `tests/` will hold 600 files when the real merge runs. Upstream's 541 deletions are
  unchanged.

**Outside `tests/`:** 148 paths.

| Path | How deleted | Disposition | Flag |
|---|---|---|---|
| `.github/workflows/test.yml` | modify/delete | restored from the fork side at the merge; Task 8 rewrites it | test support (CI) |

| `crates/spam-filter/src/analysis/llm.rs` | modify/delete | SEL-only (both sides); moved to `crates/spam-filter/src/analysis/ai/`; 1 hook, a disposition for Task 5 | |

| `crates/common/src/sharing/acl.rs` | clean | replaced: grants in `crates/common/src/sharing/grants.rs` and the index's ACL keys (`rebuild_shares`, `grantee_cleanup`); no hook, re-read by Task 4 | |

| `crates/services/src/task_manager/{alarm,dkim,dns,maintenance,merge_threads,restore_item,spam_classifier}.rs` (7) | clean (renames) | replaced at `crates/services/src/task_manager/tasks/<same name>.rs`; `alarm` (4 hooks, Task 4) and `maintenance` (1 hook, Task 6) carry hooks | |
| `crates/services/src/task_manager/{acme,destroy_account,imip,report}.rs` (4) | clean | replaced at `crates/services/src/task_manager/tasks/<same name>.rs` (similarity 15%, 28%, 40%, 37%, below git's rename threshold); no hook | |
| `crates/store/src/search/bm_u32.rs`, `bm_u64.rs` | clean | subject replaced: the internal search store's bitmap caches gave way to `crates/store/src/search/{term,leaf,split,local,codec}.rs`; no hook | **index** |
| `crates/trc/src/serializers/binary.rs` | clean | subject replaced: stored traces are span documents (`crates/common/src/telemetry/tracers/store.rs:235`); no hook, see finding 12 | |
| `crates/types/src/dead_property.rs` | clean | replaced by `crates/types/src/metadata/` and `Collection::Metadata`; no hook, but `groupware/src/calendar/seal/` imports it, Task 4 | **metadata** |
| `crates/utils/src/codec/base32_custom.rs` | clean | replaced by `encodify` (`base32::STALWART`); its inline test module is listed in the next section | |
| `crates/utils/src/codec/leb128.rs` | clean | moved to `crates/utils/src/codec/leb128/mod.rs` | |
| `resources/scripts/minify_html.sh` | clean | subject deleted upstream (developer tooling for the HTML templates; no build or test reads it) | |
| `crates/dav-proto/resources/requests/*` (94), `crates/dav-proto/resources/responses/*` (20) | clean | test support for dav-proto's 0.16.27 inline tests (`src/requests/mod.rs:154`, `src/responses/mod.rs:687` read them); upstream moved those tests to private slots; Task 2 restores the fixtures with the tests | **test support** |

The counts reconcile: 6 modify/delete plus 142 clean (114 dav-proto fixtures, 4 in
`common`, 1 in `email`, 1 in `imap-proto`, 4 in `migration`, 11 in `services`, 2 in
`store`, 1 in `trc`, 1 in `types`, 2 in `utils`, 1 under `resources/`).

Every other fixture path that an inline test reads survives the merge: the `resources/schema/`
and `resources/html-templates/` files. The `resources/email-templates/` path in
`utils/src/template.rs` sits in a commented-out test.

### Inline test modules the merge removes

0.16.27 has 132 upstream `cfg(test)` modules in `crates/`, and the merge removes all of
them:
- 129 are removed from files that survive;
- 2 go with their deleted files;
- 1 survives only inside the conflicted `http/src/request.rs`, which v1.0.1 replaced with a
  slot.

They hold 282 active test functions outside the SEL-only crates, plus 183 in the 22 modules
of `scim` (8) and `scim-proto` (14), which are dispositioned, not restored. 127 modules
became private slots in the same file; the other 5 have subjects that moved. Five modules
are not named `tests` (`serde_impl`, and `test` four times); Task 2 keeps their names.
Together with the fork's own 232 unit tests (10 in upstream files, 222 in fork-owned files),
the totals reconcile with the baseline's 514 inline tests outside the SEL-only crates. The
baseline JSON records the restored-file name for each module.

### Private-test slots in v1.0.1

v1.0.1 has **562** `#[path = "…/stalwart-tests/…"]` attributes, all under
`stalwart-tests/unit/`. They split by the `cfg` on the module:
- **Test-only, 535:** the guard includes `test`.
  - 532 use `all(test, stalwart_tests)`;
  - 1 uses `all(test, stalwart_tests, any(feature = "postgres", feature = "mysql"))`;
  - 1 uses `all(test, stalwart_tests, feature = "postgres")`;
  - 1, `crates/store/src/write/mod.rs:442` (`mod contention_tests`), uses
    `all(test, stalwart_tests, any(feature = "rocks", feature = "postgres", feature =
    "mysql", feature = "foundation"))` spread over ten lines.
- **Support, 27:** the guard has `stalwart_tests` without `test`, so these modules would
  compile into non-test builds. There are 25 plain `cfg(stalwart_tests)`, plus
  `store/src/backend/mod.rs:36` (`sql_fault`, with postgres or mysql) and `:166`
  (`block_hook`, with rocks or sqlite). They are `test_support`, `probe`, `ops`,
  `rocksdb_skips`, `reference`, `sql_fault` and `block_hook` modules in `common`,
  `services`, `smtp`, `spam-filter`, `store` and `utils`.

The brief's 534 + 27 = 561 misses `contention_tests`: a one-line match on the `cfg` finds
no `test` in it. With it the split is 535 + 27 = 562. No build of this fork sets
`stalwart_tests`, so none of the 562 modules compiles.

Relative to `v1.0.0` (560 slots; one commit apart), v1.0.1 adds 3 slots and removes 1:

| Module | Change | What else changed in v1.0.1 | Review in |
|---|---|---|---|
| `crates/http/src/api/mod.rs:437` | new slot (`unit/http/src/api/tests.rs`) | only the slot | Task 3, with Task 4: the router the fork extends with `vault`, `contacts` and `shares` |
| `crates/store/src/registry/local.rs:134` | new slot (`unit/store/src/registry/local/tests.rs`) | the local data-store file is now written with mode 0600; a process nonce was added | Task 2 (the harness keeps `RegistryStore` helpers); Task 8 for fresh-store creation; no Task 3 to 6 hook touches it |
| `crates/telemetry/src/exporter/spans.rs:127` | slot moved from `crates/common/src/telemetry/tracers/spans.rs` | file moved into the new `telemetry` crate | Task 3 (C3, stored trace content), with Task 6 (span documents in the index) |

### Interface sheet

Task 1, part C (2026-10-30). This sheet freezes the interfaces that Tasks 2–6 share, before
the primary assigns writers. It was checked against the integration worktree at `0ddbbc1a3`
(code identical to `9a7574203`) and the read-only extraction of `v1.0.1` (`23b69755c`).

How to read it:
- Crate paths are relative to `crates/`, as in the plan. `tests/...` paths are from the
  repository root. "Fork" lines are the worktree at 0.16.27; "1.0.1" lines are `v1.0.1-src/`.
- Everything here is frozen, except items marked **Proposed — primary to rule**. Each of those
  names one alternative.
- "One-line insertion" means a call into a fork-owned module from an upstream file, as the
  design's principle requires. Where upstream's mechanism works unchanged, the sheet says so.
- Contracts are restated in one line each, from the design's C1–C11 table.

#### Overview

| # | Adaptation | Producer | Consumers | Contracts |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Keyless `Authenticated` | Task 3 | Tasks 3, 4, 5 | C1, C2, C3, C4 |
| 2 | Request and connection key ownership | Task 3 | Tasks 4, 5, 6 | C2, C3, C4 |
| 3 | Account epoch and classification | Task 3 | Tasks 4, 5, 6 | C1, C3, C4, C9 |
| 4 | Split calendar views | Task 4 | Tasks 5 (contacts redelivery reads), 6 | C6, C7, C9 |
| 5 | Metadata and extras mapping | Task 4 (calendars), Task 6 (IMAP/JMAP metadata) | Tasks 6, 7 | C6, C9, C3 |
| 6 | Pending-ID fences | Task 5 (mail), Task 4 (calendar) | Task 6 | C5, C7, C8 |
| 7 | Search and snapshot inputs | Task 6 | Task 7 | C3, C5, C8 |
| 8 | Harness | Task 2 | Tasks 3–7 | C10, C11 |

#### 1. Keyless `Authenticated`

**Fork at 0.16.27**
- `Server::authenticate(&self, req: &AuthRequest) -> trc::Result<AccessToken>`
  (`common/src/auth/authentication.rs:49-53`) wraps
  `authenticate_with_keys(&self, req: &AuthRequest) -> trc::Result<(AccessToken, Option<Arc<SessionKeys>>)>`
  (`:61-77`). That function runs `route_auth_request` (`:120`), asserts
  `Permission::Authenticate`, then detaches the keys: `token.session_keys().cloned()` and
  `token.without_session_keys()` (`:70-71`).
- Keys come from two places inside `route_auth_request`:
  - the marker diversion: `credential.secret == ZA_MARKER` → `za_verify_password` →
    `ZaVerification::{Valid(keys), Invalid, MissingMfaToken, NoRecord}` (`:264-286`);
  - the app-password wrap: `za_open_app_wrap` after `validate_credential`'s success
    (`:546-568`).
  Both attach with `AccessToken::with_session_keys` (`:306-309`, `:574-577`).
- Directory refusals: `za_refuse_directory_login` (call `:220-226`, definition `:622`) and
  `za_refuse_directory_account` (Basic `:242-243`, Bearer `:428-429`, definition `:644`).
- The impersonation marker `AccessToken::with_za_impersonation` (`:150`, `:372`).
- The failure path `authentication_failure` (`:82-116`) skips the enumeration delay under
  `cfg(not(feature = "test_mode"))` (`:89`).
- Callers of `authenticate_with_keys`: `http/src/auth/authenticate.rs:149`,
  `http/src/api/vault.rs:589`, `imap/src/op/authenticate.rs:74`,
  `pop3/src/op/authenticate.rs:68`. Callers of `authenticate`: SMTP
  (`smtp/src/inbound/auth.rs:114`), ManageSieve (`managesieve/src/op/authenticate.rs:76`),
  OAuth (`http/src/auth/oauth/auth.rs:287`, `:401`).

**1.0.1**
- `pub struct Authenticated { pub access_token: AccessToken, pub expires_in: Option<u64> }`
  (`common/src/auth/authentication.rs:35-38`).
- `pub async fn authenticate(&self, req: &AuthRequest) -> trc::Result<Authenticated>`
  (`:52-94`), the same as v1.0.0 (`47df4c0ba`, line 52). It dispatches:
  - `authenticate_basic(&self, req, username: &str, secret: &str, mfa_token: Option<&str>) -> trc::Result<AccessToken>`
    (`:123`): recovery admin `:133-183`; app passwords `AppPassword::parse` `:199` →
    `validate_credential` (`:544`, scoped token `:621`); the directory branch `:228-250`
    with `assert_account_directory` at `:246`; the internal password
    `verify_mfa_secret_hash(&self.inner.data.password_verifier, …)` at `:269`; the master
    user `:322-337` (`AccessToken::new_maybe_invalid`, `:337`);
  - `authenticate_bearer(&self, req, username: Option<&str>, token: &str) -> trc::Result<Authenticated>`
    (`:357`): API keys, internal OAuth (`TOKEN_HEADER` → `authenticate_oauth_token`, `:493`),
    and the external directory with `assert_account_directory` at `:404`.
  It then asserts `Permission::Authenticate` (`:74`). On error it waits 50–500 ms under
  `#[cfg(not(stalwart_tests))]` (`:83-89`) and calls `auth_failure` (`:91`, defined at `:96`).
- `AccessToken` gained `token_scope: Option<TokenScope>` (`common/src/auth/mod.rs:182-186`),
  with `TokenScope::{Grant { grant_id, scopes, client }, External { scopes }}`
  (`:188-200`) and `assert_allows_oauth_scopes(self, OAuthScopes) -> trc::Result<Self>`
  (`common/src/auth/access_token.rs:503`).
- Callers: `http/src/auth/authenticate.rs:106`, `http/src/auth/oauth/auth.rs:609`,
  `imap/src/op/authenticate.rs:140`, `pop3/src/op/authenticate.rs:118`,
  `smtp/src/inbound/auth.rs:226`, `managesieve/src/op/authenticate.rs:93`.
- Plan and design citations checked: the signature, "unchanged in 1.0.1", and the Task 3
  sites `authentication.rs:246-250, 467` all match. Two notes:
  - the plan's "preserve" refers to upstream's signature; the fork's own 0.16 signature
    returns `AccessToken`;
  - since `stalwart_tests` is never set, the 1.0.1 delay runs in every test build unless
    Task 2 regates it (open point 1.1).

**Frozen adaptation**
- `Server::authenticate` keeps upstream's signature and returns a keyless `Authenticated`
  on every protocol.
- Fork-owned sibling, as at 0.16:
  `pub async fn authenticate_with_keys(&self, req: &AuthRequest) -> trc::Result<(Authenticated, Option<Arc<SessionKeys>>)>`.
  It holds upstream's body of `authenticate` unchanged, plus one line after the permission
  assertion. That line detaches the keys from `authenticated.access_token`.
  `authenticate` becomes `self.authenticate_with_keys(req).await.map(|(a, _)| a)`.
  `expires_in` and the scope pass through untouched.
- Keys ride the outer `AccessToken` (item 2) from `authenticate_basic` and
  `validate_credential` up to the detach. `authenticate_bearer` never yields keys.
- Insertion sites at 1.0.1:
  - the marker branch at `:269`: `if credential.secret == ZA_MARKER { za_verify_password(…) } else { upstream }`;
  - `za_open_app_wrap` after `validate_credential` succeeds, before `:621`;
  - `za_refuse_directory_login` before `directory.authenticate` (`:228-243`);
  - `za_refuse_directory_account` after `directory.authenticate` returns and before
    `assert_account_directory` (`:246`), and before `:404` in the Bearer arm;
  - the R35 marker on both impersonation returns (`:156-158`, `:337`);
  - `NoRecord` keeps `ZA_REASON_VAULT_MISSING` and the terminal Account-before-vault order.
- **Producer:** Task 3. **Consumers:** Task 3 (HTTP, IMAP, POP3, vault and contacts
  endpoints), Task 4 (`http/src/api/shares.rs` uses the vault endpoints' verification
  helpers `za_verify_primary` and `za_reread_verified`), Task 5 (the session predicates of
  item 2).
- **Contracts:**
  - C1: the vault record alone decides login; every vault write keeps its revision CAS and
    `za_commit`/`za_vault_commit` invalidation.
  - C2: the common result is keyless; only protocol layers hold keys.
  - C3: no credential or key reaches an error, trace or cache key.
  - C4: ordinary accounts take upstream's path; scope, expiry and grant revocation stay
    upstream's.

**Open points**
- 1.1 **Proposed — primary to rule:** Task 2 regates the enumeration delay
  (`authentication.rs:83-89`) as `#[cfg(not(any(stalwart_tests, feature = "test_mode")))]`.
  This restores the 0.16 test timing (fork `:89`) and touches one attribute.
  Alternative: keep the delay in tests and accept 50–500 ms per failed login in the suites.

#### 2. Request and connection key ownership

**Fork at 0.16.27**
- The outer `AccessToken` carries `session_keys: Option<Arc<vault::session::SessionKeys>>`
  and `za_impersonated: bool` (`common/src/auth/mod.rs:141-151`). It has the methods
  `with_session_keys`, `without_session_keys`, `with_za_impersonation`, `za_is_impersonated`,
  `session_keys` and `za_keys_for(account_id)` (`common/src/auth/access_token.rs:471-505`).
  `AccessTokenInner` holds no key.
- HTTP:
  - `authenticate_headers` returns `trc::Result<(Option<InFlight>, AccessToken)>`
    (`http/src/auth/authenticate.rs:16-101`); the uncached path is `authenticate_uncached`
    (`:106-246`).
  - The cache is `http_auth: Cache<[u8; 32], HttpAuthCache>`, keyed by
    `Caches::za_fingerprint(token)`, a keyed BLAKE3 (`common/src/lib.rs:182`, `:295`).
  - The fork added `generation: u64` and `za_impersonated: bool` to `HttpAuthCache`
    (`lib.rs:281-291`).
  - Resident keys are `za_keys: vault::cache::KeyCache` (`lib.rs:184`). `za_insert_keys` and
    `za_sweep_keys` evict the HTTP entry by fingerprint (`lib.rs:302-321`).
  - The test pause hooks are in `http/src/auth/authenticate.rs:257-330` (`za_test`).
- Strips: `without_session_keys()` on EventSource and WebSocket (`http/src/request.rs:315`,
  `:327`).
- IMAP: `SessionData::refresh_access_token` carries the keys and the R35 marker
  (`imap/src/core/mod.rs:208-229`). The predicates are `Session::za_key_session` (`:199`)
  and `SessionData::za_key_session` (`:232`).
- POP3: `State::za_key_session` (`pop3/src/lib.rs:85-95`).

**1.0.1**
- `pub struct HttpAuthenticated { pub in_flight: Option<InFlight>, pub access_token: AccessToken, pub epoch: RevocationEpoch }`
  (`http/src/auth/authenticate.rs:20-24`), returned by
  `Authenticator::authenticate_headers(&self, req, session) -> trc::Result<HttpAuthenticated>`
  (`:26-147`).
- `pub struct HttpAuthCache { account_id: u32, expires: u32, credential_id: Option<u32>, revision: u64, token_scope: Option<TokenScope> }`
  with `const _: () = assert!(size_of::<HttpAuthCache>() <= 40);`
  (`common/src/lib.rs:395-403`). The struct is exactly 40 bytes now.
- `HttpAuthEntries` keys entries by a SipHash-1-3 128-bit hash of the token, under two
  random per-process keys (`common/src/cache/http_auth.rs:17-26`, `:53-58`). Its API takes
  the raw token only: `get(token)`, `remove(token)`, `retain(..)`, `is_live`, `expires_in`.
- `GrantRevocations(AtomicU64)` (`:35`) has `epoch()`, an epoch-fenced
  `insert(entries, token, entry, epoch)`, `evict(entries, grant_ids)` and `clear(entries)`
  (`:99-140`).
- `AccessTokenInner` is asserted at most 96 bytes (`common/src/auth/mod.rs:228`). The outer
  `AccessToken` has no size assertion. It is built by seven struct literals in
  `common/src/auth/access_token.rs` (`:417`, `:426`, `:449`, `:466`, `:711`, `:873`, `:888`).
- Renewals: `Server::renew_access_token(&self, access_token: &AccessToken, remote_ip) -> trc::Result<AccessToken>`
  (`common/src/auth/oauth/grant.rs:195-209`) and `refresh_access_token(..) -> trc::Result<Option<AccessToken>>`
  (`:211-…`). IMAP's `SessionData::refresh_access_token` (`imap/src/core/mod.rs:278-283`)
  and the WebSocket loop (`jmap/src/websocket/stream.rs:97-107`) call them.
- IMAP `SessionData { account_id, access_token, … }` (`imap/src/core/mod.rs:73-83`) is built
  by `SessionData::new(session, access_token, in_flight)` (`imap/src/core/mailbox.rs:39`)
  at `imap/src/op/authenticate.rs:195-199`. POP3 keeps
  `State::Authenticated { mailbox, in_flight, access_token }` (`pop3/src/lib.rs:49-60`),
  set at `pop3/src/op/authenticate.rs:166`.
- EventSource and WebSocket arms: `http/src/request.rs:249-259` and `:261-273`; scope
  assertions at `:257` and `:269`.
- Plan and design citations checked: they match (`authenticate.rs:20-24`; `http_auth.rs:35-38`;
  `101-137` sits inside `impl GrantRevocations`, `:99-140`).
- Not recorded in the plan or design: the 40-byte assertion leaves no room for the fork's
  `generation` and `za_impersonated` fields, and caller-chosen keys are gone, so the fork's
  `http_auth.remove(&fp)` calls have no 1.0.1 equivalent.

**Frozen adaptation**
- The outer `AccessToken` keeps the two fork fields, `session_keys` and `za_impersonated`,
  never in `AccessTokenInner`. The seven literals default them. All the 0.16 methods stay.
  New fork method: `za_carry(self, from: &AccessToken) -> Self`, which copies both fields
  for renewals.
- HTTP:
  - `HttpAuthenticated` is unchanged. Keys ride `HttpAuthenticated.access_token`.
  - `authenticate_headers` is the only HTTP attach point. The vault, contacts and shares
    endpoints build per-request tokens from a fresh `authenticate_with_keys`.
  - Upstream's `HttpAuthEntries`, `HttpAuthCache` and `GrantRevocations` are used unchanged.
    Upstream's keyed SipHash already makes the HTTP cache key non-reversible (C3).
  - The fork keeps `za_keys` keyed by its own `Caches::za_fingerprint(token)`.
- Hit rule (upstream entry live and revision equal):
  - read `self.account(id)` and fail closed: on error, `http_auth.remove(token)` and
    propagate;
  - if the account is not a key account, take upstream's path (plus open point 2.1);
  - if it is a key account, serve only when `za_keys.get(&fp, now)` holds keys with
    `keys.account_id == id`, `keys.generation != 0` and
    `keys.generation == account.za_generation()` (item 3);
  - otherwise remove both entries (`http_auth.remove(token)`, `za_keys.remove(&fp)`) and
    verify fully.
  This replaces the 0.16 `generation == 0` branch and the eviction coupling: a key-account
  entry without live keys is never served.
- Miss rule: call upstream's `revocations.insert(…, epoch)` only for a non-key account, or
  for a key account whose keys pass the generation fence. Then call
  `za_insert_keys(fp, keys, now)`. Keep the 0.16 double-check after publish, which
  withdraws an entry when the account turned into a key account.
- Strips: `.without_session_keys()` after the scope assertion at `request.rs:257` and `:269`.
- IMAP: call `authenticate_with_keys` at `imap/src/op/authenticate.rs:140`. Attach the keys
  to the token before `SessionData::new` (`:195-199`), so `imap/src/core/mailbox.rs` needs
  no key change.
- POP3: the same at `pop3/src/op/authenticate.rs:118`. The keys live in
  `State::Authenticated.access_token` until the connection drops.
- SMTP, ManageSieve and the OAuth login (`http/src/auth/oauth/auth.rs:609`) keep upstream's
  keyless `authenticate`.
- Session predicates keep their 0.16 names and meaning: `Session::za_key_session`,
  `SessionData::za_key_session` (IMAP) and `State::za_key_session` (POP3), each true when
  `access_token.za_keys_for(account_id)` holds. Task 3 produces them; Task 5 consumes them.
- Key lifetimes: `vault::cache::KeyCache` and its `ZA_KEY_IDLE_SECS`, `ZA_KEY_MAX_AGE_SECS`
  and `ZA_KEY_MAX_ENTRIES` bounds are unchanged (`vault/src/cache.rs:22-77`). Password
  changes and app-password revocation keep the next-authentication semantics: no erasure of
  keys a connection already holds.
- **Producer:** Task 3. **Consumers:** Task 4 (DAV handlers, `handle_dav_request`'s drain
  trigger, sharing resolve), Task 5 (mail readers, trace predicates), Task 6 (it must
  prove that no background path obtains keys).
- **Contracts:**
  - C2: HTTP keys live for one request; IMAP and POP3 keys live for the connection;
    SMTP, ManageSieve, WebSocket and EventSource hold none; the idle and hard bounds stay.
  - C3: no reversible credential cache key; keys never reach a persistent cache or snapshot.
  - C4: OAuth scope, expiry and grant revocation on hits and misses stay upstream's.

**Open points**
- 2.1 **Proposed — primary to rule:** on an HTTP cache hit, re-derive the R35 marker from the
  request. A cached entry exists only after a successful login, and every successful login
  whose Basic username has the master form is an impersonation (`authenticate_basic`
  `:143`, `:322`). Bearer, API keys and app passwords cannot impersonate (`:200-206`). So
  the rule is "Basic and `UsernameParts::new(username).is_master()`", and
  `HttpAuthCache` needs no field. Alternative: do not cache impersonated logins at all.
  That costs every master-user HTTP request an Argon2 verification.
- 2.2 **Proposed — primary to rule:** apply `za_carry` inside `Server::renew_access_token`
  and `refresh_access_token` (`common/src/auth/oauth/grant.rs:208`, `:220`), two lines that
  cover IMAP and WebSocket. A WebSocket token cannot gain keys this way, because it is
  stripped at the upgrade. Alternative: carry at the protocol call sites
  (`imap/src/core/mod.rs:280`, `jmap/src/websocket/stream.rs:102-107`).

#### 3. Account epoch and classification

**Fork at 0.16.27**
- `AccountCache` carries `za_generation: u64` and `za_public_key: Option<[u8; 32]>`
  (`common/src/auth/mod.rs:93-96`). The flag `ACCOUNT_IS_KEY_ACCOUNT = 1 << 9` is at
  `:111`, with `AccountCache::is_key_account` at `:343`.
- `za_public_key` is written but never read. The served key is read fresh from the store
  (`za_served_public_key`, `common/src/auth/vault.rs:643-645`).
- `Caches.account_epoch: AtomicU64` (`common/src/lib.rs:189`), initialised in `Caches::parse`
  (`common/src/config/inner.rs:116`).
- `try_account` (`common/src/cache/principals.rs:415-673`) works in this order:
  - it reads the epoch before the registry read (`:439`);
  - it classifies by the marker and reads the vault (`:465-490`), mapping
    `is_vault_unusable` to `(0, None)`;
  - it sets the flag (`:505-507`);
  - it inserts and then rechecks the epoch (`:663-669`).
- The `access_token` loader has the same fence (`common/src/auth/access_token.rs:320`,
  `:341-347`). A key account's `credential_version` follows the vault revision (`:200-221`).
  The `access_to` filter drops a key account's mail collections (`:126-140`).
- Epoch bumps: `invalidate_all_local_caches` (`common/src/cache/invalidate.rs:328-353`)
  and the `AccessToken` and `Account` arms (`:378-405`). Those arms also drop `http_auth`
  entries and call `za_keys.remove_account`.
- Lookup rules: `za_is_key_account` fails open on an unknown id (`vault.rs:420-426`); the
  auth-cache and directory-login checks use `account()`, which fails closed.
  `za_invalidate_account` sends `AccessToken` and `Account` (`vault.rs:648-656`).

**1.0.1**
- `pub struct AccountCache { name, id, addresses, #[cfg(feature = "enterprise")] id_tenant, id_member_of: Box<[u32]>, quota_disk, description, storage: Option<Box<AccountStorage>>, locale, flags: u64, active_script: ActiveScriptSlot }`
  (`common/src/auth/mod.rs:96-113`).
- `const _: () = assert!(size_of::<AccountCache>() <= 112);` (`:121`). With `enterprise`
  the struct is 110 bytes, padded to 112. One more `u64` makes 120 and fails the
  assertion; the harness builds with `enterprise`.
- `pub struct AccountStorage { pub quota_objects: Option<ObjectQuota>, pub encryption_key: Option<EncryptionKeys> }`
  (`:115-119`). Its only constructor is `AccountStorage::boxed(..) -> Option<Box<Self>>`
  (`:123-135`). It returns `None` when both fields are empty. It is boxed, so it does not
  count toward the 112 bytes.
- Flags go up to `1 << 8` (`:139-147`), so `1 << 9` is still free.
- `try_account` (`common/src/cache/principals.rs:385-616`): the registry read is at `:401`,
  the flags at `:437`, and an unfenced `let _ = guard.insert(cache.clone());` at `:610`.
  The `access_token` loader inserts unfenced at `access_token.rs:344`, and so does
  `access_token_from_account` (`:350-…`).
- `Caches` (`common/src/lib.rs:217-265`) has no epoch.
- `invalidate_local_caches` is now synchronous and batched (`invalidate.rs:548-…`): the
  `AccessToken` arm is at `:567-570`, the `Account` arm at `:588-591`, the `http_auth`
  retain at `:660-669`, and the grant eviction at `:671-676`. `invalidate_all_local_caches`
  is at `:505-529`.
- `CacheInvalidation` gains `GroupwareResources`, `MessageCache`, `PrivateMetadata`,
  `OAuthGrant` and `ActiveSieveScript` (`common/src/ipc.rs:143-176`).

**Frozen adaptation**
- Classification: keep `ACCOUNT_IS_KEY_ACCOUNT = 1 << 9` in `AccountCache.flags`. Set it in
  `try_account`'s User arm next to `let mut flags = ACCOUNT_IS_USER;` (`principals.rs:437`),
  after the marker check on `account.password_credential()`. `is_key_account()` stays in a
  fork-owned `impl AccountCache` block.
- Generation: see open point 3.1. Drop `za_public_key` from the cache, because no reader
  uses it.
- `Caches` gains the fork fields `account_epoch`, `za_keys`, `za_fingerprint_key` and
  `za_account_page_origin`. They are initialised in `Caches::parse` with the same five
  lines as 0.16 (`inner.rs:114-118`).
- Fences:
  - load the epoch before the registry read in `try_account` (`principals.rs:401`) and in
    both access-token loaders (`access_token.rs:326`, `:350-…`);
  - replace each unfenced `guard.insert` (`principals.rs:610`, `access_token.rs:344` and its
    sibling) with the 0.16 insert-then-recheck.
- Bumps: `fetch_add` before the clears in `invalidate_all_local_caches` (`:505`) and at the
  top of the `AccessToken` (`:567`) and `Account` (`:588`) arms. In the `Account` arm, also
  `http_auth_accounts.insert(*id)` and `za_keys.remove_account(*id)`. In the `AccessToken`
  arm, `za_keys.remove_account(*id)`. In `invalidate_all_local_caches`, `za_keys.clear()`;
  upstream already clears `http_auth` through `grant_revocations.clear` (`:508-511`).
- Kept as at 0.16: `za_is_key_account` (fail open), the fail-closed `account()` users,
  `za_invalidate_account`, the `access_to` filter in `build_access_token`
  (`access_token.rs:46`), and the key-account `credential_version`. That version feeds
  upstream's `assert_credential_version` (`common/src/auth/oauth/token.rs`).
- **Producer:** Task 3. **Consumers:**
  - Task 4: account-id gates in the drain, the fan-out and sharing;
  - Task 5: mail classification and `za_is_key_rcpt`;
  - Task 6: the index worker's branch, the `ShareRevokeCleanup` skip and `za_has_tasks`.
- **Contracts:**
  - C1: classification never replaces the vault read a write needs;
  - C4: ordinary accounts keep upstream's loader;
  - C9: no fork field in a stored type (`AccountCache` and `AccountStorage` are in-memory);
    flag `1 << 9` checked free;
  - C3: no key material in the account cache.

**Open points**
- 3.1 **Proposed — primary to rule:** keep `za_generation: u64` in the boxed
  `AccountStorage`. Build it through a fork helper that replaces
  `AccountStorage::boxed(quota_objects, encryption_key)` at `principals.rs:538`, and always
  boxes for a key account. Read it with `AccountCache::za_generation()`. This keeps the
  0.16 fence on both the hit and the insert, inside the same epoch-fenced entry.
  Alternative: hold no generation in the cache; the insert fence reads the vault revision
  fresh, and hits rely on invalidation alone.

#### 4. Split calendar views

**Fork at 0.16.27**
- One record holds everything:
  `CalendarEvent { names, display_name, data: CalendarEventData, preferences, flags, dead_properties: DeadProperty, size, created, modified, schedule_tag }`
  (`groupware/src/calendar/mod.rs:132-143`).
- The sealing API (`groupware/src/calendar/seal/event.rs`) takes or returns that record:
  `seal_event` (`:192`), `seal_event_in_domain` (`:206`), `unseal_event` (`:296`),
  `unseal_event_pairs` (`:319`), `event_token_pairs` (`:341`), `unseal_event_archive`
  (`:366`), `classify_event_storage` (`:395`), `unseal_event_in_domain` (`:497`),
  `event_pairs_in_domain` (`:519`), `event_view_in_domain` (`:538`), `rewrap_event`
  (`:558`), `archived_event_is_sealed` (`:168`).
- Authority: `ZaCalendarContext { owner_id, calendar_id, personal_id, stored, domain, boundary }`
  with `assert_in(&self, batch: &mut BatchBuilder)`, which asserts the stored calendar
  archive (`groupware/src/calendar/sharing/resolve.rs:80-95`). Siblings:
  `ZaUidLease::assert_in` (`common/src/za/lease.rs:209`), the conversion's `assert_in`
  (`sharing/convert.rs:111`) and copy/move's `assert_in` (`dav/src/calendar/copy_move.rs:1339`).

**1.0.1**
- Metadata row: `pub struct CalendarEvent { names: Vec<DavName>, uid: String, display_name: Option<String>, start: i64, duration: u32, created: i64, modified: i64, size: u32, etag: u32, flags: u16, schedule_tag: Option<u32> }`
  (`groupware/src/calendar/mod.rs:179-191`).
- Content: `pub struct CalendarEventContent { data: CalendarEventData, preferences: Vec<EventPreferences> }`
  (`:196-199`), with `CalendarEventData { event: ICalendar, time_ranges, alarms, base_offset, base_time_utc, duration }`
  (`:317-324`), `EventPreferences { account_id, updated, instances: Vec<EventUserData> }`
  (`:380-384`) and `EventUserData` (`:389-395`).
- Writers:
  - `CalendarEvent::update_full(self, content, changed_by, event: Archive<&ArchivedCalendarEvent>, event_content: &ArchivedCalendarEventContent, account_id, document_id: u32, parent_id: Option<Slot>, batch) -> trc::Result<String>`
    (`groupware/src/calendar/storage.rs:148`);
  - `update_meta(self, changed_by, event, account_id, document_id: u32, parent_id: Option<Slot>, batch)`
    (`:175`);
  - `insert(self, content, changed_by, account_id, document_id: impl Into<PendingId>, parent_id: Option<Slot>, next_alarms: EventAlarms, batch)`
    (`:201`).
  **Each one ends with `batch.commit_point()`** (`:170`, `:195`, `:233`).
- Builders: `GroupwareWrite::full(meta, content, ctx) -> trc::Result<Self>`
  (`common/src/storage/index/split.rs:100-113`). It serializes the content, then calls
  `meta.refresh_from_content` and sets `etag = content_hash(content_archive) ^ meta_hash()`.
  `GroupwareWrite::meta_only` is at `:115-120`; `SplitUpdate::full(current_meta, current_content, meta, content, ctx)`
  at `:145-160`; `SplitUpdate::meta_only` at `:162-170`.
- `refresh_from_content` (`groupware/src/calendar/index.rs:208-248`) derives `uid`,
  `start`, `duration`, `size`, the to-do, floating and alarm flags, and the privacy flags
  (`:247`, `privacy().apply_to_flags`) from the content.
- New snapshot writer: `notify_direct_change` (`groupware/src/calendar/storage.rs:1242-1287`)
  stores `CalendarEventNotificationContent::{Direct { previous, current }, Itip { message, previous, current }}`,
  each a full `ICalendar` (`mod.rs:217-227`). DAV PUT calls it with
  `current: content.data.event.clone()` (`dav/src/calendar/update.rs:~806-815`). DAV
  serves notifications (`dav/src/calendar/scheduling.rs:55-130`, `dav/src/request.rs:282`).
- `Store::assign_document_ids` no longer exists. Ids come only from
  `BatchBuilder::reserve_document_id` slots (`store/src/write/batch.rs:103`), resolved at
  commit (`AssignedIds::slot`, `store/src/write/mod.rs:1584`).
- Plan and design citations checked: they match (`storage.rs` signatures, `split.rs`,
  `inbound.rs:705-709`, `itip.rs:854-875`, `mkcol.rs:114`, `get.rs:180`,
  `copy_move.rs:682, 726, 987, 1032`, `dav/src/calendar/mod.rs:289`). Three gaps:
  - `calendar/index.rs:247` is the flag line inside `refresh_from_content`, which starts at
    `:208`;
  - the plan names `notification.rs` only as a reader; it is also a content writer, and DAV
    serves what it writes;
  - the plan does not mention that `assign_document_ids` is gone, although key pinning
    spec 5.1 relies on it (fork `dav/src/calendar/update.rs:749-757`).

**Frozen adaptation**
- Seal and sanitize before the builders. The fork seals the tree into a
  `CalendarEventContent` (tree sealed in place, `X-ZA-EXTRA`, then `X-ZA-KEY` last on the
  root) and sanitizes the metadata row (item 5) before it calls
  `insert`/`update_full`/`update_meta`. `CalendarEventData::new` and `refresh_from_content`
  then run on the sealed tree. No fork code sets `uid`, `size` or `flags` afterwards.
- The seal API is retargeted, not rewritten. The functions above take
  `CalendarEventContent`, or its archive, plus the metadata row's fields named in item 5.
  The read view is the pair `(Archive<&ArchivedCalendarEvent>, CalendarEventContent)`,
  with the content unsealed. The ETag comes from the stored row (`CalendarEvent.etag`), so
  a read-only view keeps its stored-version identity.
- Assertion placement: every authority assertion (`ZaCalendarContext::assert_in`, the
  UID-lease fence, conversion, copy/move, the provisioning fence) is emitted into the batch
  **immediately before each** guarded `insert`/`update_full`/`update_meta`. Each of those
  ends with `commit_point()`, which opens a new transaction once the batch is large (over
  1000 operations or 5 MB, `batch.rs:33-34`). `add_commit_point` re-emits only the account
  and collection, never an assertion (`batch.rs:673-693`). Fork code never calls
  `commit_point()` between an assertion and the write it guards.
- Notifications: open point 4.1.
- Per-user data: a key account's `CalendarEventContent.preferences` stays empty (the 0.16
  rule).
- Privacy: field-policy version 2 in `seal/policy.rs`. The flags are derived by upstream
  only.
- **Producer:** Task 4. **Consumers:**
  - Task 3: `http/src/api/contacts.rs`'s redelivery calls `seal_event_in_domain`,
    `unseal_event_in_domain` and `CalendarEvent`;
  - Task 6: `build_calendar_document`, `grantee_cleanup`.
- **Contracts:**
  - C6: sealing happens before serialization and derived indexing; only the allowed flags
    derive from visible properties;
  - C7: sharing uses the current principal's keys, own-id permission and ring identity, and
    keeps the physical event and link fences;
  - C9: no fork field in `CalendarEvent` or `CalendarEventContent`;
  - and the rule that sealing never adds, removes or reorders components.

**Open points**
- 4.1 **Proposed — primary to rule:** a key-account owner's calendar writes create no
  notification. `notify_direct_change`, `notify_calendar_removal` and the iTIP notification
  path return early through a one-line call into a fork module (`storage.rs:1242`, `:~1290`).
  So the owner's DAV notification collection stays empty. This is 0.16 parity, since 0.16
  has no notifications, and it is the design's "defined refusal" for a new content surface.
  Alternative: seal each snapshot under the calendar's key domain, and unseal in
  `dav/src/calendar/scheduling.rs`.
- 4.2 **Proposed — primary to rule (needs a spec check):** key pinning 5.1 needs the
  create's document id before the fan-out. Task 4 first establishes whether a
  reservation-only batch allocates an id that is never reused, the property 0.16's
  `assign_document_ids` had. If it does, commit that batch before the fan-out and pass
  `AssignedIds::slot(slot)` as `ZaSource.document_id`. Alternative: record the withheld
  entry's source after the create commits, which reorders spec 5.2's phases and needs a
  spec ruling.

#### 5. Metadata and extras mapping

**Fork at 0.16.27**
- The event extras: `Extra { display_name, dead_properties: DeadProperty }` (format 1) and
  `ExtraV2 { display_name, dead_properties, pairs }` (format 2), sealed under the event's
  data key into `X-ZA-EXTRA` (`groupware/src/calendar/seal/event.rs:33-50`, `:59-115`).
- The calendar's `Bundle { name, description, color, dead_properties }` sits in the owner's
  `CalendarPreferences.name` as `"$za$" + envelope + "|" + bundle`; a custom timezone is
  sealed in place (`seal/collection.rs:1-41`).

**1.0.1**
- `types::DeadProperty` is gone. So are `CalendarEvent.dead_properties` and
  `Calendar.dead_properties`; `Calendar` now has `presence: u8` (`groupware/src/calendar/mod.rs:41-49`).
  `CalendarPreferences { account_id, name, description, sort_order, color, flags, time_zone, default_alerts }`
  is unchanged in shape (`:80-89`).
- Metadata store keys: `account / Collection::Metadata (14) / class / …` in
  `Subspace::Property` (`store/src/write/key.rs:589-608`, `:665`, `:873`).
- Container header: `[FORMAT_VERSION, kinds, count]`, validated by `header` and `validate`
  (`types/src/metadata/view.rs:62-69`, `:72-109`). A DAV entry is parsed at `:111-135`.
  Name rules: `check_property_name` (`types/src/metadata/xml/validate.rs:84-91`). Value
  rules: `validate_dav_value` (`xml/view.rs:68-81`).
- Builder: `MetadataBuilder::{new, from_view, set_dav(XmlName, &XmlValue), encode}`
  (`types/src/metadata/builder.rs:114`, `:118`, `:194`, `:224`).
- Dead-property writer: `dav/src/common/dead/patch.rs` `prepare` (`:122`).
  - It loads `current` (`:130-135`), builds from `current_view` (`:136-139`), measures
    `previous_len` from that view (`:144-146`), then encodes:
    `ContainerChange::new(current.as_ref(), builder.encode(), builder.edit())` (`:185`).
  - It checks limits (`check_edit(MetadataScope::Shared, …)`, `:186-196`) and quota
    (`:197`), derives kinds and file presence from `change.next()` (`:218-226`), and writes
    with `change.into_write(…)` (`:230`).
  - `ContainerChange::new` returns `None` when the previous and next bytes are equal
    (`common/src/storage/metadata/change.rs:23-37`).
- Value readers: `dead_properties` (`dav/src/common/propfind/dead.rs:118`) and
  `dead_property_names` (`:132`). Both return iterators that borrow the `&MetadataBuf`.
  Their callers are `propfind/item.rs:778` and `propfind/mod.rs:745`.
- Identity and size readers:
  - `DeadContainers::get` (`dav/src/common/dead/containers.rs:84-92`) and
    `stored_entries` (`:102-111`) yield
    `StoredEntry { document_id, size, hash }` (`common/src/storage/metadata/stored.rs:24-28`,
    `:52-58`);
  - that hash becomes `AssertValue::Archive(ArchiveVersion::Hashed)` in
    `StoredEntry::clear` (`:60-64`);
  - also `stored_len` (`:94`), `copy_growth` and `check_growth` (`:242-260`), and
    `container_kinds` (`:320`).
- Copy: `DeadContainers::copy(&mut self, server, source: &MetadataBuf, document_id: impl Into<PendingId>, previous, batch)`
  (`containers.rs:135-150`). It calls `.view()` at `:147`. Its calendar callers are in
  `dav/src/calendar/copy_move.rs` (`:1312`, `:1618`).
- Presence: `rewrite_presence` for `CalendarEvent` (`groupware/src/metadata/mod.rs:39-61`)
  makes a metadata-only rewrite of the row.
- Plan and design citations checked:
  - the key layout (`key.rs:589-608`), the header (`view.rs:62-69`) and `rewrite_presence`
    (`mod.rs:40-60`) match;
  - "DAV writes only `Shared` (`dead/patch.rs:190`)": `:190` is the Shared-scope limit
    check, and the write is at `:230`;
  - "seal `next` before `change.into_write(…)`": `next` is fixed at `:185` and read three
    times before `:230`, so it must be sealed before `:185`;
  - "unseal after `.view()` in `containers.rs` (`get`, `stored_entries`)": neither function
    calls `.view()`, and `stored_entries` must stay on the sealed bytes for its CAS hash;
  - `Entry { key, value }` is `pub(super)` in `types/src/metadata/entries.rs:19`. That is
    correct, but it is not reachable from fork code; the builder is.

**Frozen mapping (fresh stores only; no 0.16 format is read)**

| 0.16 field | 1.0.1 home | Sealing |
|---|---|---|
| Event tree | `CalendarEventContent.data.event` | Unchanged: sealed in place under the per-write data key, `X-ZA-KEY` last |
| Event `display_name` (in `Extra`) | Open point 5.1 | Open point 5.1 |
| Event token pairs (`ExtraV2.pairs`) | `X-ZA-EXTRA` in the content | Unchanged |
| Event `dead_properties` | `Collection::Metadata` `Shared` container of the event | The sealed wrapper below |
| Calendar name, description, colour | Owner's `CalendarPreferences` (unchanged shape) | `Bundle` without `dead_properties` |
| Calendar `dead_properties` | `Collection::Metadata` `Shared` container of the calendar | The sealed wrapper below |
| Custom timezone | `CalendarPreferences.time_zone` | Unchanged (`calendar-tz`) |
| Event preferences (JMAP only) | `CalendarEventContent.preferences` | Always empty for a key account |
| — (new) notification snapshots | `CalendarEventNotificationContent` | Open point 4.1 |
| — (new) presence bits, `Viewer`/`Owner` counters | Upstream | Not sealed: they name kinds, not values |

- Extras codec: drop `dead_properties` from `Extra` and `ExtraV2`, since the type is gone.
  The format-1 and format-2 split stays (format 2 only when there are pairs).
- Sealed wrapper: build it with upstream's builder, never by hand:
  `MetadataBuilder::new().set_dav(<fork namespace and name>, &<text-only value>)` then
  `encode()`. This gives the header `[FORMAT_VERSION, MetadataKinds::DAV, 1]` by
  construction. The value's text is base64 of `payload version byte || key envelope || ciphertext of the original container`.
  The trusted reader, the checked validator, presence derivation, quota and cleanup then
  read it as a valid upstream container.
- Sites (one-line calls into a fork module, keyed by the request's token):
  - `patch.rs` `prepare`: unseal `current` for the builder's view only (`:136`). Keep the
    sealed `current` as `ContainerChange`'s previous, so its hash CAS is upstream's. Compare
    plaintext before sealing, so a no-op PROPPATCH still writes nothing. Then seal the
    encoded `next` before `ContainerChange::new` at `:185`. Measure limits and quota on the
    sealed sizes on both sides.
  - PROPFIND: unseal at the two callers (`propfind/item.rs:778`, `propfind/mod.rs:745`) into
    an owned buffer that outlives the iterator. `dead_properties` and
    `dead_property_names` stay upstream's.
  - `get`, `stored_entries`, `stored_len`, `copy_growth`, `check_growth`,
    `container_kinds`, `MetadataBuf::read`, `metadata_used_quota`,
    `container_cleanup_from`, `destroy_owner_metadata` and `purge_private_metadata` stay
    keyless, on the sealed bytes.
  - COPY and MOVE: in `za_copy_move_event`'s path, pass `DeadContainers::copy` a resealed
    `MetadataBuf` (source key domain to destination key domain).
- Conversion and rotation: dead containers now live outside the event. At 0.16 they rode
  in the event's extras, so the event's rewrap covered them. Task 4's conversion journal,
  rollback and future-write rotation must now include each container (concern in the
  report).
- IMAP METADATA and JMAP object metadata: Task 6 refuses them for key accounts.
  - IMAP: `imap/src/op/metadata/{get,set}.rs`, and `LIST … RETURN (METADATA …)` at
    `imap/src/op/list.rs:181-182`, which matches the plan.
  - JMAP: `jmap/src/api/metadata/{write,apply,get,preload,copy}.rs`, **and**
    `query.rs`/`filter.rs` (`MetadataQuery::evaluate`, `:102`), the `*/query` path that the
    plan's list omits.
- **Producer:** Task 4 for the calendar mapping and the wrapper; Task 6 for the IMAP and
  JMAP refusals. **Consumers:** Task 6 (leak rules for `Collection::Metadata`), Task 7.
- **Contracts:**
  - C6: no hidden property reappears through dead properties or metadata-only writes;
  - C9: no fork field or hand edit in `types::metadata` or `store/src/write/metadata.rs`;
  - C3: names never in keys (`key.rs:589-608`); no plaintext container for a key
    account's calendar or event.

**Open points**
- 5.1 **Proposed — primary to rule:** the event's `display_name` stays in the content's
  `X-ZA-EXTRA`. A key account's metadata row holds `display_name: None`. A displayname
  PROPPATCH (`dav/src/calendar/proppatch.rs:543`, `:580`) on a key-account event becomes a
  full, sealed write (`update_full`) instead of upstream's `update_meta`. This reuses the
  0.16 codec and ring logic unchanged. Upstream's metadata-only ETag also changes, because
  `meta_hash` covers `display_name` (`index.rs:189`). Alternative: put `"$za$" + ciphertext`
  in the row's `display_name`, sealed under the calendar's key domain, and keep
  `update_meta`.
- 5.2 **Proposed — primary to rule:** the wrapper's identifiers are namespace
  `urn:x-zero-access:sealed`, local name `container`, and payload version byte `1`. Its
  associated data is `za/v1|dead-container|<account>|<collection>|<event UID, or empty for a calendar>`,
  with no document id, because a COPY's destination id is still a pending slot
  (`containers.rs:139`). The key envelope is a fresh data key wrapped exactly like an
  event's, so conversion and rotation rewrap it. Task 4 records the exact bytes.
  Alternative: seal under the calendar's own data key from its collection envelope. That
  needs no per-container envelope, but rotation must then reseal every container.

#### 6. Pending-ID fences

**Fork at 0.16.27**
- Single-message ingest with a trailing provisioning fence. The fence is
  `Server::za_provisioning_fence(&self, batch, account_id)`
  (`common/src/auth/vault.rs:486-494`). It switches the batch to
  `Collection::Principal`/document 0 and asserts `PrincipalField::ZeroAccessVault`
  absent, except on PostgreSQL and MySQL.
- The R55 training fence sits before each `commit_point()` of the cross-account copy loops.
- Calendar writes take an id from `assign_document_ids` before the fan-out (item 4).

**1.0.1**
- `email_ingest_into(&self, params: &mut IngestEmail, batch: &mut BatchBuilder, group_thread: Option<PendingId>) -> trc::Result<PendingIngest>`
  (`email/src/message/ingest.rs:131-136`, body `:257-866`).
- `email_ingest_finish(&self, pending: PendingEmail, assigned_ids: &AssignedIds) -> IngestedEmail`
  (`:137-141`, body `:868`). `email_ingest` is the single-message wrapper (`:235-255`) that
  SMTP delivery, Sieve, JMAP `Email/set` and the restore task use.
- `IngestGroup::ingest(&mut self, server, params, key: ThreadKey) -> trc::Result<()>` and
  `flush(&mut self, server) -> trc::Result<Vec<IngestedEmail>>`
  (`email/src/message/ingest/group.rs:87-123`, `:125-160`). Its callers are IMAP APPEND
  (`imap/src/op/append.rs:183`) and JMAP (`jmap/src/email/grouped.rs:62`).
- `PendingId::{Assigned(u32), Slot(Slot)}` (`store/src/write/mod.rs:103-106`);
  `AssignedIds` (`:121-127`).
- Facts that fix the fence rules:
  - inside `email_ingest_into`, the batch's first operation is `batch.with_account_id(account_id)`
    at `:676`; the plaintext blob is held before that (`hold_blob`, `:661-672`);
  - the threading key (`:779-785`), the spam sample (`:736-756`), the index queue entry
    (`:786`) and the `MergeThreads` task (`:812-818`) are written after it;
  - a group keeps its batch after a rejection (`MessageIngest::Error` or quota), and
    discards it after any other error (`group.rs:108-114`, `is_rejection` `:157-170`);
  - `email_ingest_into` adds no commit point, and a group flushes before it grows large
    (`must_flush_before`, `:61-66`), so one group commits as one transaction;
  - `write_batch` commits each commit chunk as its own transaction
    (`store/src/dispatch/store.rs:299-312`).
- `BatchBuilder::assert_value` (`batch.rs:183-196`); `commit_point` splits only when the
  batch is large (`:656-662`); `add_commit_point` (`:673-693`).
- Plan citations checked: `email_ingest_into`, `email_ingest_finish` and
  `IngestGroup::{ingest,flush}` match the plan's signatures (`email_ingest_finish` returns
  the value, not a `Result`).

**Frozen adaptation**
- Mail (Task 5):
  - The key-account branch decides inside `email_ingest_into`, before `hold_blob`
    (`:661`). It stages the sealed blob instead of the plaintext blob, and writes the stub
    in place of the metadata.
  - It never writes the threading key, the spam sample or `MergeThreads`. It keeps
    `queue_document_index` (the worker indexes the stub's visible fields, item 7).
  - It fills `PendingEmail`'s existing fields content-free: `thread_recheck: None`, the
    Message-ID redacted, and an empty `private_commit`. So `email_ingest_finish` needs no
    keys and no new field.
  - Every refusal for a key account happens before `:676`, as upstream's rejections do. A
    refusal raised after `:676` must not be a rejection error, so the group discards its
    batch.
  - Ordinary classification: right after `batch.with_account_id(account_id)` (`:676`),
    emit the vault-absence assertion for that account. Rule: **one assertion per account
    per commit chunk**. A group can mix accounts, so each account's segment carries its
    own.
  - `za_provisioning_fence` changes the batch's collection and document. The next upstream
    operation sets its own (`:695`, `:764`), and the quota limits are
    account-level (`common/src/storage/quota.rs:107-122`), so that placement is safe.
  - SMTP delivery, Sieve, the restore task (`services/src/task_manager/tasks/restore_item.rs:57`)
    and `Email/set` go through the same branch. A keyless writer seals to the served public
    key, as delivery does. A writer with the owner's keys seals under `SessionKeys::mwk()`.
- Calendar (Task 4): the assertion placement rule of item 4. The UID-lease fence and the
  pin and ring writes are emitted in the chunk of the write they guard. Pending event ids
  flow as `PendingId::Slot` until `AssignedIds` resolves them (open point 4.2).
- Cross-account copy (Task 5): the R55 fence stays before every `commit_point()`.
- **Producers:** Task 5 (mail), Task 4 (calendar). **Consumer:** Task 6 (the index worker
  and `za_has_tasks` read what these writers queue).
- **Contracts:**
  - C5: every ordinary writer asserts vault absence in its effective commit, and every
    key-account message is a stub plus a sealed blob;
  - C7: lease, pin and ring fences hold at the effective transaction;
  - C8: the fences cover every new queue and commit segment.

**Open points**
- 6.1 **Proposed — primary to rule:** Task 2's store test support adds one hook,
  `FORCE_COMMIT_SPLIT`, in `store/src/za_test.rs` under `test_mode`. It makes
  `commit_point()` split as if the batch were large. Without it, no test with fixture-sized
  data reaches a second chunk (the threshold is 1000 operations or 5 MB), so the
  per-chunk assertion rules of items 4, 6 and 7 are untested. Alternative: build fixtures
  large enough to split naturally, which is slow and depends on the thresholds.

#### 7. Search and snapshot inputs

**Fork at 0.16.27**
- `services/src/task_manager/index.rs`:
  - the email branch indexes a key account's message, or a stub of an account that no
    longer exists, by `ReceivedAt` and `Size` only, without reading the blob (`:480-524`);
  - plain documents are wrapped by `Server::za_index_fence` (`common/src/auth/vault.rs:511`,
    R58), and the calendar builder returns `NotIndexed` for key accounts and sealed
    archives (`:555-600`).
- `Server::za_has_tasks` (`vault.rs:737`) matches every `Task` variant exhaustively (R54).
- The DAV resource and message caches are built from stored archives.

**1.0.1**
- Queue key: `SearchIndexClass::Queue { index, id_prefix: u32, id_suffix: QueueDocumentId, created_at: u64 }`,
  serialized as `[index][id_prefix][id_suffix][created_at]` in `Subspace::SearchQueue`.
  The marker is `QueueIndex { index, partition }`, and the status key is `QueueStatus`
  (`store/src/write/mod.rs:962-1002`, `key.rs:567-586`, `:868-870`).
- Producers: `BatchBuilder::queue_document_index` and `queue_document_unindex(index, account_id, document_id)`
  (`batch.rs:977-1010`; value `1` for index, `0` for un-index). `queue_trace_index` uses
  `id_prefix = (span id >> 32)`, not an account id (`:1012-1024`).
- Worker:
  - `process_items` (`services/src/index_queue/index.rs:190-306`) builds documents, flushes
    them through the sink (`index_account_documents` at `:572`), and only then clears the
    processed queue items in a separate batch (`:287-295`);
  - email items read the metadata row first, and skip an index item whose row is gone
    (`:308-397`); un-indexing goes through `delete_email_metadata`, which reads
    `row.raw_headers()` (`:834-855`);
  - the builders are `build_email_document(server, account_id, document_id, row: MetadataRow)`,
    which reads the whole blob (`services/src/index_queue/document.rs:29-62`), and
    `build_calendar_document`, which reads `CalendarEventContent` (`:88-120`).
- Store: `Store::index_account_documents(&self, account_id, index, index_documents: Vec<IndexDocument>, unindex_document_ids: Vec<u32>)`
  (`store/src/search/index.rs:21-56`) calls `account_batch` (`:299-341`), which returns
  `AccountIndexer::build_batch` (`store/src/search/term.rs:276-388`).
  **`build_batch` adds commit points:**
  - `add_commit_point()` every 1000 term merges (`term.rs:27`, `:364-368`);
  - `commit_point()` after each `Document` set or clear (`:373-383`).
  The `Document` keys come **last**.
- `IndexDocument { index, fields }` (`store/src/search/mod.rs:175-178`) is an in-memory
  builder.
- Retry: a failing partition retries with a delay of
  `MIN_RETRY_DELAY (30 s) << attempts`, capped at `MAX_RETRY_DELAY = 30 * 60`
  (`services/src/index_queue/manager.rs:316-320`, `mod.rs:37-38`). It never fails
  terminally. `x:IndexQueueStatus/set` resets the status
  (`jmap/src/registry/mapping/index_queue.rs:191-240`).
- `Task` variants (`registry/src/schema/structs.rs:5893-5922`):
  - `IndexDocument`, `UnindexDocument` and `IndexTrace` are removed;
  - `ShareRevokeCleanup` (`TaskShareRevokeCleanup { object_type, account_id, document_id, status }`,
    `:6120-6127`), `DmarcReportWindow` and `TlsReportWindow` are added;
  - `RestoreArchivedItem` and `TenantMaintenance` exist only under `enterprise`.
- Share revocation cleanup: `stage_calendar`, `stage_event` (with `update_full` at `:194`)
  and `stage_notification` are in `services/src/task_manager/tasks/grantee_cleanup/stage.rs`
  (`:84`, `:124`, `:207`).
- Snapshots:
  - `SwapTier` persists `Snapshot::Messages(Arc<MessageStoreCache>)` and
    `Snapshot::Resources(Arc<GroupwareResources>)` (`common/src/cache/swap/writer.rs:92-95`);
  - their builders read stored archives only: for a calendar the URL slug, ACLs,
    preference flags and timezone; for an event the names, start, duration, UID, ETag and
    flags (`groupware/src/cache/calcard.rs:306-345`, `common/src/lib.rs:550-580`);
  - restoring a resource snapshot checks its decode and base path (`groupware/src/cache/mod.rs:786-850`);
  - `CacheInvalidation::GroupwareResources` and `MessageCache` drop the account's snapshots
    (`invalidate.rs:571-582`).
- Plan and design citations checked:
  - **`account_batch` "commits each pass in one write with no commit point
    (`store/src/search/index.rs:299-341`)" is wrong.** The pass is one `write_batch` call,
    but it commits one transaction per chunk. This undercuts the evidence for R62's and
    R63's "subject deleted", and for R58 placed once.
  - "Scan for every `SearchIndex`" (R54) would read Tracing entries, whose `id_prefix` is
    not an account id.
  - The other cites match (`manager.rs:316-359`, `index_queue.rs:191-240`,
    `structs.rs:6120-6127`, `index.rs:308-392`, `:834-855`).

**Frozen adaptation**
- Email document: in `build_email_document`, branch before the blob read
  (`document.rs:36-45`, the `get_blob` at `:38`). For a key account, or a stub of an
  account that no longer exists (R47), return the visible-only document (`ReceivedAt`,
  `Size`), as 0.16 did. The test is
  `email::message::za::is_stub` on the row's `raw_headers` format byte. Plain returns are
  wrapped by the R58 carrier.
- Calendar document: `build_calendar_document` returns `Ok(None)` for a key account, or for
  sealed content (`archived_event_is_sealed`, retargeted to the content). The worker
  records that as nothing to index (`index.rs:226-247`).
- Un-index: `delete_email_metadata` recognizes a stub by its format byte before it resolves
  the thread subject (`index.rs:845-852`).
- R58: the carrier field on `IndexDocument` (Task 2) marks documents built from an account
  read as ordinary. `account_batch` places the vault-absence assertion **in every commit
  chunk** of the batch `build_batch` returns: at the head, and after every commit point
  (open point 7.1). Skip it on PostgreSQL and MySQL, as at 0.16.
- R54: `Server::za_has_tasks` keeps its exhaustive match over `Task`.
  - `ShareRevokeCleanup` counts for its `account_id`; the report windows are excluded; the
    `enterprise`-only variants get `#[cfg]` arms.
  - It also scans the account's queue prefix `[index][account_id]` for **Email, Calendar,
    Contacts and File**, plus their `QueueStatus` keys. It does not scan Tracing, whose
    partition is a span bucket, nor InMemory, which has no queue entries.
  - It keeps its position before the R51, R60 and R62 scans.
- R62: the term scan (`Store::has_account_search_terms`, ported by Task 2) stays. For the
  per-chunk guard and R63, see open point 7.2.
- Share revocation cleanup: `stage_event`, `stage_calendar` and `stage_notification` return
  without staging for a key-account owner, through a one-line call (`stage.rs:84`, `:124`,
  `:207`). `rebuild_shares` runs unchanged.
- Snapshots: no fork code in `common/src/cache/swap/`. Upstream's builders read stored,
  sealed or visible-only archives; no unsealed response view enters `MessageStoreCache` or
  `GroupwareResources`; `SessionKeys` are never reachable from either. This is upstream's
  mechanism unchanged; Task 6 only proves it with the swap suite.
- **Producer:** Task 6. **Consumer:** Task 7 (coverage and leak gates).
- **Contracts:**
  - C8: key mail keeps only arrival time and size; sealed calendar content and attachment
    text stay out of indexes; fences cover every queue and commit segment;
  - C5: background work never acquires keys;
  - C3: no decrypted content in persistent snapshots.

**Open points**
- 7.1 **Proposed — primary to rule:** a fork function in a store-crate fork module,
  `za_assert_each_chunk(batch: &mut BatchBuilder, account_id)`, is called on one line in
  `account_batch` after `build_batch` returns (`index.rs:338`). It prefixes every chunk
  with the vault-absence assertion. Alternative: emit the assertion inside `build_batch`
  before each `add_commit_point` and `commit_point` (`term.rs:365`, `:381-382`): three
  upstream lines instead of one.
- 7.2 **Proposed — primary to rule:** retire R62's per-chunk `Document`-key guard and R63's
  confirmation read, with corrected evidence:
  - at 1.0.1 the `Document` keys are written in the last chunks, not chunk 0;
  - one partition worker serializes an account's index and un-index entries of one index
    (`index.rs:56-104`), so an un-index between two chunks needs a lost cluster lease;
  - the R58 assertion in every chunk stops a superseded worker's tail after setup.
  Keep R62's term scan for terms orphaned by a failed later chunk. Alternative: keep a
  per-chunk guard, rebuilt on the last chunk's `Document` key.

  - `BlobStoreSwap { store: BlobSwapStore, retention, … }` (`:721-732`), with

- Boot order changed: `build_ipc` runs before `Telemetry::parse(&mut bootstrap, &storage, ipc.index_queue_notify())`
  (`boot.rs:148-151`, `common/src/config/telemetry.rs:189`).

- Plan citations checked: `server.rs:362` and `:592`, and `boot.rs:158, 225`, match. Gaps:
  the telemetry signature and order change, `process_nonce`, and the missing `Storage` at

**Frozen adaptation**
- `TestServerBuilder` keeps its 0.16 API. Changes:
  - `build_with_opts` calls `build_ipc` before `Telemetry::parse(&mut self.bootstrap, &storage, ipc.index_queue_notify())`;
  - it binds `let (cache, swap_rx) = Caches::parse(&mut self.bootstrap, &core.storage).await;`
    (`:362`);

    `common::cache::swap::SwapTier::start(&inner, swap_rx)`, as boot does.

- The swap bootstrap option: `pub async fn with_cache_swap(self, swap: CacheSwap) -> Self`.
  It is implemented as `self.with_object(Cache { swap, ..Default::default() }).await`, the

  the baseline.

  `tests/src/utils/storage.rs`. Its body follows 1.0.1's registry store types.
- `RegistryStore::{new, clone_with_public_url}` stay `#[cfg(feature = "test_mode")]` in
  `store/src/build/registry.rs`, next to upstream's `stalwart_tests` slot. `new` sets
  `process_nonce: rand::random()`.
- `wait_for_tasks` keeps its signature and also waits for the account-partitioned
  `SearchIndexClass::Queue` entries and their status keys, retries included (Task 2).
- **Producer:** Task 2. **Consumers:**
  - Tasks 3–6: every suite;
  - Task 6: `cache_swap_tests` boots one server with `LocalFile` under the server's temporary
    directory, and one with `BlobStore(BlobStoreSwap { store: BlobSwapStore::Default, .. })`
    and `BLOB_STORE=FileSystem`;
  - Task 7.
- **Contracts:**
  - C10: the harness neither hides nor drops a required test; `test_mode` hooks and the
    weak Argon2 never reach shipping builds;
  - C11: enterprise harness results stay separate from the OSS product gate.

**Open points**
- 8.1 **Proposed — primary to rule:** `cache_swap_tests` sets `min_account_size: 0`, a small
  `flush_changes`, and forces writes with `SwapTier::flush()`. Otherwise the 1 MiB default
  skips every fixture account, and the swap cases pass without writing a snapshot.
  Alternative: assert `snapshots_written() > 0` with defaults and large fixtures.
