| `tests/src/utils/{server,storage,cleanup}.rs`, `tests/src/lib.rs` | Task 2 | Tasks 3–6 add test modules and helpers in `tests/src/utils/za.rs` | Change the builder API |

##### Order

1. Task 2, then Task 3, then Task 4, then Task 5, then Task 6, with serialized writers. The
   crate graph forces this order:
   - `common` needs `store` and `types`;
   - `groupware` needs `common`;
   - `email` needs `groupware`;
   - `services` needs `email`, `smtp`, `groupware` and `spam-filter`;
   - **`jmap` needs `services`** (`jmap/Cargo.toml:15`);
   - `http` needs `jmap`, `dav`, `services` and `scim`;
   - `managesieve` needs `imap` and `email`.
2. **Proposed — primary to rule (crate checks):** Task 5 closes with
   `cargo check -p email -p imap -p pop3 -p smtp -p spam-filter`. Task 6 closes with
   `cargo check -p services -p store -p jmap -p http@1.0.1 -p managesieve`. In the plan,
   Task 5 checks `-p jmap` before Task 6 ports `services`, and no task checks `http`,
   `managesieve` or `spam-filter`, although Tasks 3, 4 and 5 edit them. Alternative: keep
   the plan's lists, and record Task 5's `jmap` check and the missing crates as "blocked
   until Task 6" and "first checked at Task 7".
3. A file whose owner closed earlier, and that fails to compile only at a later task's
   check, goes back to its owner. Example: Task 3's IMAP login edits are first compiled by
   Task 5. The later task reports the failure and does not patch the file.

##### Named handoffs

- **HTTP shares versus contacts.**
  - Task 3 owns `http/src/api/mod.rs`, `vault.rs` and `contacts.rs`'s endpoint shell: the
    routes, CORS, primary verification, pin transactions and `contacts/forget`.
  - Task 4 owns `shares.rs`, and `contacts.rs`'s `za_redeliver` with its drain, fan-out,
    pinning, sequence and seal calls (`contacts.rs:230-500`).
  - Task 3 freezes `za_verify_primary` and `za_reread_verified` before Task 4 starts. Task 4
    edits `contacts.rs` only after Task 3 closes.
- **Store test support versus assertions.**
  - Task 2 owns `store/src/za_test.rs` (the existing hooks, the `Pause` type, the proposed
    `FORCE_COMMIT_SPLIT`), the R58 carrier type compiling, and `has_account_search_terms`.
  - Task 6 owns where assertions go (`account_batch`, open point 7.1) and their semantics.
  - Task 6 may append new hooks to `za_test.rs` after Task 2 closes; Task 2 places no
    assertion.
- **Protocol login versus mail reader files.**
  - Task 3 owns the login handlers and the session-key carriers listed above, with the
    pre-auth and whole-session raw I/O masking (its `raw_input` and `tracing` tests).
  - Task 5 owns the readers and the per-event detail redaction (`ImapEvent::Fetch` and
    `Store`, SMTP DATA and recipient masking, Sieve and spam filter), and consumes Task 3's
    predicates unchanged.
  - Shared files (`smtp/src/inbound/session.rs`, `http/src/request.rs`) are edited by
    Task 3 first and Task 5 second.

### Task 1 rulings

The primary's decisions during Task 1, numbered as in the execution ledger. Each gives the
decision, the reason and what it costs if it is wrong. Rulings 9–29 settle the interface
sheet's proposals and the inventory's findings, and 30–33 come from the task review and its fix round; Tasks 2–6 carry them in their briefs.

**Setup**

1. **Worktree.** `git worktree add` at `.worktrees/stalwart-1-upgrade`, on branch
   `stalwart-1-upgrade` from `stalwart-1-upgrade-plan` (`0ddbbc1a3`, a docs-only delta over
   `9a7574203`). The session's own worktree tool would have branched from `origin/main` and
   dropped the plan documents. Cost if wrong: none to the code.
2. **Three parallel agents.** The baseline, the inventory and the interface sheet were
   written in parallel. They wrote disjoint files, only the baseline ran cargo, the
   inventory predicted the merge with `git merge-tree`, and none committed. Cost if wrong:
   one part redone.
3. **Commit before the merge.** The baseline and this note are committed before
   `git merge --no-commit`, since a conflicted merge can't take a separate commit.
4. **Task 1 ends with the merge started.** `MERGE_HEAD` is set, `tests/` and the workflow are
   restored, the conflicts are unresolved and nothing is committed. When to commit the merge
   is decided before Task 2.

**Baseline**

5. **`za::external_directory`** is recorded with no coverage figure and the flag
   `added_after_measurement`. Its expected modes are the steps that run the za suite. It was
   added at `76969bed3`, after the `5b914ddb5` measurement.
6. **The ACL variant's figures stand.** `d375c5dc1` adds a three-line wait and no call.
7. **The executed rule needs three counts:** the body record, the region that starts the
   body's last top-level statement, and the closing-brace exit region. The baseline showed
   on its own exports that rustc routes every `return` through the closing-brace block, so
   the two-count rule passes an early return. Completion markers on all 198 bodies would
   have edited restored upstream tests. Under three counts the baseline is unchanged
   (173 calls executed somewhere) and no call needs a marker. Cost if wrong: Task 7 adds
   markers for calls whose tail sits in a branch.
8. **The coverage threshold is the exact fraction,** 17,821 of 18,490 lines (96.38%). The
   design's 96.4% rounds it, and a literal check fails the unchanged baseline.

**Interface sheet and inventory**

9. **Enumeration delay** (sheet 1.1). Task 2 regates the failed-login delay
   (`common/src/auth/authentication.rs:83-89`) to also skip under `test_mode`, as at 0.16.
   The product never builds `test_mode`. Cost if wrong: the test timing of failed logins
   differs from production.
10. **Impersonation marker** (sheet 2.1; revised after the review's M1). On an HTTP cache
    hit, re-derive R35's marker from the cached token itself, whatever the mechanism:
    `decode_plain_auth(token)` with `UsernameParts::new(..).is_master()`. `HttpAuthEntries`
    keys on the token alone, so a Bearer header carrying a Basic login's token hits that
    login's entry, and a rule that read the mechanism would derive no marker.
    `HttpAuthCache` is asserted at most 40 bytes and is already 40, so it gets no field.
    Cost if wrong: an impersonation route that doesn't use a master-form username is
    missed; Task 3's tests cover the routes that exist.
11. **Carry on token renewal** (sheet 2.2; revised after the review's I1). Two carries,
    kept apart:
    - **The marker.** R35's impersonation marker is carried inside `renew_access_token` and
      `refresh_access_token` (`common/src/auth/oauth/grant.rs:195-230`), so every renewal
      keeps it. At 1.0.1 the WebSocket loop renews its token on every request
      (`jmap/src/websocket/stream.rs:97-108`). A renewed token that lost the marker would
      serve an impersonated session the key account's keyless `Mailbox/get` and `Email/set`
      (C4, C5). The marker only restricts, so carrying it in the shared functions fails
      safe.
    - **The keys.** They're carried only at the call site that holds them for a connection,
      IMAP's renewal (`imap/src/core/mod.rs:280`); POP3 doesn't renew. Invariant 5 names
      exactly who holds keys, and a call-site carry fails closed if upstream adds a renew
      caller.
    - Task 3 adds a za case: an impersonated WebSocket, a forced token refresh, then
      `Mailbox/get` still refused.

    Cost if wrong: a renewal path that bypasses both functions drops the marker. The new
    case covers the WebSocket path.
12. **The authentication generation** (sheet 3.1). Keep `za_generation` in the boxed
    `AccountStorage`, built through a fork helper, so the 0.16 hit and insert fences stay
    exact. `AccountCache` is asserted at most 112 bytes. `AccountStorage` is an in-memory
    type, and C9 covers stored types. Move `za_public_key` there too if it has a reader;
    drop it only if it has none. Cost if wrong: one upstream in-memory struct gains a
    field, a new merge hotspot.
13. **Calendar notifications** (sheet 4.1; revised after the review's I2). The first
    version rested on a wrong premise, that 0.16 had no notifications. In fact
    `CalendarEventNotification` is the 0.16 scheduling-inbox item, and release 2 stores
    sealed ones under key accounts for the drain (`seal/item.rs`, `drain.rs`). So:
    - **Direct-change notifications.** Upstream's (`notify_direct_change`, and through it
      `notify_calendar_addition` and `notify_calendar_removal`) produce nothing for a
      key-account owner: one fork call returns early. At 1.0.1 they store full `ICalendar`
      snapshots, and DAV serves them.
    - **Sealed inbox items.** The fork's items stay `CalendarEventNotification` records in
      1.0.1's split notification record. The sealed message rides in
      `CalendarEventNotificationContent::Itip`'s `message`, with `previous` and `current`
      set to `None`. Task 4 defines the row's `calendar_ids`, `etag` and
      `NotificationAudience` flags without reading plaintext, and records the mapping in
      this note before it ports the drain.
    - **The drain's mirror.** Its hand-mirror of `itip_ingest`'s import branch
      (`itip.rs:339-352`) and of `commit_itip_merge` (`:956-970`) writes no plaintext
      `Itip` snapshot.
    - **Deletion.** Upstream's quota eviction (`NotificationQuota::reserve`,
      `storage.rs:1086-1150`) and reaper (`reap_calendar_notifications`,
      `notification.rs:370-425`) must not delete a pending sealed item before the drain sees
      it, or Task 4 records why that deletion is safe.

    Cost if wrong: key accounts lack 1.0's direct-change notifications, which 0.16 didn't
    have either.
14. **The create's id before the fan-out** (sheet 4.2; mismatch 8). `assign_document_ids` is
    gone. Task 4 establishes whether a reservation-only batch allocates an id that is never
    reused. If it does, Task 4 uses one. If not, it stops and brings key pinning 5.2's phase
    order to the primary for a spec ruling.
15. **Event display names** (sheet 5.1). The name stays sealed in `X-ZA-EXTRA`; a key
    account's metadata row holds `None`; and a displayname PROPPATCH on a key-account event
    becomes a sealed `update_full`. Cost if wrong: a key account's metadata-only ETag
    behaviour differs from upstream's.
16. **The dead-property wrapper** (sheet 5.2). The identifiers are as proposed: namespace
    `urn:x-zero-access:sealed`, name `container`, version byte 1. Each container gets its own
    data key, wrapped like an event's, so conversion and rotation rewrap it. Where the
    calendar's document id is assigned when sealing, the associated data binds it. Where it
    isn't, as for a COPY's pending destination, Task 4 records the limit. Cost if wrong: a
    format change, which is cheap on fresh stores.
17. **`FORCE_COMMIT_SPLIT`** (sheet 6.1). Task 2 adds this test hook to `store/src/za_test.rs`
    under `test_mode`, so the per-chunk rules are tested. Cost if wrong: one test-only line
    in an upstream file.
18. **R58 in every chunk** (sheet 7.1; mismatch 1). `account_batch` splits a pass, as the
    design now records. One fork call after `build_batch` (`store/src/search/index.rs:338`)
    adds the vault-absence assertion to every chunk of a pass that holds a fenced document.
    Cost if wrong: one key read per chunk on ordinary accounts' index passes.
19. **R62 and R63** (sheet 7.2; inventory finding 10). R62's per-chunk guard and R63's
    confirmation read retire if Task 6 confirms from source that one partition worker runs
    an account's index and un-index entries in order. If it doesn't, a guard on the last
    chunk's `Document` key replaces R62's. R62's term scans stay. Cost if wrong: on one node,
    orphaned terms block that account's provisioning (remedy: recreate the account).
20. **The swap suite** (sheet 8.1). `cache_swap_tests` sets `min_account_size: 0` and a
    small `flush_changes`, calls `SwapTier::flush()`, and also asserts
    `snapshots_written() > 0`.
21. **Crate checks** (sheet, order item 2). Task 5 closes with `cargo check -p email -p imap
    -p pop3 -p smtp -p spam-filter`, and Task 6 with `-p services -p store -p jmap
    -p http@1.0.1 -p managesieve`. A file that first fails at a later task's check goes back
    to its owner. Cost if wrong: Task 5's JMAP edits are first compiled at Task 6.
22. **R54's queue scan** (mismatch 9) counts only the account-partitioned indexes (Email,
    Calendar, Contacts, File). `Tracing` entries carry a span id prefix, not an account id.
23. **Corrected cites** (mismatches 5, 6, 10–13, 15). Where the interface sheet corrects a
    path, line or signature cited by the plan or design, the sheet wins. For dead
    properties:
    - unsealing happens at the callers of `dead_properties` and `dead_property_names`;
    - `stored_entries` stays on the sealed bytes, because its hash is a compare-and-swap
      value;
    - PROPPATCH runs upstream's limit checks on the plaintext container, skips a no-op write
      by comparing plaintext, and seals before `ContainerChange::new`.
24. **The UID lease's storage** (inventory finding 1). v1.0.1 removed `ValueClass::InMemory`,
    and its own `try_lease` runs on the in-memory store, which a data-store batch can't
    assert. So the lease moves to `ValueClass::Any(AnyClass { subspace, key })` in the data
    store. That is upstream's own mechanism for ad-hoc keys, used by SMTP's aggregate
    reporting window. Task 3, with Task 2's store hooks, picks a subspace that no upstream
    purge or cleanup clears unasserted, and the cleanup and leak scanners cover it. C7 is
    unchanged: fenced writes assert the lease, and the asserting sweep remains its only
    cleanup. Cost if wrong: a generic cleanup that clears the subspace would drop live
    leases, which the lease tests catch.
25. **SEQUENCE** (inventory finding 2). The fork's module is renamed
    `groupware/src/calendar/sequence_floor.rs`, and upstream's `sequence.rs` is taken as
    supplied. Task 4 re-reads the floor and the reconciliation against `ICalendarSequence`,
    and uses upstream's where it already covers the floor.
26. **`Store::is_read_replica`'s SEL-marked arm** (inventory finding 8) is not carried. Task 2
    re-derives whether the function is still needed and writes any replacement without
    naming an SEL-only variant. Cost if wrong: an enterprise-built test binary wouldn't
    treat a SQL read replica as one; no test uses one.
27. **The LLM hook** (inventory finding 9) is a recorded C3 limit, not a port. The
    enterprise-enabled test binary contains the SEL LLM analysis without the fork's
    redaction, and no za test configures an LLM. The product excludes it.
28. **The raw Authorization value in errors.** v1.0.1 puts the raw header value into errors
    at `http/src/auth/authenticate.rs:73-78` (`.id`) and `:94-98` (`.details`). The fork
    removed both at 0.16. Task 3 carries the removal, and they join the hook inventory as
    C3 hooks.
29. **Dead properties outside the event** (sheet concern 3; the review's M12). At 1.0.1 a
    container is no longer part of the event's extras. So each of these handles every
    container explicitly, resealing or rewrapping it:
    - sharing's conversion, rollback and rotation;
    - a MOVE or COPY between calendars or key domains.

    Task 4 records the conversion journal's identity rule for containers.

**After the task review**

30. **The pending-item flag** (the review's I3). The fork's
    `EVENT_NOTIFICATION_ZA_PENDING = 1 << 2` (`groupware/src/calendar/mod.rs:125`) is the
    same bit as 1.0.1's `EVENT_NOTIFICATION_IS_DESTROY` (`v1.0.1 mod.rs:158`), and keeping
    both constants compiles. Task 4 moves the fork flag to a bit that no 1.0.1 notification
    flag uses (`NotificationAudience` holds bits 4 and 5). A compile-time check makes it
    differ from every upstream flag, and the notification flags join C9's collision
    checks. Fresh stores need no migration of the bit.
31. **Gate granularity** (the review's I6, M2, M3 and M10). Each of these fails the gates,
    where at 0.16.27 the thing it guards held:
    - **A step.** The gates read per step, not per mode, so a loss under only
      `BLOB_STORE=FileSystem` fails.
    - **A helper.** Every `tests/src` function that ran in a step at 0.16.27 must run there
      under the same three counts, so an unawaited helper future or a helper's early return
      fails.
    - **A loop.** Each of the 12 loop-tailed sub-test bodies needs a positive loop-body count
      where it had one, so a lost fixture fails.
    - **The cohort.** The coverage gate also reports a runtime-only cohort figure, without
      the inline `#[cfg(test)]` modules and the fork's test-support files, and both figures
      must hold their baseline fractions.

    Task 7's diff step also flags a removed `.await`, an added `let _ = <call>` and an
    added `drop(<call>)` of a future. Cost if wrong: a gate that fails on a legitimate
    restructuring, which then gets an expectation-ledger entry.
32. **The strict-rule tooling is committed** (the review's M15) under
    `tests/qualification/tools/`. It has a self-check that reproduces every recorded
    `last_statement` region on the `5b914ddb5` sources, so Task 7 builds on the method
    rather than reimplementing it.

    Recorded limits of the helper gate (the re-review's N3 and N6):
    - **Locator refinements.** The locator refines the rule in four ways, none of which can
      pass a body that skipped a last statement holding code:
      - a statement with no region of its own passes to the one before it;
      - `let`, `if let` and `while let` are anchored at the scrutinee;
      - among regions with the same start, the highest count wins;
      - ties go in symbol order.
    - **Exemptions.** The gate never requires 81 `tests/src` functions, the baseline's
      documented exemptions:
      - 63 `#[tokio::test]` entry points, which the libtest status gate covers instead;
      - 13 that complete only through `return`;
      - 3 that the locator can't anchor;
      - 2 that didn't complete at 0.16.27.
    - **Future-building helpers.** The twelve helpers whose last statement is an async
      block (six request builders that drive the race tests and six `SessionManager`
      methods) are also required to run that block's own record (`tail_block`) where it
      ran at 0.16.27, so a future that is built but never run fails.
33. **No test licence; licensed enterprise objects take the SEL disposition** (inventory
    fix round 1; rewritten after the re-reviews' N1 and R2-1).
    - **No licence.** The test licence's two `test_mode` sites sit in the SEL-only
      `common/src/enterprise/config.rs` (`:87`, `:90` at 0.16.27). The fork doesn't edit SEL
      code, and a licence minted by fork code would get around the licence check.
    - **What changes.** At 1.0.1 an enterprise-built server refuses `MaskedEmail`,
      `ArchivedItem`, `Metric` and `Trace` without a licence (`assert_enterprise_object`,
      `jmap/src/registry/mod.rs:18-42`). These objects exist only in enterprise builds, so
      the OSS product has none of them. Five retained non-SEL sub-tests use them:
      `system::delivery`, `system::authorization`, `system::directory`,
      `telemetry::tracing` and `telemetry::metrics`.
    - **The harness keeps the `enterprise` feature**, as CLAUDE.md says. Dropping it would
      stop those five from compiling, and with them the telemetry suite, `za::disabled`'s
      disabled-tenant case, `za::mail::delivery`'s restore-refusal case,
      `smtp::inbound::antispam` and `scim_tests`, which design Decision 1 keeps compiling.
    - **What needs a licence.** Without one, `core.enterprise` is `None`, so licensed
      features are off as well as the four objects: alerts and AI models among them. That
      reaches `telemetry::alerts` (its alert email) and `smtp::inbound::antispam`'s LLM case
      too (the third re-review's R3-1).
    - **The licensed parts take the SEL-only disposition,** in line with Jay's decision on
      SEL tests: they come back only if a licence is bought, and then only in a private
      repository.
      - `telemetry::tracing` and `telemetry::metrics` take it as whole calls, since every
        assertion in them is on `Trace` or `Metric` objects and a skipped body would pass
        the gate empty (R3-2).
      - Elsewhere, Task 2 identifies each part that needs a licence. It removes or
        cfg-gates that part with an expectation-ledger entry giving this evidence, and
        keeps the rest of the sub-test running.
      - In-memory metrics are OSS, so a part that tests them stays.
      - No part is adapted to expect the refusal. If a part's subject also exists in the
        OSS product, Task 2 stops and brings it to the primary.

    Cost if wrong: lost coverage of enterprise objects the OSS product doesn't ship.

The inventory's findings 3–7 and 11–14 are directives to their owner tasks and need no
choice. The baseline's observation that imap `uidbatches::test` reaches its final loop with
zero iterations in both modes, so its contiguity assertion never ran at 0.16.27, goes to
Task 7.

## Task 2

Part 2a (workspace, manifests, `types`, `store`, `vault`) is recorded in the conflict ledger
cells it resolved. Part 2b restored the test infrastructure: the inline unit test modules,
the upstream test hooks, the harness, the SEL-only removals and ruling 33's licensed parts.
Paths are under `crates/` unless they start with `tests/`.

### Restored unit test modules

Every one of the 132 upstream modules on Task 1's list has a disposition. The 22 SEL-only
`scim` and `scim-proto` modules are dispositioned, not restored. The 7 fork modules in
upstream files stay inline in their files, under their own names; all but
`jmap/src/vacation/set.rs`'s sit in conflicted files, which their owners keep through the
resolution.

The 110 others, each in a fork-owned file named as the baseline's `restored_tests` mapping
says, with SPDX headers copied from fork-owned neighbours:

| Disposition | Modules | Where |
|---|---:|---|
| Restored and declared; the crate compiles and its tests pass | 68 | `types` 1, `store` 3, `utils` 5, `trc` 1, `registry` 3, `nlp` 10, `jmap-proto` 5, `imap-proto` 35, `dav-proto` 5 (`serde_impl` included) |
| Restored and declared; the crate compiles only at its owner's check (ruling 37) | 33 | `common` 14, `directory` 3, `groupware` 3, `jmap` 2, `managesieve` 1, `pop3` 2, `services` 2, `smtp` 3, `spam-filter` 3 |
| Restored; the counterpart is conflicted, so the declaration is handed off (ruling 35) | 4 | `common/src/auth/authentication.rs`, `directory/src/core/secret.rs`, `http/src/request.rs`, `spam-filter/src/analysis/url.rs` |
| Moved with its subject | 5 | see below |

Declarations follow the brief's rule, placed right after upstream's private slot:
`#[cfg(test)] #[path = "<stem>_restored_tests.rs"] mod <name>;` (`restored_tests.rs` for
`mod.rs` and `lib.rs`). Two `nlp` modules were `pub mod tests` at 0.16 and stay `pub`.

Moved modules (the baseline mapping is updated for the three whose file moved):

| 0.16 module | 1.0 subject | Restored as | libtest identifier |
|---|---|---|---|
| `common::network::tests` | `is_global_ip`, now `utils/src/outbound.rs:294` | `utils/src/outbound_restored_tests.rs`, module `tests` (merged) | `utils::outbound::tests::global_ip_classification` |
| `services::state_manager::http::tests` | `is_same_organization`, now `utils/src/outbound.rs:277` | the same file and module | `utils::outbound::tests::same_organization_redirects` |
| `groupware::tests` | `encode_path_segment`, replaced by `common::storage::dav::{canonical_path_segment, canonical_uri_path}` (`common/src/storage/dav/mod.rs:143-149`) | `common/src/storage/dav/restored_tests.rs`, an equivalent test of the new pair (internals replaced; DAV paths are public behaviour) | `common::storage::dav::tests::*`, same three names |
| `imap_proto::utf7::tests` | the external `encodify::utf7::IMAP` | `imap-proto/src/utf7_restored_tests.rs`, declared from `lib.rs` as `mod utf7` with an inline `tests` | unchanged, `imap_proto::utf7::tests::*` |
| `utils::codec::base32_custom::tests` | the external `encodify::base32::STALWART` | `utils/src/codec/base32_custom_restored_tests.rs`, declared from `codec/mod.rs` as `mod base32_custom` with an inline `tests` | unchanged |

The last two have no in-tree subject left, which the brief's rule would call "subject
deleted". They test a third-party dependency, the `encodify` crate, by choice: the fork
depends on its behaviour (mailbox names decoded with `IMAP.lenient()`, the STALWART
alphabet in blob ids, state tokens and blob-store paths), and keeping them keeps their
libtest identifiers.

The `common::storage::dav` equivalent was run against a copy of 1.0's functions in a scratch
crate (3 passed), since `common` does not compile yet.

Internals replaced, with an equivalent test (both public behaviour): `utils::cheeky_hash`
(a stored key format; 1.0 moved the length byte to the end and stores up to 16 bytes
directly) and the DAV path pair above. No module is "subject deleted" or "internals replaced,
no equivalent".

Adaptations of names and call shapes only (no ledger entry): `store` http lookup
(`refresh_interval`, `custom_user_agent`), `store` split (`SearchFilter::{Integer, Text}`
replace `Operator`), `jmap-proto` references (`AHashMap`), `imap-proto` receiver
(`Token::Argument` takes `ArgumentBytes`), `imap-proto` list and select (`ListItem`'s
`objectid` and `metadata`, `permanent_flags`), `imap-proto` fetch's `serialize_fetch`
(`FetchItem::serialize`, `ChainedBytes`), `dav-proto` tokenizer (`Namespace` import) and
responses (lock owners built as `XmlValue`, 1.0's replacement of `DeadProperty`), and
`spam-filter` pyzor (fix round 1: 1.0 removed the `PyzorDigest` trait, so two local helpers
build a `SpamFilterInput::from_message` over the message's `MessageTexts` with default text
limits and call its `pyzor_digest` and `pyzor_create_message`; `spam-filter` first compiles at
Task 5's check, which confirms the adaptation).

The `dav-proto` request and response tests read 114 fixtures under `dav-proto/resources/`,
which the merge deleted; they are restored from the fork side. Upstream's `parse_requests`
rewrites the JSON fixtures instead of comparing them (its comparison is commented out at
0.16 too): under 1.0 it rewrites 26 of them. The committed fixtures are 0.16's; do not commit
a run's rewrite.

Three non-module `cfg(test)` items that 1.0 regated (`common/src/expr/parser.rs`'s panic,
`dav-proto`'s `PartialEq for Token` and `PropStat::new`) are back under `cfg(test)`, as at
0.16. Task 1's table missed `dav-proto`'s 99 `cfg_attr(test, …)` serde attributes in
`lib.rs` and `schema/{collation,mod,property,request,response}.rs`, which 1.0 regated to
`all(test, stalwart_tests)` and which the restored request and response tests need; they are
back under `cfg_attr(test, …)`.

### Upstream test hooks

Of the table's 121 "restore" rows:
- **Restored, 79.** 68 here and 11 in part 2a (`store` 10, `types` 1). Each turns
  `stalwart_tests` back into `feature = "test_mode"`, or writes a fork-owned replacement where
  1.0 moved or removed the code:
  - `common`: `config/mailstore/scripts.rs`, `config/network.rs`, `config/smtp/resolver.rs`,

    table) `storage/index/build.rs`'s `REVOKE_CLEANUP_WINDOW`, listed in the second inventory
    table next to the test-hook table;
  - `network/dns/records.rs`: 0.16's test provider domain, `pebble.letsencrypt.org`,
    restored in `provider_domain` (1.0 removed the branch);
  - `email`: `mailbox/{destroy,manage}.rs`, `message/index/search.rs`;
  - `jmap`: `api/event_source.rs`, `mailbox/query.rs`; `registry/query.rs`: 0.16's ascending
    default order restored under `test_mode` (1.0 removed it);
  - the push loopback exemption (`jmap/src/push/set.rs:445` at 0.16): Task 1's table says
    1.0 removed it, but it moved. 1.0's `validate_push_url` goes through `OutboundPolicy`,
    whose `is_public_domain` and `is_permitted_ip` (`utils/src/outbound.rs:246`, `:269`)
    allow `localhost` and loopback under `all(stalwart_tests, not(test))`. Both are regated

    `utils/test_mode`, so `jmap`'s restored push test sees the exemption as at 0.16. The
    exemption now covers every outbound policy check in a test build, as upstream's test
    configuration does;
  - `services`: `state_manager/{http,push}.rs`, `task_manager/tasks/dkim.rs` (its two sites,
    and 0.16's fixed test keys in a fork-owned `tasks/dkim_test_support.rs`, declared as

    0.16's code (1.0 left the protocol types unchanged);

- **Handed to the owners of conflicted files, 29.** Listed under "Handoffs from Task 2".
- **Not restored, 13:**
  - ACME retries (`services/src/task_manager/acme.rs`, 4 rows): subject deleted. 1.0's
    `tasks/acme.rs` has no retry loop; a failed renewal returns `Outcome::transient` and the

### `UNIT_TEST_FEATURES` for 1.0 (Tasks 7 and 8)

Re-derived from `tests/Cargo.toml`'s dev-dependencies (their `test_mode` and `enterprise`
features), its default `rocks` and `sqlite`, and `dns-update/test_provider`:

```
common/test_mode common/enterprise
coordinator/enterprise
dav/test_mode
directory/enterprise directory/sqlite
dns-update/test_provider
email/test_mode email/enterprise
groupware/test_mode
jmap/test_mode jmap/enterprise
managesieve/enterprise
services/test_mode services/enterprise
smtp/test_mode smtp/enterprise
spam-filter/test_mode spam-filter/enterprise
store/test_mode store/enterprise store/rocks store/sqlite
trc/enterprise
utils/test_mode
vault/test_mode
```

- Dropped from 0.16's list: `coordinator/test_mode`, `dav-proto/test_mode`,
  `directory/test_mode`, `http_proto/test_mode`, `imap/test_mode`, `managesieve/test_mode`
  and `pop3/test_mode`. No 1.0 crate of the fork has those features.
- **`http/test_mode` and `http/enterprise` cannot be listed.** 1.0's `telemetry` crate
  depends on the crates.io `http` crate, and `--features http/<feature>` also asks that
  dependency for the feature, so resolution fails ("package `telemetry` depends on `http`
  with feature `test_mode` but `http` does not have that feature"); `http@1.0.1/test_mode`
  is not accepted either. `http/enterprise` is on anyway, through `stalwart`'s default
  features. `http`'s unit tests need `test_mode` for the fork's `za_test` module, so Task 8
  runs them in a step of their own:
  `cargo test -p http@1.0.1 --lib --bins --features test_mode,enterprise`.
- `cargo tree --workspace --exclude tests --exclude scim --exclude scim-proto -e features`
  with this list resolves; it enables `types/test_mode` (through `dav-proto`'s
  dev-dependency) and `mail-auth/test` (through `smtp/test_mode`).

### Harness

`tests/src/utils/{server,storage,cleanup}.rs` follow the interface sheet's section 8. The
tests crate cannot compile before Task 7, so they were written from the 1.0.1 source and
checked by `rustfmt` only.

- `TestServerBuilder` keeps its 0.16 API. `build_with_opts` builds the IPC before
  `Telemetry::parse(&mut bootstrap, &storage, ipc.index_queue_notify())`, binds
  `(cache, swap_rx)` from `Caches::parse(&mut bootstrap, &core.storage)`, and calls
  `SwapTier::start(&inner, swap_rx)` right after building `Inner`, as boot does.
  `inner_with_rxs` passes `&self.server.core.storage` and drops the receiver.
- `with_cache_swap(CacheSwap)` inserts a `Cache` singleton with that tier (ruling 20's
  suite). No suite calls it, so every server keeps the registry default, `Disabled`.
- Telemetry is enabled with `core.is_enterprise_edition()`, as boot does, instead of 0.16's
  `true`: with no test licence (ruling 33) the enterprise build runs unlicensed.
- Renames: `NetworkListener::implicit_tls`; `fetch_groupware_resources` returning
  `GroupwareResources`; the S3 store's `access_key_id` and custom region `{url, region}`.
- `RegistryStore::{new, clone_with_public_url}` are part 2a's (`process_nonce:
  rand::random()`); `RegistryEnvStores` stays fork-owned in `storage.rs`.
- `wait_for_tasks` keeps its signature. It waits for the scheduled tasks
  (`TaskQueueClass::Task`, now keyed by `TaskId`) and for the search index queue: every key
  of subspace `SearchQueue` (`y`), that is, the entries (`SearchIndexClass::Queue`, grouped
  by partition through `SearchIndexClass::queue_partition`), the markers of the partitions
  that hold queued work (`QueueIndex`, set by `queue_index_task` when an entry is queued and
  cleared when the partition drains) and the failure statuses (`QueueStatus`, a
  `SearchIndexStatus`). With
  `skip_not_due`, a partition whose retry is due later is skipped, entries included. The
  index queue has no permanent failure, so `skip_permanent_failures` applies to tasks only.
- `cleanup.rs` matches 1.0's `Subspace` enum exhaustively in `subspace_rule`, with no
  catch-all arm, and iterates `Subspace::ALL`. Rules are keyed on the enum, never on the
  byte:
  - leftovers (reported): `Acl`, `Immutable`, `QueueMessage`, `TaskQueue`, `ReportOut`,
    `ReportIn`, `Indexes`, `DeletedItems`, `BlobLink`, `TelemetrySpan`, `TelemetryMetric`,
    `QueueEvent`, `Blobs`, `IndexProperty`, `SearchQueue`;
  - registry items, indexes and primary keys: kept for singletons and the fixture types, as
    at 0.16. A reference now starts with `RegistryClass::REFERENCE_MARKER`; its target's type
    decides, as at 0.16;
  - `Counter`: document-id and change-id counters deleted and registry id counters kept, as
    at 0.16 (by `CounterKind` byte); any other counter the store purge left is a leftover;
  - `Property`: the vault and pin exemption, unchanged (account, `Collection::Principal`,
    field 150 or 151);
  - `System`: the schema version and node-id claims kept, anything else a leftover;
  - `SearchTerm`, `SearchDocument`: leftovers, except on PostgreSQL and MySQL, which create
    no such table (`Subspace::is_internal_fts`);
  - `Logs`: deleted after the check, as at 0.16. 1.0 also keeps share notifications there,
    as log entries of `SyncCollection::ShareNotification` (key: account id, `9`,
    notification id). Upstream writes one for the grantee on every grant, regrant, change
    and revoke, and keeps them with the change log, so they are a live account's normal
    state. They are deleted with the logs, and their accounts are returned with the vault
    accounts (`AccountState`); `assert_is_empty` reports any whose account no longer exists,
    since destroying an account clears its `Logs` prefix (fix round 2).
- `store_lookup_expire_all` is gone: its subject, in-memory values in the data store, does
  not exist at 1.0. `store_destroy` and `search_store_destroy` cover 1.0's subspaces and
  search stores; resetting an ElasticSearch or MeiliSearch store panics, since 1.0 moved
  `drop_indexes` into upstream's private test support (no CI step uses them).
- Not settled (left as at 0.16, for their owners): `TestServer::all_queued_messages` uses
  0.16's `QueuedMessages` and `SmtpSpool::next_event(&mut Queue)`, which 1.0's SMTP queue
  replaced (`next_event(Option<QueueName>) -> Option<u64>`).

### SEL-only removals and ruling 33

- Removed with their sub-test calls: `tests/src/directory/oidc.rs` (`oidc::test`),
  `tests/src/system/archiving.rs` and `tenant.rs` (`archiving::test`, `tenant::test`), and
  the 8 SEL-only files of `tests/src/scim/`.
- `scim_tests` takes the SEL-only disposition (ruling 38). Its entry function sat in the
  SEL-only `tests/src/scim/mod.rs`, and its only subject is the SEL-only `scim` crate. So
  the whole suite goes:
  - the three dual-licensed files that only it declared (`conformance.rs`, `oidc.rs`,
    `tenant.rs`, which import `ScimTest` from that `mod.rs`) are removed with it;
  - `tests/src/lib.rs` no longer declares `scim`;
  - the tests crate's `scim` dev-dependency is removed: nothing else in `tests` uses it
    (checked with grep), and `http/enterprise` still enables `scim/enterprise`.

  They stay in git history and come back only with a bought licence, in a private
  repository. Fix round 1 also removed the SCIM tooling only that suite used: the
  `scim-proto` dev-dependency (with its lockfile line), the SCIM-tester container helpers
  (`ensure_scim_tester`, `scim_tester_exec` and their `OnceCell` in
  `tests/src/utils/containers.rs`) and `tests/docker/scim/` (its Dockerfile and driver).
- `get_token_for_client`, which the dual-licensed `tests/src/directory/issuer.rs` imported
  from the SEL-only `oidc.rs`, is written in `issuer.rs` from RFC 6749: the resource owner
  password credentials grant (4.3) against the token endpoint of the issuer's discovery
  document, with HTTP Basic client authentication over form-urlencoded credentials (2.3.1).
- Ruling 33's licensed parts are listed in the expectation ledger.

### Task 2 rulings

The primary's decisions during Task 2, numbered on from Task 1's.

34. **Task 2 resolves every conflict in `store`, `types` and `vault`,** because its check
    is `cargo check -p types -p store -p vault`. That includes the files Tasks 4 and 6 own
    (`store/src/query/log.rs`, `search/index.rs`, `write/batch.rs` and `write/mod.rs`):
    - upstream's code is taken as supplied;
    - each fork hook is kept only where it compiles without a design its owner must still
      make;
    - every deferred hook is recorded in the conflict ledger with its owner.

    Cost if wrong: an owner re-ports a hook from `9601038de`, guided by its inventory row.
35. **Conflicted files of Tasks 3–6 stay with their owners.** Test-hook sites and
    restored-module `#[path]` declarations inside them are handed off (see "Handoffs from
    Task 2"), so no task edits inside another task's conflict markers. Ruling 9's regate of
    the failed-login delay therefore lands with Task 3. Cost if wrong: the owners carry a
    short extra list.
36. **Task 2 ran as two sequential dispatches,** 2a (the workspace, manifests, lockfile and
    the `types`, `store` and `vault` hooks) and 2b (the test infrastructure), each
    committing, with one task review at the end.
37. **Every task's crate check also builds that crate's test targets:**
    `cargo check -p <crates> --tests`, with `--features test_mode` where the crate has it.
    Restored unit modules and test hooks then compile with their crate before Task 7. Cost
    if wrong: longer checks.
38. **`scim_tests` takes the SEL-only disposition.** Its entry function is in the SEL-only
    `tests/src/scim/mod.rs`, and its subject is the SEL-only `scim` crate. Its three
    dual-licensed sibling files and the `tests` crate's `scim` dependency are removed, and
    design Decision 1 is amended. Cost if wrong: they come back from history, with a bought
    licence, in a private repository.
39. **The default telemetry stores inside an SEL snippet.** The bootstrap inserts a default
    `TracingStore` and `MetricsStore` inside an SEL snippet (`defaults.rs:498`, `:506`). The
    fork doesn't edit it, and adds no cleanup exemption. Task 7 shows whether they write
    anything without a licence. If they do, the harness removes them after bootstrap
    through the registry API. Cost if wrong: one harness step at Task 7.
40. **`system::delivery`'s spam cases keep their subject** (the Task 2 review's M7). Part 2b
    moved the GTUBE-to-Junk and `card-exists` cases from masked addresses to
    `john.doe@example.org`, john's alias from creation, and flagged it after the fact,
    where ruling 33 said to stop first. The ruling ratifies the move:
    - the spam score and the `card-exists` override depend on the recipient account and
      the sender, not on the envelope address;
    - the assertions are unchanged;
    - delivery to a masked address is enterprise-only and takes the SEL disposition.

    Cost if wrong: none for the OSS product.

## Task 3

### Part 3a: `directory`, `common` and `vault`

Part 3a resolved the fifteen conflicted files of `common` and `directory` (cells in the
conflict ledger, including Task 4's `storage/transaction.rs`, Task 5's
`scripts/plugins/mod.rs` and Task 9's two OTEL files under ruling 41), and ported the fork's
hooks in those crates. `vault` needed no change: Task 2 had it compiling, and its 45 tests
pass. The shape of the port follows interface sheet items 1-3 and rulings 9-12 and 24:

- `Server::authenticate` keeps upstream's signature and returns a keyless `Authenticated`.
  The fork's `authenticate_with_keys` holds upstream's body and returns
  `(Authenticated, Option<Arc<SessionKeys>>)`; the keys are detached from the token after
  the `Authenticate` permission check. Keys come only from the marker diversion and from a
  key account's app-password wrap; `authenticate_bearer` never yields them.
- `AccessToken` keeps upstream's `token_scope` and adds `session_keys` and
  `za_impersonated`, all defaulted by `AccessToken::from_parts`, the one constructor.
  `renew_access_token` and `refresh_access_token` carry R35's marker
  (`za_carry_impersonation`, ruling 11) and never carry keys.
- The authentication generation lives in the boxed `AccountStorage` (ruling 12), with the
  public key, which `tests/src/za/setup.rs:145` reads; `AccountCache` is unchanged.
- The vault record is written as `VAULT_RECORD_VERSION || Archiver` output, uncompressed
  and hashed, and read with checked decoding (`deserialize_untrusted`), errors reduced to
  `vault_corrupted` without the stored bytes. No 0.16 reader is kept (fresh stores). Its key
  is unchanged: `Subspace::Property`, `account id || Principal || field 150 || document 0`
  (151 for pins), so the cleanup's vault and pin exemption keeps its rule (Task 2 handoff).

#### Deferred, by owner

**Part 3b**
- `http/src/auth/authenticate.rs`: the hit rule of interface sheet 2 is now load-bearing.
  `Caches::za_insert_keys` and `za_sweep_keys` no longer remove HTTP entries (1.0's
  `HttpAuthEntries` keys on a SipHash of the token, not on the fork's fingerprint), so a key
  account's cached entry must be served only while `za_keys.get(&fp, now)` holds keys with
  `keys.account_id == id`, `keys.generation != 0` and
  `keys.generation == account.za_generation()`, and removed otherwise. Until 3b lands this
  rule, nothing else stops a key-account entry whose keys were evicted from being served
  keyless. The generation is now the method `AccountCache::za_generation()`, not a field.
  Also ruling 10 (marker re-derived on a hit), ruling 28 (no raw Authorization value in
  errors), the three regression cases and the `za_test` hooks.
- `http/src/api/vault.rs` and `contacts.rs`: `authenticate_with_keys` now returns
  `(Authenticated, keys)`: use `.access_token`. `Server::authentication_failure` is kept
  (delay, then upstream's `auth_failure`, which now also applies `details_to_reason`).
- IMAP and POP3 logins: call `authenticate_with_keys` (`imap/src/op/authenticate.rs:140`,
  `pop3/src/op/authenticate.rs:118` at v1.0.1) and attach the keys before the session is
  built. IMAP's renewal (`imap/src/core/mod.rs:280`) carries the keys at the call site; the
  marker is already carried by `renew_access_token`.
- EventSource and WebSocket (`http/src/request.rs`): `.without_session_keys()` after the
  scope assertion.
- `services/src/task_manager/scheduler.rs`'s `ZaKeySweep` calls `Caches::za_sweep_keys`,
  which keeps its signature and return value.
- `jmap/src/registry/mapping/account.rs:239` verifies the current password for a
  self-service change with `verify_mfa_secret_hash`; it is one of the registry refusal
  sites (a marker credential never verifies there).
- Tests read `account.za_generation()` and `account.za_public_key()` (methods now).

**Task 4**
- `za_eval_if` takes 1.0's `arena: &mut Bump` like `eval_if` (the fan-out's call sites).
- Nothing of `storage/transaction.rs` is deferred (see its ledger cell).

**Task 5**
- The `PluginContext` constructors in `smtp/src/scripts/event_loop.rs` and
  `email/src/sieve/ingest.rs` set `za_redact`; `za_eval_if`'s new arena argument at the
  spam filter's call sites.

**Task 6**
- The lease sweep's call site in the maintenance task's purge step
  (`services/src/task_manager/tasks/maintenance.rs`):
  `common::za::lease::sweep_expired_uid_leases(server.store())`, unchanged signature.
- `INDEX_WRITE_PAUSE` has no call site (0.16's `services::task_manager::index` is gone);
  `INDEX_CHUNK_PAUSE` and `INDEX_GUARD_FAIL_ONCE` still compile, without call sites.
- R51, R60 and R62's scans in `auth/vault.rs` (`za_has_*_search_documents`,
  `za_has_*_search_terms`) were ported mechanically onto 1.0's
  `SearchIndexClass::Document { index, account_id, document_id }` and Task 2's
  `has_account_search_terms`, so that the vault module compiles; whether they stay, and
  their semantics over 1.0's index layout, are Task 6's (inventory finding 10).

**Task 7 and Task 2**
- The emptiness check must know the lease's home (below): a lease key left in
  `Subspace::System` under prefix 27 is reported.

**Task 9**
- The OTEL service version: v1.0.1 builds the OTLP resource in the `telemetry` crate
  (`crates/telemetry/src/otlp/resource.rs:10`, `SERVER_VERSION`); the two `common` files
  were taken as supplied.

#### The UID lease's subspace (ruling 24)

The lease is `ValueClass::Any(AnyClass { subspace: Subspace::System, key })` in the data
store, with `key = ZA_UID_LEASE_PREFIX (27) || account id (u32 BE) || SHA-256(uid)`
(`common::za::lease::LEASE_SUBSPACE`). Evidence (paths in the worktree):
- `Subspace::System` (`store/src/lib.rs:174`) is a `Shape::Value` subspace. Its only other
  keys are the schema version (`SystemKind::SchemaVersion = 0x00`,
  `common/src/manager/schema.rs:26`, `:62`) and the cluster's node-id leases
  (`SystemKind::NodeId = 0x01 || u16`, `store/src/write/key.rs:516`, `:1063-1066`). Byte 27
  collides with neither, and the one iteration of the subspace, `NodeSlot::list`
  (`store/src/build/cluster.rs:160-197`), scans `ValueClass::NodeId(0)..NodeId(u16::MAX)`,
  which a lease key never falls in.
- None of the bulk deletions listed in the `store/src/dispatch/lookup.rs` ledger cell reaches
  it: `Store::purge_store` clears only zero counters in `Shape::Counter` subspaces;
  `danger_destroy_account` deletes per-account prefixes in `Counter`, `Logs`, `Indexes`,
  `Property`, `Immutable`, `IndexProperty` and the ACLs; the change-log truncation,
  the private-metadata cleanup, the telemetry purges and the search-queue clears work in
  their own subspaces. The console's range delete is operator-driven.
- `is_new_install` (`common/src/manager/schema.rs:75-100`) does not scan `System`. A restore
  into a store holding a live lease would count it as data (`common/src/manager/restore.rs:96`);
  leases last seconds, so only a restore during traffic meets it.
- The unit test `za::lease::tests::the_generic_purge_leaves_lease_keys` now runs
  `Store::purge_store` over expired and live leases (expectation ledger).

C7 is unchanged: fenced writes assert the lease (`ZaUidLease::assert_in`), and
`sweep_expired_uid_leases` is still the only cleanup, clearing each expired key with an
assertion on the value it read.

#### R54's classification (ruling 22)

`Server::za_has_tasks` first scans the index queue of each account-partitioned index
(`SearchIndexClass::queue_range(index, account_id)` for Email, Calendar, Contacts and File,
in the data store): any entry, an index (`1`) or an unindex (`0`), counts. 1.0's index work
has no `Task` variant, so this scan replaces 0.16's `IndexDocument` and `UnindexDocument`
arms; Tracing is left out because its partitions are span-id buckets, not accounts. It then
makes one pass over the task queue, matching every 1.0.1 `Task` variant exhaustively:

| `Task` variant | Counted | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `MergeThreads` | yes | Writes the account's threading data; a retained one holds a subject of up to 15 bytes verbatim. |
| `CalendarItipMessage` | yes | Writes an iTIP message into the account. |
| `CalendarAlarmEmail`, `CalendarAlarmNotification` | no | Content-free (ids and times), and possibly due days ahead. |
| `AccountMaintenance` | no | Scheduled for every account by the purge and reindex sweeps; any index work it causes goes through the index queue, which the first scan covers. |
| `DestroyAccount` | no | Not a provisioning state. |
| `ShareRevokeCleanup` (new in 1.0) | no | Purges grantee references after a revoked share of the owner's object (`GranteeCleanup` with `Purge::LostAccess`, guarded on the object's archive version, `services/src/task_manager/tasks/grantee_cleanup.rs:127-158`); it writes no message, event or index document of its own, and an owner with a shared object already fails the emptiness checks. Its skip for key accounts is Task 6's (interface sheet 3). |
| `DmarcReport`, `TlsReport`, `DmarcReportWindow`, `TlsReportWindow` (the windows are new in 1.0) | no | No account. |
| `StoreMaintenance`, `SpamFilterMaintenance`, `AcmeRenewal`, `DkimManagement`, `DnsManagement` | no | No account. |
| `RestoreArchivedItem`, `TenantMaintenance` (enterprise only) | no | Absent from the OSS product; one `cfg(feature = "enterprise")` wildcard arm covers them without naming an SEL-only variant (ruling 26's rule), and the product build stays exhaustive. |

The 409 "account has unfinished tasks" refusal is kept; its order before R51's scan is the
caller's (`http/src/api/vault.rs`, part 3b).

#### Follow-up: R46's class (found by part 5d)

`Server::za_has_property` scanned `ValueKey::property`, the property class, but at 1.0
`EmailField::Metadata` (field 71) is stored in the immutable class, so R46's refusal in
`za_assert_no_data` never saw a destroyed message's metadata row. Evidence, in
`v1.0.1-src/crates/`:
- `store/src/write/mod.rs:1741-1747`: `From<EmailField> for ValueClass` maps `Metadata` and
  `SortKeys` to `ValueClass::Immutable` and every other field to `ValueClass::Property`.
  `email/src/message/index/metadata.rs:36` (`set`) and `:41` (`clear`) write and remove the
  row through that conversion.
- `store/src/write/key.rs:799`: `ValueClass::Immutable` is `Subspace::Immutable`.
  `:785-797` asserts, in debug builds, that Email field 71 is never written as
  `ValueClass::Property`. So 0.16's scan would trip that assertion in a debug build, and in a
  release build it reads an empty range.
- Upstream reads the row the same way: `email/src/message/messagedata.rs:171` and
  `services/src/task_manager/tasks/destroy_account.rs:290`.
- R46's subject still exists at 1.0. A deleted message's row is cleared only when the index
  queue's worker processes its unindex entry: `services/src/index_queue/index.rs:856`
  calls `metadata.unindex(batch, ..)`, which clears field 71.

The fix: `za_has_property` now takes `field: impl Into<ValueClass>` and scans the class that
conversion yields, through the store-level `has_field_store`. The caller
(`http/src/api/vault.rs:914`) and the two Task 6 assertions in `tests/src/za/mail/fence.rs`
pass `EmailField::Metadata` unchanged, and now read the immutable class.
`za_has_documents(.., Field::ARCHIVE)` still scans the property class, where 1.0 keeps
archives (`From<Field> for ValueClass`, `store/src/write/mod.rs:1776-1780`;
`ValueKey::archive`, `store/src/write/key.rs:203`).

The unit test `auth::vault::tests::the_metadata_scan_reads_the_class_1_0_stores_it_in` writes
field 71 as upstream does, then asserts:
- that the conversion yields the immutable class;
- that the scan finds the row;
- that another account sees nothing;
- that the archive class sees nothing.

The other scans of `za_assert_no_data` and the provisioning fence were checked against 1.0's
classes, and none has the same problem:
- **The archive checks** (Email, CalendarEvent, CalendarEventNotification, Calendar):
  `Field::ARCHIVE` in the property class, as above.
- **R52**: `RegistryClass::Index { AccountId, SpamTrainingSample, .. }` is still where 1.0
  keeps the account index of a sample. `spam-filter/src/modules/classifier.rs:1005-1050`
  clears that index. It lives in `Subspace::RegistryIndex` (`store/src/write/key.rs:826`),
  serialized as object, index, key, then item (`:431-440`), so the scan covers one account.
- **R54**: written for 1.0 in part 3a (the index queue's `SearchIndexClass::Queue` and the
  task queue).
- **R51 and R60**: `SearchIndexClass::Document { index, account_id, document_id }` is the key
  1.0's index writer sets per document (`store/src/search/term.rs:375-383`).
- **R62**: Task 2's `has_account_search_terms` reads `Subspace::SearchTerm`, which
  `unindex_account` clears.
- **The provisioning and index fences**: `PrincipalField::ZeroAccessVault` maps to
  `ValueClass::Property` (`store/src/write/mod.rs:1759-1763`), where the vault record is
  written.

### Part 3b: HTTP, protocol logins and session ownership

Part 3b resolved the fourteen remaining Task 3 conflicts (cells in the conflict ledger,
including the files shared with Tasks 5 and 6 under ruling 41) and ported the non-conflicted
Task 3 sites: `http/src/api/vault.rs` (and the authentication surface of `contacts.rs`), the
JMAP registry refusals and the SMTP and ManageSieve logins. In outline:

- **The HTTP hit rule** (interface sheet 2; 3a's concern 1). 1.0's `HttpAuthEntries`
  hashes the raw token, so an entry cannot be dropped when its keys are evicted or swept.
  `za_cache_hit` (`http/src/auth/authenticate.rs`) is therefore the only C2 check on a hit:
  the account is read with `account()` (fail closed: both entries removed and the error
  returned); an ordinary account takes upstream's path; a key account's entry is served only
  with resident keys under the token's keyed fingerprint whose account is the entry's and
  whose generation is non-zero and equal to `AccountCache::za_generation()`. Anything else
  removes both entries and verifies in full. The miss path keeps upstream's revocation epoch
  on every insert and the 0.16 generation fence; keyless key-account results (Bearer,
  impersonation, a pre-setup hash) are never cached.
- **Ruling 10.** On a hit the marker is re-derived from the token itself
  (`za_is_impersonation`). Since a key account's entry without keys is never served and an
  impersonation never yields keys, the marker matters on a hit only for ordinary accounts,
  where it restricts nothing; it is kept as the ruling frames it.
- **Ruling 28.** The Basic decode error and the unsupported-mechanism error carry no
  Authorization value.
- **Key ownership.** HTTP keys ride `HttpAuthenticated.access_token` for one request and are
  dropped after the scope check on EventSource and WebSocket. IMAP and POP3 logins call
  `authenticate_with_keys` after upstream's bearer, scope and permission checks and refuse a
  keyless key-account session with `za_mail_login`; IMAP's renewal carries the keys at its
  call site (ruling 11). SMTP, ManageSieve and the OAuth login stay keyless.
- **Vault endpoints at 1.0.** `za_verify_primary` destructures `(Authenticated, keys)`;
  registry objects are boxed (`ObjectInner::Account(Box<Account>)`);
  `RegistryWriteResult::ForeignKeyOutOfScope` is named in the trace; `hash_secret` takes the
  password verifier; base64 is `encodify`'s. The default-calendar check reads 1.0's layout:
  a calendar's dead properties are no longer in its archive, so an untouched default
  calendar is one whose shared metadata container (`Server::metadata_container`) is absent.
  `setup-token`'s 0.16 tenant scoping is dropped: at 1.0 `AccessToken::tenant_id` and
  `UserAccount::member_tenant_id` exist only inside SEL snippets, so the scoping has no
  subject in the product build (see the report's concerns).
- **Tests.** `za::caches` gains `scoped_cache_hit_preserves_scope`,
  `revoked_grant_cannot_repopulate_cache` (the loader parked at `za_test::set`, the existing
  pause between verification and the cache insert), `expired_bearer_cache_entry_is_refused`,
  `keyless_key_account_logins_are_not_cached` and `impersonated_websocket_refresh`. OAuth
  tokens are minted in-process (`utils::za::za_grant_token`), since 1.0 binds every access
  token to a grant and resolves only registered, stateless or metadata-document clients.

### Task 3 rulings

41. **A conflicted file with several owners is resolved whole by its first owner** in task
    order. That owner ports its own hooks, and ports or defers the others', recording each
    deferral with its inventory row and owner, as under ruling 34. Cost if wrong: a later
    owner re-ports a deferred hook from `9601038de`.
42. **Task 3 ran as two dispatches.** 3a did `directory`, `common` and `vault`, which compile
    now. 3b did the HTTP and protocol login sites, which compile at Tasks 5 and 6.
43. **Task 3's review ran on Fable at xhigh.** The plan sends authority kernels to the
    strongest permitted reviewer, and Jay gave standing permission for Fable reviews.
44. **A restored test that fails on 1.0's behaviour is adapted by kind:**
    - an intended change gets its assertion updated, with an expectation-ledger entry;
    - a timing artefact waits for the outcome;
    - an apparent upstream bug keeps its subject where it survives, and gets a case
      documenting 1.0's behaviour.

    No test is ignored silently. The two apparent upstream bugs found so far are recorded
    privately.
45. **The fork's tenant check on `setup-token` is dropped, with the SEL-only disposition.**
    At 1.0 the tenant fields exist only inside SEL snippets. So the OSS product has no
    tenants, and enterprise binaries are never deployed. Cost if wrong: an enterprise build
    wouldn't scope a tenant admin's setup tokens, an unsupported configuration.
46. **An HTTP cache hit reads the account cache for every account** (the Task 3 review's
    F4). The hit rule classifies each cached entry through `account()`, which fails closed.
    So an `account()` error on a hit refuses where upstream would serve, and a recovery
    admin's hit does an uncached registry lookup. This is an accepted failure-mode deviation
    under C4: the alternative would serve a key account's entry unclassified. Cost if wrong:
    a registry outage refuses cached logins it could have served.

## Task 4

### Part 4a: `groupware`

Part 4a resolved `groupware`'s six conflicted files and ported every fork module of the crate
onto 1.0.1's split calendar records (commit `a92064a43`). The DAV, dead-property and HTTP
shares call sites are part 4b's; the new za tests are part 4c's. `cargo check -p groupware`
and `cargo check -p groupware --tests --features test_mode` are warning-free, and
`cargo test -p groupware --lib` passes 162 tests (with or without `test_mode`).

The shape of the port:

- **The event pair.** At 1.0 an event is a metadata row (`CalendarEvent`) and a content record
  (`CalendarEventContent`). The seal codec works on an in-memory pair,
  `seal::ZaEvent { meta, content }`, which callers split back into the two records upstream's
  writers take. `seal_event_in_domain` seals the content's tree in place and moves the row's
  `display_name` into `X-ZA-EXTRA` (ruling 15: a key account's row holds `None`). It runs
  before upstream's builders, so `refresh_from_content` and the index see only the sealed
  tree. Read views return `ZaEventView { content, display_name }`: the unsealed content with
  the stored content archive's version, and the bundle's display name.
- **Extras.** `Extra` and `ExtraV2` lose `dead_properties` (format 1 and 2 otherwise
  unchanged). The calendar `Bundle` loses `dead_properties` too; a shared calendar keeps its
  five-part ring format, with a shared bundle that is now always empty.
- **Per-user data.** `seal_event_in_domain` refuses content whose `preferences` are not empty
  (`SealError::Structure("event preferences")`), so a key account's per-user event data can
  never be stored, sealed or not.
- **Conflicts.** `calendar/mod.rs` takes 1.0.1's module list plus the fork's
  (`sequence_floor` per ruling 25) and moves `EVENT_NOTIFICATION_ZA_PENDING` to `1 << 15`
  with a compile-time check against every upstream notification flag (ruling 30).
  `dates.rs` adds `CalendarEventData::new_for(…, &DefaultAlerts, key_account)`: for a key
  account the rule-expansion trace names each error's kind and drops 1.0.1's new `Contents`
  value (the whole tree). `itip.rs` keeps `SendSealed` and the RSVP refusals, and puts the
  provisioning fence in every commit chunk of the iMIP import and merge (before the event
  write, before the notification quota's eviction, before the notification insert and at the
  end). `event_cancel.rs` keeps `itip_cancel_from_snapshot`, which returns the raw message
  before upstream's per-recipient split; `itip_cancel` splits as upstream does. `storage.rs`
  is 1.0.1's with the hooks below.

#### Notification mapping (ruling 13)

- **Direct changes.** `notify_direct_change` returns early when `za_is_key_account(account_id)`
  (`storage.rs`, the one fork call). `notify_calendar_addition` and `notify_calendar_removal`
  go through it, as do the DAV and JMAP callers.
- **Sealed inbox items** (`seal::item::sealed_item_record`), built without reading plaintext:

  | Field | Value |
  |---|---|
  | `event_id` | the linked event when known (an import records the new event's slot, through `into_builder`'s pending id), else `None` |
  | `changed_by` | the sender, `ChangedBy::PrincipalId` |
  | `calendar_ids` | empty |
  | `flags` | `EVENT_NOTIFICATION_PRIVATE` (upstream's `NotificationAudience::Members`: only the owner's members see it), plus `EVENT_NOTIFICATION_ZA_PENDING` while pending, plus `EVENT_NOTIFICATION_IS_CHANGE` on a drained merge |
  | `size`, `etag` | upstream's builder: `refresh_from_content` sums the sealed snapshot's size, and the etag hashes the sealed content archive |
  | `created`, `modified` | upstream's insert; the drain's rewrite keeps `created` |
  | content | `CalendarEventNotificationContent::Itip { message: <the sealed tree>, previous: None, current: None }` |

- **The drain's mirror** rewrites the item row with `SplitUpdate::full` over the stored row and
  content (both asserted), with the drained tree resealed under the master key and no
  snapshot. Upstream's import creates a notification next to the event; the drain's item row
  is that record, so it writes none.
- **Hiding.** `NotificationViewer::can_view` (`notification.rs`) refuses an item with the
  pending bit (spec 6.6), so JMAP's notification views never list one. DAV's inbox listing
  does not go through `can_view`; its filter is part 4b's.
- **Deletion before the drain sees an item.**
  - The reaper (`reap_calendar_notifications`) selects only rows whose non-empty
    `calendar_ids` all lie in the deleted calendars, so it never selects a fork item.
  - Quota eviction (`NotificationQuota::reserve`) deletes the account's oldest notification on
    two paths only. `notify_direct_change` returns early for a key account before it reserves.
    `itip_ingest` and `commit_itip_merge` reach only accounts read as ordinary, and every
    commit chunk of their batch carries the provisioning fence, so on a key account nothing
    commits, eviction included. The fan-out's item writes do not reserve. JMAP's calendar
    copy reserves, but JMAP calendars are refused for key accounts.
  - `itip_auto_expunge` expunges notifications older than the hold period, pending or not,
    as 0.16 did. Recorded as parity, not new.

#### Ruling 14: the create's id before the fan-out

**The property holds.** An id reserved by a batch that commits is never reused:

- `BatchBuilder::reserve_document_id` adds a `ReservationClass::DocumentId` reservation
  (`store/src/write/batch.rs:105`). Its key is the per-account, per-collection counter
  `ValueClass::DocumentId` (`CounterKind::DocumentId`, `Subspace::Counter`;
  `store/src/write/mod.rs:906-916`, `key.rs:493`).
- At commit each backend reads that counter inside the transaction, writes `counter + count`
  and assigns `last - count + 1 ..= last` (RocksDB `backend/rocksdb/write.rs:221-246`, SQLite
  `backend/sqlite/write.rs:69-76`, FoundationDB `allocate_counters`;
  `AssignedIds::fill_slots`, `write/mod.rs:1570`). A transaction that aborts allocates
  nothing.
- The counter only grows. No code decrements it; `purge_store` deletes only zero-valued
  counters, and a `DocumentId` counter is at least 1 after its first allocation;
  `danger_destroy_account` clears it with the account.
- A batch with reservations and no operations is still written: `write_batch` always runs
  the final commit point (`batch.rs:1072-1092`), and `commit_batch` does not skip it.
- 0.16's `assign_document_ids` was an `add_and_get` on the same counter
  (0.16.27 `store/src/dispatch/store.rs:197-215`), so the property is the one key pinning
  spec 5.2 relied on.

Use (part 4b): commit a batch holding only `reserve_document_id(account_id,
Collection::CalendarEvent)`, read `AssignedIds::slot(slot)`, pass it as `ZaSource.document_id`
to the fan-out, then create the event with `PendingId::Assigned(id)`. In `groupware`, the
fan-out's item writes and the drain's import reserve their ids in their own batch, since
nothing before their commit needs the id.

#### Ruling 25: the sequence re-read

- **The floor stays.** Upstream's `ICalendarSequence` (`has_scheduling_changes`,
  `increment_sequence`) is called only by JMAP's `CalendarEvent/set`
  (`jmap/src/calendar_event/set.rs:899-919`), never by DAV PUT. It raises a lowered
  SEQUENCE to the previous value plus one, matching components by type and recurrence
  instant at floating time. DAV PUT therefore still needs `za_sequence_floor`
  (`sequence_floor.rs`, part 4b's call site).
- **The reconciliation stays.** 1.0.1's `organizer_handle_update` shares one
  `SequenceIncrement` between the full and partial updates, returns `increment_sequences` to
  the caller, and builds each CANCEL at the highest of old+1, the increment and the main
  sequence (`scheduling/organizer.rs:260-470`). As far as the source shows, that fixes the
  0.16 defect the reconciliation compensated (a shadowing local that lost a partial's
  increments). The reconciliation only raises values to each instance's maximum, so on
  1.0.1's output it is a no-op where messages and the committed tree agree. Kept unchanged;
  a test that it is a no-op on 1.0.1's output would let a later task retire it.
- **`carries_master`** (`fanout.rs`) still holds: 1.0.1 keeps the partial-drop block that
  sends a master REQUEST as the full update and drops that recipient's partials
  (`organizer.rs:225-249`).
- **Identifiers.** The five fork units move from `calendar::sequence::tests::*` to
  `calendar::sequence_floor::tests::*` (Task 7's baseline mapping).

#### `delete_changes` and `CalendarIdentity`

- `delete_changes` (`common/src/storage/transaction.rs:116-170`) clears
  `VanishedCollection::CalendarIdentity` (250) over the same range as a Calendar truncation
  of 252. Confirmed by source.
- `CalendarIdentity`'s change group is Calendar's: `sync_collection()` maps it to
  `SyncCollection::Calendar` and `change_group()` follows (`types/src/collection.rs:114-139`),
  so both records of one deletion share the change id and the log key's group.
- `storage.rs` logs calendar deletions and event unlinks with `log_calendar_vanished`
  (calendar delete: its own id; event: its calendar's id). The other callers (DAV COPY and
  MOVE, JMAP set) are part 4b's and Task 6's.
- `store/src/query/log.rs` needs nothing more from `groupware`; the paged sync tokens at the
  consumer (inventory finding 3) are part 4b's.

#### Field policy 2

- `seal::policy::is_visible_entry(component, entry)` replaces `is_visible_property`, which is
  now private to the module. CLASS is visible on VEVENT, VTODO and VJOURNAL when its one value
  is `Classification(Public | Private | Confidential)`; any other value, a second value or
  another component seals it. Callers: `seal/tree.rs` (`seal_component` and its test),
  `seal/event.rs` (`classify_event_storage`), `sharing/format.rs` (`sealed_timezone`). The leak
  scanner (`tests/src/za/leak.rs:457`) must switch to it (part 4c).
- `POLICY_VERSION` stays 1.
- No fork code computes `EVENT_PRIVATE` or `EVENT_SECRET`. The drain's merge keeps the stored
  classification exactly as `commit_itip_merge` does (`EventPrivacy::from_flags` on the
  stored row, `set_privacy` when a scheduling component's merged CLASS differs).
- Units: `standard_class_is_visible_on_event_task_and_journal`,
  `other_class_values_and_components_stay_sealed`, `a_parsed_extension_class_stays_sealed`,
  and `drain::tests::standard_class_stays_visible_and_derives_the_flags` (a merged, sealed
  tree through upstream's `refresh_from_content`: PRIVATE and CONFIDENTIAL set their flag,
  `X-SOMETHING` is sealed and leaves both clear, the recorded limit).

#### Dead-property containers (rulings 16, 23 and 29)

`seal/container.rs` holds the codec. The wrapper is built with upstream's
`MetadataBuilder::set_dav` and `encode`: header `[FORMAT_VERSION, DAV, 1]`, one DAV entry
`{urn:x-zero-access:sealed}container`, a text-only value. The text is base64 of:

```text
payload  = 1 (version) || bound (0 or 1) || envelope || sealed
envelope = the event shape: 1 || 1 || wrapped DEK (event wrapping key, AAD
           za/v1|container-key|<owner>|<scope>), or 1 || 3 || calendar id ||
           generation || wrapped DEK (collection key, purpose ck-container-dek)
sealed   = seal_padded(DEK, za/v1|dead-container|<owner>|<scope>, container bytes)
scope    = <collection byte> | <bound> | <event UID>        (an event)
           <collection byte> | <bound> | <calendar id, BE>  (a calendar, bound 1)
           <collection byte> | 0 |                           (a calendar sealed at MKCOL)
```

Each container has its own data key. A calendar's container binds its document id when the id
is assigned at sealing; one sealed by MKCOL (a pending slot) does not, until its next property
write. That is ruling 16's recorded limit: under the owner's event wrapping key, such a
container could be moved between the account's own unshared calendars by someone with store
write access (integrity only; under a collection key the envelope binds the calendar id
anyway). An event's container binds its UID, which COPY and MOVE keep.

**The conversion journal's identity rule for containers (ruling 29).** A container is
identified by its document: owner account, collection and document id. Conversion records
nothing per container; the container's own envelope class (event wrapping key or collection
key) is its state, as an event's envelope is.

- An event's container is rewrapped in the same commit chunk as the event's own rewrap, ahead
  of `update_full_at` under the context and guard assertions, and conditional on the stored
  container's hash (upstream's `MetadataWrite`).
- The calendar's container moves with the ring write: owner key to collection key at `Init`,
  collection key to owner key at `Restore`.
- An event that is already in the target class but whose container is not gets a
  container-only write (a resume after an interruption).
- Forward validation requires every container of the calendar's events to be under the
  collection key and to open in the domain; rollback validation refuses one that still is.
- Rotation needs no container rewrite: readers hold every generation and writers use the
  newest, as for events.

#### Primitives for part 4b

- **Seal API** (`calendar::seal`): `ZaEvent`, `ZaEventView`; `seal_event(&mut ZaEvent, keys,
  account_id, pairs)`, `seal_event_in_domain(&mut ZaEvent, domain, pairs)`,
  `unseal_event`, `unseal_event_pairs`, `unseal_event_in_domain(&mut ZaEvent, domain)`,
  `rewrap_event(&mut ZaEvent, from, to)`; on stored archives `event_token_pairs(&content,
  keys, account_id)`, `unseal_event_archive(&meta, &content, keys, account_id)`,
  `event_pairs_in_domain(&meta, &content, domain)`, `event_view_in_domain(&meta, &content,
  domain)` (`meta` is the deserialized row, `content` the content archive);
  `classify_event_storage(&CalendarEventContent)`,
  `archived_event_is_sealed(&ArchivedCalendarEventContent)`, `za_event_uid(&content)`.
- **Ruling 15.** `reseal_event_in_domain(&mut ZaEvent, domain, edit)` unseals the stored pair,
  applies `edit` (set `meta.display_name`), and seals again under a fresh data key with the
  same token pairs. A displayname PROPPATCH then writes `CalendarEvent::update_full` with the
  result; the row keeps `display_name: None`.
- **Writes** (`storage.rs`): `update_full_at`, `update_meta_at`, `insert_at` (fenced rebuilds
  keep their timestamps); `delete_with_events_za(…, za_keys) -> Vec<ZaDeleteMessage>` (asserts
  the calendar's context in every event's chunk and before the calendar delete);
  `delete_za_at(…, domain, cutoff) -> Option<ZaDeleteMessage>`; `remove_all_za_at`. The caller
  asserts the event's context in the chunk of `delete_za_at`. `ZaDeleteMessage.messages` is
  `Option<Vec<ItipMessage>>`, upstream's `itip_cancel` after its per-recipient split.
- **Scheduling**: `ItipSendStatus::resolve(…, owner_is_key)` (JMAP passes `false`);
  `itip_cancel(…, policy)` is upstream's; `itip_cancel_from_snapshot` returns the raw message.
- **Containers**: `seal_container(plain, domain, ZaContainerScope)`, `open_container(sealed,
  domain, ZaContainerOf)`, `rewrap_container`, `reseal_container`, `is_sealed_container`,
  `container_is_ck`, `CONTAINER_NAMESPACE`, `CONTAINER_NAME`. At the DAV sites: PROPPATCH opens
  the stored container for the edit's view, compares plaintext, and seals `next` with
  `Calendar(Some(id))` for an existing calendar, `Calendar(None)` at MKCOL and `Event(uid)`
  for an event, before `ContainerChange::new`; PROPFIND opens into an owned buffer; COPY and
  MOVE reseal with `reseal_container` when the key domain changes (an event's scope, its UID,
  is unchanged).
- **Sealed items**: `seal::item::{sealed_item_record, item_message, into_item_message}`;
  `unseal_item_archive` now takes the item's content archive.
- `CalendarEventData::new_for(ical, tz, max, &DefaultAlerts, key_account)`.

#### The viewer-side readers

- `privacy.rs`: `EventPrivacy::from_flags` reads flags upstream derives from the visible
  CLASS, keyless, as upstream does. `check_access`, `may_be_set_by` and `into_private_view`
  run in DAV on the unsealed view (part 4b).
- `hidden.rs`: used only by JMAP, which refuses key accounts' calendars.
- `participants.rs`: used by `index.rs` (the calendar search document, Task 6), `identity.rs`,
  `instance_filter.rs` and the alarm task (`services`, Task 6). On a sealed tree they find no
  participants: no leak, less function. DAV runs them on the unsealed view (part 4b).
- `overlap.rs`: used by `dates.rs` from TRANSP and STATUS, both visible, so the same on a
  sealed tree.
- `notification.rs`: `can_view` hides pending items; the reaper is safe (above).
- `alerts.rs`: `DefaultAlertsResolver` runs on the plaintext view in the drain and in
  `storage.rs`, and reads the calendar's default alerts, which are visible. DAV GET and COPY
  are part 4b's.

### Part 4b: DAV, JMAP refusals and the HTTP endpoints

Part 4b resolved every conflict in `dav`, ported the 0.16 `propfind.rs` hooks into
`common/propfind/` (the file is deleted), resolved `jmap/src/api/request.rs` whole (ruling 41)
and `jmap/src/calendar_event/set.rs`, and ported `http/src/api/{shares,contacts}.rs` beyond the
login code (commits `4beb2ae0f`, `79695301b`, `ae37bdd7d`). `cargo check -p dav --tests` and
`cargo check -p groupware -p dav --tests --features test_mode` are warning-free;
`cargo test -p dav --lib` passes 2 tests (one new: `tombstones_need_identity_coverage`). `jmap`
and `http` compile at Task 6: their four files are rustfmt-clean and were checked against
v1.0.1's signatures by reading. One `groupware` line changed: `ZaKeyDomain::owner` is public, for
MKCOL's container (below).

#### The dead-property wrapper at the DAV sites (rulings 16, 23 and 29)

The wrapper is 4a's (`seal/container.rs`, layout in part 4a): header `[FORMAT_VERSION, DAV, 1]`,
one DAV entry `{urn:x-zero-access:sealed}container` whose text-only value is base64 of
`1 || bound || envelope || seal_padded(DEK, za/v1|dead-container|<owner>|<scope>, container)`.
The DAV sites choose the scope and domain as follows:

| Site | Domain | Scope sealed | Scope opened |
|---|---|---|---|
| MKCOL (`calendar/mkcol.rs`) | the owner's (`ZaKeyDomain::owner`) | `Calendar(None)`, bound 0: the id is a pending slot (ruling 16's recorded limit) | none (no stored container) |
| Calendar PROPPATCH | the calendar's fresh mutation context | `Calendar(Some(id))`, bound 1 | `Calendar(id)` (either bound) |
| Event PROPPATCH | the event's write context (its calendar's) | `Event(uid)` | `Event(uid)` |
| PROPFIND, REPORT, `propname` | the item's read context (memoized per request) | none | as PROPPATCH |
| Event MOVE across key domains | source context to target context | `Event(uid)`, resealed under a fresh data key (`reseal_container`) in the event's own commit | `Event(uid)` |
| Calendar COPY or MOVE (`za_copy_container`, `za_move_overwrite_container`) | source context to the owner's | `Calendar(Some(target))`, the target id from a reservation-only batch (ruling 14) or the overwritten id | `Calendar(source)` |

- **PROPPATCH** (`dead/patch.rs`): `DeadPatch::apply_za(.., Some(&ZaDeadSeal))` opens the stored
  container into the builder's view, measures `previous_len` and the entry bound on the
  plaintext, compares plaintext (an unchanged container writes nothing), and seals `next` before
  `ContainerChange::new`, whose previous value is the stored sealed container, so the hash CAS is
  upstream's. The `check_edit` limit runs on the plaintext `next`; the quota check runs on the
  sealed change, which is what is stored. `apply` is `apply_za(.., None, ..)`.
- **PROPFIND**: the container is opened at the callers of `dead_properties` and
  `dead_property_names` (`propfind/mod.rs`, both the `propname` branch and the main loop, before
  `add_propfind_item` and `into_property_names`) into an owned `MetadataBuf` that keeps the
  stored size and hash. A container that does not open fails that item (500).
- **COPY within an account** adds a link to the same document, so the event's container is
  unchanged; a COPY into or out of a shared calendar is refused (`shared_copy_refusal`), so a
  COPY never changes key domain.
- Keyless paths (`stored_entries`, `MetadataBuf::read`, quota, cleanup, purge) are upstream's, on
  the sealed bytes.
