| 0.16 site | 1.0 site |
|---|---|
| `propfind.rs` `PropFindData.calendar_memo`, `new` | `propfind/data.rs` (field and constructor) |
| `propfind.rs:278` root discovery (`foreign_home_visible`) | `propfind/mod.rs` `handle_propfind_request`, before `prepare_principal_propfind_response` |
| `propfind.rs:322` `za_home_listing` | `propfind/mod.rs` `handle_dav_query` |
| `propfind.rs:405`, `:475` `propname` context, pending items, 404 rule | `propfind/mod.rs` `propname` branch through `za::za_propname_item` and `dav_query_response` |
| `propfind.rs:508`, `:525` stored/view split, `za_archive_view` | `propfind/mod.rs` main loop through `za::za_propfind_item` and `ZaViews::apply`, after the batch load or the resource-cache answer and before `calendar_event_view` (inventory finding 4) |
| `propfind.rs:599`, `:1118` trace ids | `CalendarQueryHandler::for_content(.., account_id, document_id)` in `propfind/{mod,item}.rs` |
| `propfind.rs:1255` tail | `dav_query_response` |
| `propfind.rs:1288` helpers | `propfind/za.rs` (`dav_query_response`, `foreign_calendar_contexts`, `invalid_calendar_sync_token`, `tombstone_prefixes`, `authorize_calendar_tombstones`) |
| `propfind.rs:1417` foreign sync | `propfind/get.rs` `get`: both tombstone streams read with `vanished_with_change_id` over `Query::Range(from, snapshot)` of a `State` or `ChangesPage` token (inventory finding 3); boundary refusal compares the token's `from` |
| `propfind.rs:1565` subtree restriction | `propfind/get.rs`, after `display_children` |
| `propfind.rs:1597` truncated-change refusal | no code: 1.0's `needs_full_rebuild` refuses a truncated change set for every account, and `ChangesPage` pages the rest |
| `propfind.rs:1668` authorized tombstones | `propfind/get.rs` vanished list |
| `propfind.rs:1818`, `:1856` assisted discovery | `propfind/get.rs` shared-account loop |
| `propfind.rs:1918` multiget collection | `propfind/get.rs` `multiget` |
| `encode_path_segment` (prefixes, shares href) | `common::storage::dav::canonical_path_segment` and `DavResourceName::account_base_path` (inventory finding 6) |
| `copy_move.rs` `assert_value(.., AlignedBytes)`, `ValueClass::ChangeId` | `ArchiveBytes`; `ValueClass::ChangeId(Calendar group)`, `last_change_id(owner, Calendar)` |
| `update.rs` `assign_document_ids` | reservation-only batch, then `PendingId::Assigned` (ruling 14) |
| `update.rs`, `delete.rs` `scheduling_account_info` | `calendar::za_roles` (sender = writer plus owner's addresses; 1.0's handlers pass the owner's) |
| `CalendarEvent::update_at` / `insert_at` | `update_full_at`, `update_meta_at`, `insert_at` over the stored row and content |
| `sequence::{za_sequence_floor, za_set_sequences}` | `sequence_floor::..` (ruling 25) |

#### Decisions recorded for the primary

- **1.0's whole-request conflict retry** (`request.rs`, `MAX_CONFLICT_RETRIES`) is skipped when
  the token holds session keys. The fork's handlers run their own bounded retry, and a replay
  would run a fan-out again after its items committed (invitations spec 5.4). Plain sessions
  keep upstream's retry.
- **Per-user data on a key account's write.** A sharee's PUT is not split into per-user
  preferences (`is_user_data_only` is false and `extract_user_data` is skipped): the whole tree is
  shared data, as at 0.16, and `seal_event_in_domain` would refuse non-empty preferences.
- **`za_assert_is_unique_uid`** follows 1.0's `assert_is_unique_uid`: 403 `NoUidConflict` (0.16
  answered 412), and a sharee whose only conflict is a secret event gets a plain 403.
- **Own-items rights.** 1.0's own-item paths (`EventAcl::may_manage_own_items`) read ownership
  from the stored tree, which is sealed for a key account, so they refuse (403); a key
  account's writes still need the fork's operation bits through the fresh context. Fail-closed,
  recorded.
- **Sizes.** A key account's stored `size` is the sealed tree's. PROPFIND's metadata view, GET's
  and the inbox's HEAD measure the plaintext view instead.

#### The viewer-side readers at the DAV sites

- `dav/calendar/view.rs`, `filter.rs` and `CalendarQueryHandler` run on the unsealed content:
  PROPFIND and REPORT replace the stored content before `calendar_event_view` and the filters;
  GET and PUT pass the view; free-busy unseals before the scan.
- `DefaultAlertsResolver` runs on the view in GET, PUT and PROPFIND. The key path of COPY never
  serves content, and upstream's COPY paths that run it are unreachable for key accounts
  (cross-account copies are refused).
- The privacy flags are read from the resource cache, as upstream does
  (`assert_event_privacy_access`, `EventPrivacy::from_flags`); no fork code reads or overrides
  them.
- The inbox (ruling 13): PROPFIND and `propname` leave out pending items; GET answers 404 for
  one and unseals a drained one's content.

| `split_metadata_only_update_keeps_sealed_content`: a dead-property PROPPATCH (container sealed; the presence bit set on the row by `rewrite_presence`), a rename MOVE (`update_meta`) and the property's removal, each leaving the content record byte for byte; owner and grantee reads keep the row's ETag (304 on it); the scan of row, content and container; negative control: a row-only write of a protected display name, which the scan must catch | `dav_seal` | `dav_seal::test_dead_properties` |
| Per-user event data: `assert_no_event_preferences` (`utils/za.rs`) after a PUT (`dav_seal::test`), COPY and MOVE (`test_dead_properties`), a metadata-only write (`split_…`), the organizer's scheduling write (`fanout::test`), the drain's import and merge (`drain::test`), a sharee's PUT (ruling 49, the differential) and the inbound case | several | — |
| Field policy 2, the differential: a key and an ordinary owner each share `class/` with a sharee (account API "edit"; DAV ACL read and write); GET, HEAD and PROPFIND per event, PROPFIND depth 1, calendar-query and multiget, then PUT, PROPPATCH, LOCK/UNLOCK, MOVE, MOVE back, DELETE and GET give equal statuses and equal normalized bodies; the privacy rule on both sides (PUBLIC 200, PRIVATE 403, CONFIDENTIAL 404); the stored bits after PUT, the sharee's writes, PROPPATCH, MOVE, COPY and a CLASS change from PRIVATE to PUBLIC; the recorded limits: free-busy (ruling 51: the ordinary grantee gets the public event's busy period, the key account's grantee none, `Withheld`), a sharee's COPY (403 on the key side, `shared_copy_refusal`; 201 on the ordinary side) and `CLASS:X-SOMETHING` (sealed, both bits clear, served in full to the key account's sharee; the ordinary account derives Private and refuses its sharee) | `sharing::sharing_class_differential` | `sharing::test` |
| Field policy 2, inbound: a CLASS change from PRIVATE to PUBLIC through `itip_ingest` (two ordinary accounts) and through the drain's merge (two key accounts); the copy's CLASS lines and stored bits are equal on the two paths after the import and after the merge, and the bits follow the kept CLASS | `sharing::sharing_class_inbound` | `sharing_class_differential` |
| `cleanup_cannot_restore_revoked_ring`: after the revoke, its queued `ShareRevokeCleanup`, a refresh of every cache and a later grant to a third account, the revoked account gets exactly what a never-granted account gets (read and write) and is never listed again | `sharing` | `sharing_class_inbound` |
| `revoke_cleanup_does_not_rewrite_sealed_event`: the grantee writes (a PUT and a dead property), the revoke commits, then a `ShareRevokeCleanup` of the calendar is queued explicitly (so the worker certainly runs) and must leave every event's row and content bytes and ETag as the revoke left them; no record holds plaintext; negative control: a planted plaintext event the same scan catches | `sharing` | `cleanup_cannot_restore_revoked_ring` |
| `incoming_timezone_update_survives_seal`, end to end (4a's unit has the same name): a moved occurrence with a new VTIMEZONE through the fan-out and the drain's merge; the copy keeps the zone and the override's TZID, stores the zone's name sealed, and a time-range REPORT finds the occurrence at 14:00Z and not at its old slot | `drain` | `drain::test_errors` |

The leak scanner (`leak.rs`) uses `is_visible_entry` and adds its own CLASS rule, independent
of the product's predicate: a visible CLASS holds exactly one standard classification on a
VEVENT, VTODO or VJOURNAL. The rest of `leak.rs` (its event checks on the 1.0 records and the
new subspaces) is Task 6's.

#### Fixture names allocated

All at example.com: `deadprop-owner`, `deadprop-reader` (dav_seal); `split-meta-owner`,
`split-meta-reader` (dav_seal); `sharing-class-owner`, `sharing-class-reader`,
`sharing-class-plain-owner`, `sharing-class-plain-reader`, `sharing-class-organizer`,
`sharing-class-attendee`, `sharing-class-plain-organizer`, `sharing-class-plain-attendee`;
`sharing-revoke-owner`, `sharing-revoke-reader`, `sharing-revoke-later`. The ordinary accounts
are destroyed by their tests (the suite destroys only key accounts) and are not inserted in
the suite's account map. Neither sharing file used the `sharing-class-` or `sharing-revoke-`
labels before.

#### How the existing modules were adapted

- **Helpers** (`utils/za.rs`): `insert_event` writes the pair with `CalendarEvent::insert` and
  a reserved id; `StoredEvent` (row and content, `za_event`, `tree`, `bytes`, `as_bytes`,
  `etag`, `pairs_in`, `next_alarm_time`, and a `version` pair of both records' versions) with
  `stored_event` and `write_stored_event` (upstream's `update_full`) replace 0.16's one archive
  and `CalendarEvent::update`; `InboxItem` (the row's fields, and the iTIP message as `event`)
  replaces `CalendarEventNotification` in the inbox helpers, which read 1.0's split item and
  write `sealed_item_record`'s shape; `inbox_ids` reads the items' `Created` index (0.16's
  `itip_ids` is gone); `plain_dav_container` and `plant_container` write an unsealed container
  through upstream's keyless writer; `shared_container` reads one.
- **`dav_seal::raw_event`** returns the content record (the stored tree); `raw_event_records`
  returns both. Its callers in other modules are ported too:
  - `za/gating.rs`: the schedule tag is read from the row (`raw_event_records`), the tree from
    the content (`CalendarEventContent`);
  - `webdav/za_variants.rs`: the schedule tag from the row, and both canary scans over row and
    content (`StoredEvent::bytes`);
  - `za/leak.rs`: the three `raw_event` calls only. The fan-out leak test reads both records
    as one `ZaEvent` for `check_event`, whose port to the pair (with the scanner's
    new-subspace rules) stays Task 6's; the pairs test reads the content record.
- **Ruling 50** (`za/uid_lease.rs`): the four UID-conflict outcomes (a stale snapshot, an
  expired holder, a lost commit fence, a second creator) assert 403 where 0.16 asserted 412;
  the 503 and 201 cases are unchanged.
- **Sharing files**: event reads go through `StoredEvent`; `ZaEvent` field paths
  (`meta.names`, `content.data.event`); calendar dead properties are containers; 0.16's
  `delete_za` is 1.0's `delete_za_at`; `Store::write(batch.build_all())` is `write_batch`; the
  log subspace is `Subspace::Logs`; `last_change_id` takes the change group.
- **Registry renames**: `CalendarScheduling.itip_max_size` is `max_itip_size`
  (`Property::MaxItipSize`); `EmailAlias.enabled` is `enable`.

#### Assumptions Task 7 must confirm at runtime

1. A dead-property-only PROPPATCH on a key account's event writes the row through
   `rewrite_presence` only, so the content record keeps its bytes and version; so does a rename
   MOVE within a shared calendar (`update_meta`).
2. GET and PROPFIND serve a key account's event with the row's `etag` as the ETag (the view
   keeps the stored version's identity), and a conditional GET on it answers 304.
3. A corrupted sealed container fails its PROPFIND item with 500 and leaves GET at 200.
4. Apple's `calendar-color` and `calendar-order` are dead properties at 1.0 (dav-proto has no
   live property for them).
5. `destroy_owner_metadata` clears both the owner's `Viewer` counter and the viewer's `Owner`
   record, so the planted counters leave nothing for `assert_is_empty`.
6. The grant converts the calendar's and its events' containers to the collection key
   (ruling 29), and a MOVE from an unshared calendar into a shared one is admitted and
   reseals the container.
7. The differential's normalization (owner name, ETag, timestamps, tokens and DTSTAMP lines
   removed) leaves nothing else that differs between a key and an ordinary account; HEAD
   bodies are empty on both. "edit" and DAV `read`+`write` give a sharee the same rights.
   Free-busy is outside the equality block (ruling 51): both answer 200 with a VFREEBUSY, the
   ordinary grantee's holding the public event's period and the key account grantee's none.
8. An ordinary sharee's COPY within the shared calendar answers 201 (the recorded limit's
   other side), and the ordinary account's `X-SOMETHING` event derives Private and refuses its
   sharee with 403.
9. The inbound case's ordinary organizer schedules through upstream's local delivery, and the
   attendee's `itip_ingest` adds the copy to the default calendar it created; the drain's
   merge keeps the same CLASS as `commit_itip_merge`.
10. The revoke through the account API leaves the events readable by the owner, and a
    `ShareRevokeCleanup` of a key owner's calendar runs to completion without writing (this
    holds only once Task 6 makes the worker skip key-account owners).
11. The fork's flags on inbox items, as ruling 13's mapping records them (Part 4a): a drained
    item's row holds `EVENT_NOTIFICATION_PRIVATE`, plus `EVENT_NOTIFICATION_IS_CHANGE` after a
    merge. An item the drain rejects and keeps as history holds `EVENT_NOTIFICATION_PRIVATE`
    alone, which is what the lifecycle test asserts.

#### Known failures until another task lands

- `sharing_move_atomic`'s byte-oversize admission case expects `bytes > 5_000_000` from
  `take_calendar_move_plans`; part 4b records 0 bytes until Task 6 supplies a byte measure.
- `revoke_cleanup_does_not_rewrite_sealed_event` fails until Task 6's `ShareRevokeCleanup`
  skips key-account owners, if 1.0's worker rewrites their events.

#### Handoffs

- **Task 6:** `za/leak.rs`'s `check_event` takes the 1.0 pair (`ZaEvent`), with the
  scanner's new-subspace rules.
- **Task 5:** `utils/za.rs`'s mail helper `plant_plain_message`.


### Task 4 rulings

47. **Task 4 ran as three parts:** 4a (`groupware`), 4b (DAV, dead properties, shares and
    the JMAP calendar refusals) and 4c (tests). Its review ran on Fable at xhigh.
48. **No whole-request conflict retry for a session that holds keys.** A replay would run the
    fan-out again after its items committed. This also covers a key-account session writing
    to an ordinary owner's shared calendar (the review's M2, a C4 residue: that write's
    conflict goes back to the client). The fork's own retry covers calendar changes only,
    not event-level conflicts, as at 0.16 (M3). Cost if wrong: a write conflict reaches the
    client, which retries.
49. **A sharee's PUT on a key account isn't split into per-user preferences.** That is the
    0.16 rule: a key account's preferences stay empty (JMAP-only). Visible effects, from the
    review's M4: the owner sees a sharee's alarms, and the ETag and Schedule-Tag move on a
    sharee's alarm-only change. Cost if wrong: alarm sharing differs from an ordinary
    account's.
50. **A key-account UID conflict answers 1.0's 403** (own-items rights), not 0.16's 412.
    That fails closed. Tests that asserted 412 have ledger rows.
51. **Free-busy for key-account grantees stays withheld.** At 1.0.1 upstream selects a
    grantee's free-busy calendars with `match_any = true` (`dav/src/calendar/freebusy.rs:199-204`),
    so an ordinary grantee with `ReadItems` now gets busy periods, while a key-account grantee
    still gets `Withheld` (the review's I1). This is a recorded divergence: the sharing spec's
    free-busy section says so, and the differential test asserts it instead of equality.
    Serving busy periods from the unsealed view during the grantee's own keyed session would
    restore parity, but needs a sharing-spec revision, so it's left for Jay. Cost if wrong:
    key-account sharees see no busy periods where ordinary sharees do.

Recorded limit (ruling 16, the review's M5): a calendar container sealed at MKCOL binds the
calendar's id only from its next changing PROPPATCH. An unchanged PROPPATCH doesn't rebind
it, and a sharing rollback restores the unbound form. This affects integrity only.

## Task 5

### Part 5a: `email`, `spam-filter` and `smtp`

Part 5a resolved the fourteen conflicts of `email`, `spam-filter` and `smtp` (cells in the
conflict ledger), ported the fork's sealed-mail module onto 1.0's metadata rows, and
re-read the hooks that merged clean (`smtp/src/core/mod.rs`, `inbound/{data,rcpt,spam,
session}.rs`, `inbound/milter/{message,mod}.rs`, `scripts/{exec,mod}.rs`,
`spam-filter/src/{lib,analysis/mod,analysis/domain}.rs`). `cargo check -p email
-p spam-filter -p smtp` passes with and without `test_mode`, with test targets, and with
`enterprise` except one restored smtp test module (below).

#### The stub at 1.0

1.0 stores a message's metadata as a `MetadataRow` (`email/src/message/metadata/row.rs`):
an rkyv structure, a header section and a trailer. 0.16's stub put its bundle in
`raw_headers`; 1.0's row has no such field, so the stub is now a row of its own:

- **Header section:** `0x01 || envelope || seal_padded(DEK, aad_meta, full row)`, where the
  full row is upstream's `NewMetadata::encode()` output for the message (structure and
  header section, as an ordinary message's row). The format byte at its head is the one
  discriminator (`is_stub_headers`, `is_stub(&MetadataRow)`), as at 0.16. The associated
  data is unchanged: `za/v1|mail-meta|<account>|<sealed blob hash>`.
- **Structure (`stub_structure`):** the sealed blob's hash and one binary root part at
  offsets `0`, `bundle length`, `bundle length + sealed length`; nothing else. So
  upstream's size readers see stored sizes (`blob_size()` is the sealed blob's, the size
  `index_verbatim` gives the blob link), and upstream's row invariant (the root
  body starts where the header section ends) holds. A stub's structure has no header, so
  `thread_subject()` resolves to the empty name without reading the header section.
- **Opening (`open_stub`, `za_open_stub_row`):** the bundle is decrypted, the full row's
  structure is decoded with rkyv's checked access before a `MetadataRow` is built (a `pk`
  envelope can be sealed by anyone holding the public key, and a row is otherwise read
  unchecked), its header section must decode, and its blob hash must equal the stub's.
  Errors are `SealError`s mapped to the fixed unreadable error, so no decrypted byte
  reaches an error (upstream's archive errors quote the bytes).
- **`MessageData`:** a key account's message keeps upstream's visible fields (mailboxes,
  keywords, size, arrival time, thread), but its `sent_at` offset is 0 (the Date header
  stays sealed) and neither attachment keyword is set (spec 5.2 kept the attachment bit
  sealed). Its sort keys are `MessageSortKeys::default()`.
- **The view API for readers** (`za/view.rs`): `za_metadata_view(stored: MetadataRow, keys,
  account_id, document_id) -> ZaMetadataRow { row, opened }` replaces 0.16's `Cow` view;
  `opened` is what `za_open_message_blob` takes. `za_open_stub_row` replaces
  `za_open_stub_archive`. A key account's reader must read the row, not
  `MetadataStructure`: a stub's structure names only the sealed blob. `za_is_sealed_source`
  also counts 1.0's `BlobClass::Embedded` of `Collection::Email`.

#### Route map (`email` and `smtp`)

| Route | Entry | Sealing or refusal boundary |
|---|---|---|
| SMTP local delivery | `email/src/message/delivery.rs:203` (`email_ingest`, `IngestSource::Smtp`) | `email_ingest_into`: `za_ingest_wrap` with the keyless delivery token wraps to the served public key (`za_served_public_key`, read fresh); without one, `StoreEvent::UnexpectedError`, which `delivery.rs` maps to a temporary failure; `za_stage_sealed` holds the sealed blob, never the queue's; `za_index_stub` writes the stub. An ordinary account asserts vault absence after `with_account_id`. |
| Sieve delivery | `delivery.rs:223` → `sieve/ingest.rs` (`sieve_script_ingest`), each filed message through `email_ingest` (`:831`, `Smtp`) | As delivery. The user script's plugins get `za_redact`; a key account's runtime-error reason is redacted; its ingest events carry `MessageId = [redacted]`. |
| Sieve-generated messages (vacation, redirect, notify) | `sieve/ingest.rs` `send_message`; trusted scripts `smtp/src/scripts/event_loop.rs` `queue_sieve_message` | The SMTP queue (plaintext in transit, allowed); delivery to a local key account seals as above. The trusted script's events and plugin context are redacted for a key account's transaction (R57, R59). |
| SMTP DATA (any recipient) | `smtp/src/inbound/data.rs` `queue_message` | No autolearn sample for a key recipient or sender; recipients added after RCPT are classified at the end of DATA; the spam-filter input, milter client and scripts carry the redaction flag; raw reads are masked by `session.rs` (issue #20, R41). The queue holds plaintext (allowed). |
| IMAP APPEND | `imap/src/op/append.rs:224` → `IngestGroup::ingest` → `email_ingest_into` (`Imap`); `IngestGroup::flush` → `email_ingest_finish` | The wrap is the session's keys (`mk`), or the keyless refusal (`za_keyless_refusal`) before any batch operation, so a group keeps its batch as it was. No group thread, threading key, recheck or training for a key account. Each message's operations sit in one commit chunk with its own fence. `email_ingest_finish` reads nothing (content-free `PendingEmail`). |
| JMAP Email/import | `jmap/src/email/grouped.rs:40` → `IngestGroup` (`Jmap`) | As APPEND. |
| JMAP Email/set create | `jmap/src/email/set.rs:1062` → `email_ingest` (`Jmap`) | As APPEND; a key account's message with object metadata is refused before the batch (`za_metadata_refusal`, a 550 rejection). |
| Restore | `services/src/task_manager/tasks/restore_item.rs:57` → `email_ingest` (`Restore`) | Refused for a key account before the batch (550, spec 6.7); an ordinary account is fenced. |
| Cross-account copy | `email/src/message/copy.rs` `copy_message`, from IMAP COPY/MOVE (`imap/src/op/copy_move.rs:540`) and JMAP Email/copy (`jmap/src/email/copy.rs:343`) | Refused at use time when the source row's account (`CopySource::account_id`) or the target is a key account (`ZA_TRACE_MAIL_COPY`, the keyless refusal), before the row is decoded (`CopySources::take` has read its stored bytes, the sealed stub for a key account); the ordinary target asserts vault absence in the copy's one chunk. |
| Training samples | `email/src/message/ingest.rs` `add_account_spam_samples` (IMAP STORE, COPY and MOVE, Email/set, Email/copy) and `add_spam_sample` inside `email_ingest_into` | A key account is skipped (fresh `za_is_key_account`); `email_ingest_into` sets no sample for one. An ordinary account's samples assert vault absence in every commit chunk (R55: the fence after `with_account_id` and again after every `commit_point` that splits). |

No single-message guard remains around the grouped path: every guard sits in
`email_ingest_into`, which both `email_ingest` and `IngestGroup::ingest` call, and the
fence lives in the message's own chunk, which `write_batch` commits as one transaction.

### Part 5b: `imap` and `pop3`

Part 5b resolved the ten conflicts of `imap` and `pop3` (cells in the conflict ledger),
deleted the two modify/delete files (`imap/src/core/message.rs`, `imap/src/op/fetch.rs`)
with their hooks ported, wrote the body of `imap/src/core/za.rs` that part 3b declared, and
re-read the hooks that merged clean (`imap/src/core/{client,mod,session}.rs`,
`imap/src/op/{authenticate,create,delete,rename,subscribe}.rs`,
`pop3/src/{client,lib,session}.rs`, `pop3/src/op/authenticate.rs`). `cargo check -p imap
-p pop3 -p managesieve` passes with no warnings, also with `--tests` (none of the three has
`test_mode`; `managesieve`'s restored module after ruling 54, below). `cargo test -p imap -p pop3 --lib`: 8 and 2 passed.

#### R34 at 1.0 (finding 5)

0.16 ran R34's route check (`za_assert_mail_route`) inside `synchronize_messages`, before
anything was read. 1.0's `sync_view` takes a `MessageStoreCache` its callers have already
read, and several commands read a mailbox's cache without calling `check_mailbox_acl`. So
the check now sits before the cache read at every site that reads a selected or named
mailbox's message cache:

- `fetch` (`op/fetch/mod.rs`, also when IDLE passes its cache in), `store`, `search`,
  `thread`, UIDBATCHES, `handle_expunge`, `copy_move` (the source), SELECT (`select.rs`,
  which 1.0 no longer routes through `check_mailbox_acl`), STATUS (`status.rs`), LIST's
  RETURN (STATUS) (`list.rs`, which skips the item) and `selected_access`;
- `check_mailbox_acl_as`, which `check_mailbox_acl`, STATUS and `selected_access` call:
  the check moved there from `check_mailbox_acl`, which APPEND, CREATE's parent check,
  EXPUNGE, CLOSE and COPY/MOVE call;
- `sync_view` itself, as the net for a caller that does not check first.

Three paths skip instead of refusing, as 0.16 did (`write_changes`' "the calling command
completes"):
- `write_changes` (`idle.rs`; IDLE, NOOP, CHECK and every command's trailing update)
  treats a foreign key account's selected mailbox as unselected, so its cache is neither
  read nor synced, and the mailbox-list update still runs;
- the pre-command access check (`client.rs`, `assert_selected_readable`) returns before
  `selected_access` for a foreign key account, so NOOP, CHECK and IDLE complete and every
  other command refuses at its own check;
- CLOSE deselects without expunging and without reading the cache.

The listing paths keep 0.16's rule: `SessionData::new` and `synchronize_mailboxes_as` skip
a foreign key account before `account_view` reads its cache, and `za_unlist_key_accounts`
drops one the token still grants. The session's own account is listed and read; a keyless
key-account session does not exist (`za_mail_login`), except one opened before
provisioning (R33, R48).

#### Route map (`imap` and `pop3`)

| Route | Entry | Unsealing or refusal boundary |
|---|---|---|
| IMAP FETCH (FLAGS, UID, INTERNALDATE, RFC822.SIZE, MODSEQ, EMAILID/THREADID) | `op/fetch/mod.rs` `fetch` | R34 before the cache read. `FetchNeeds.structure` is false: no keys taken, nothing opened; the values come from the cache (`INTERNALDATE` is the arrival time, `RFC822.SIZE` the stored size). A key account's FLAGS carry no `$HasAttachment` or `$HasNoAttachment` (spec 5.2; see below). |
| IMAP FETCH ENVELOPE, BODY, BODYSTRUCTURE, PREVIEW, BINARY.SIZE | same | `za_mail_keys` once per command (a keyless read of a key account: `NO [NOPERM]`, `ZA_MAIL_KEYLESS`); a key account's metadata read as `MetadataForm::Row`, opened by `za_open_metadata_row` (a stub's structure alone names only the sealed blob). The envelope's Date and every part come from the opened row. An unreadable stub skips the message, and the command ends `NO [SERVERBUG]` `ZA_MAIL_UNREADABLE` after the served ones. |
| IMAP FETCH RFC822, RFC822.HEADER, RFC822.TEXT, BODY[...] (header, header fields, MIME, text, part, nested part), BINARY[...] | same, then `section.rs`, `source.rs`, `structure.rs` | As above, and `fetch_blob` then `za_open_message_blob` opens the sealed blob whole into a `Zeroizing` buffer (`ZaFetchedBlob`, held with the fetched bytes' memory reservation). Every section, header, part and decoded-source reader reads the opened row and bytes; no reader reads the blob store directly. An unreadable blob skips the message as above. A non-peek BODY sets `\Seen` as for any account. |
| IMAP SEARCH, ESEARCH, SORT | `op/search.rs` `search` → `query` | R34 before the cache read; then a filter or sort key that needs content (`za_filter_needs_content`, `za_sort_needs_content`) is refused for a key account as a whole, `NO [CANNOT]` `ZA_MAIL_SEARCH_REFUSED`, before any filter is built. The other keys read the cache (flags, sizes, arrival dates, modseq, ids). |
| IMAP THREAD | `op/thread.rs` | R34 before the cache read; the filter refusal in `query`; threads come from the cache's thread ids (a key account's messages each have their own thread). |
| IMAP COPY, MOVE (same account) | `op/copy_move.rs` | R34 before the source cache read; the copy changes mailbox memberships only (no content read); training through `add_account_spam_samples`, which skips a key account. |
| IMAP COPY, MOVE (across accounts) | `op/copy_move.rs` → `email::copy_message` | R34 on the source and on the destination, each before that account's cache read (the destination's since fix round 1, F3; `check_mailbox_acl_as` checks it again); `za_assert_mail_copy` refuses before the copy loop when either account is a key account (`NO [NOPERM]`); `copy_message` refuses again at use time (5a). An ordinary pair's training samples carry R55's fence in every chunk (`add_account_spam_samples`). |
| IMAP APPEND | `op/append.rs` → `IngestGroup` (5a) | R34 through `check_mailbox_acl` → `check_mailbox_acl_as`; sealing as in part 5a's map. |
| IMAP STORE | `op/store.rs` | R34 before the cache read; keywords only; training skips a key account; the `ImapEvent::Store` details are `[redacted]` in a key session. |
| IMAP IDLE, NOOP, CHECK | `op/idle.rs` `write_changes` | A foreign key account's selected mailbox gets no update and its cache is not read. Otherwise the update is a FETCH of FLAGS and UID only: no keys, no content pushed. |
| IMAP EXPUNGE, UID EXPUNGE, CLOSE | `op/expunge.rs`, `op/close.rs` | R34 before the cache read (EXPUNGE refused; CLOSE deselects without expunging); `expunge` keeps 0.16's no-op for a foreign key account. |
| IMAP SELECT, EXAMINE, STATUS, LIST RETURN (STATUS), UIDBATCHES | `op/select.rs`, `op/status.rs`, `op/list.rs`, `op/uidbatches.rs` | R34 before the cache read (LIST skips the item); counts and UIDs only. |
| IMAP GETACL, MYRIGHTS, SETACL, DELETEACL | `op/acl.rs` | R34 in `get_acl_mailbox`; SETACL refuses an added or widened grant on a key account's mailbox (ruling I3). |
| IMAP traces | `core/{client,session}.rs`, `op/fetch/mod.rs`, `op/store.rs` | Raw input and output `[redacted]` before authentication and in a key session; BAD errors redacted in both; `ImapEvent::Fetch` and `Store` details `[redacted]` in a key session (issue #21). |
| POP3 RETR, TOP | `pop3/src/op/fetch.rs` | `za_mail_keys`, then `za_metadata_view` over the stored `MetadataRow` (a stub opened into the full row: headers, header length and body offset come from it); an opened stub's blob opened whole by `za_open_message_blob` under the fetched bytes' reservation. A keyless read is refused; an unreadable stub or blob answers `-ERR` `ZA_MAIL_UNREADABLE`. |
| POP3 LIST, UIDL, STAT, DELE | `pop3/src/op/{list,delete}.rs` | Sizes and ids from the cache; no content read. |
| POP3 traces | `pop3/src/{client,session}.rs` | Raw input and output `[redacted]` before authentication and in a key session; parse errors marked BAD there and redacted. |

#### Filter and sort classification

1.0's `imap_proto::protocol::search::Filter` and `Sort` have the same variants as 0.16's
(`EmailId` and `ThreadId` now hold a `CompactString`). Both matches stay exhaustive, with no
wildcard.

- **Content (refused for a key account):** `BCC`, `BODY`, `CC`, `FROM`, `HEADER`,
  `SENTBEFORE`, `SENTON`, `SENTSINCE`, `SUBJECT`, `TEXT`, `TO`, as at 0.16; and, new at 1.0,
  `KEYWORD` and `UNKEYWORD` of `$HasAttachment` or `$HasNoAttachment`. 1.0 sets those two
  keywords from the parsed message (`email/src/message/index/metadata.rs:76`); a key
  account's message carries neither (spec 5.2, part 5a), only what its client stored with
  STORE, so a search on them would silently answer only the client's flags. JMAP's
  `hasAttachment` filter is refused for key accounts likewise (`tests/src/za/mail/search.rs`).
  At 0.16 the server set neither keyword for any account, and `KEYWORD $HasAttachment`
  matched client flags for both. Ruling 55 keeps the refusal.
- **Visible (served from the cache):** every other key, including `KEYWORD` and
  `UNKEYWORD` of any other flag, `BEFORE`, `ON`, `SINCE`, `OLDER` and `YOUNGER` (arrival
  time), `LARGER` and `SMALLER` (stored size), `MODSEQ`, `EMAILID`, `THREADID`, the
  sequence and UID keys and the operators.
- **Sort:** `ARRIVAL` and `SIZE` are visible; `CC`, `DATE` (the sent date, which 1.0 reads
  from `MessageData.sent_at`, 0 for a key account), `FROM`, `DISPLAYFROM`, `SUBJECT`, `TO`
  and `DISPLAYTO` need content.

The unit tables `op::search::tests::{filter_table, sort_table}` pin these verdicts.

#### Decisions recorded for the primary

- **The attachment keywords in FLAGS.** Part 5a stores no attachment keyword for a key
  account. 5b does not derive them from the opened row in FETCH FLAGS: FLAGS is served
  from the cache without keys (R33), and deriving them would make every FLAGS fetch open
  every stub, and would answer differently with and without a content attribute in the same
  FETCH. A key account's FLAGS therefore never show `$HasAttachment` or
  `$HasNoAttachment`, as at 0.16, where no account's did; SEARCH on them is refused (above).
  BODYSTRUCTURE and every attachment-derived structure answer come from the opened row.
  Ruling 55 confirms both this and the SEARCH refusal: the two keywords are new in 1.0 and
  derived from content, 0.16 set neither, and the refusal matches JMAP's `hasAttachment`
  refusal (mail spec 5.2). `za::mail::reads`' key-versus-plain FETCH comparison expects
  exactly that difference (expectation ledger).
- **A borrowed view for batch readers.** 1.0's FETCH holds each row by reference inside a
  `DocumentBatch`, and `MetadataRow` is neither `Clone` nor rebuildable from its parts. Part
  5b added `ZaMailView::za_open_metadata_row(&MetadataRow, ..) -> Option<MetadataRow>` to
  `email/src/message/za/view.rs` (Task 5's file) and made `za_metadata_view` call it, so both
  share one implementation: the same stub check, private-key unwrap and checked decode.
- **R55 in the copy loop.** 0.16's fence sat before each `commit_point()` of IMAP's
  cross-account training loop. 1.0's loop collects `SpamSamples` and commits them through
  `add_account_spam_samples`, where part 5a fences every chunk, so the CLAUDE.md silent
  dependency now points at `email/src/message/ingest.rs` for IMAP; JMAP's site is 5c's.
- **New 1.0 commands without a route check of their own:** METADATA (RFC 5464) and
  UIDBATCHES' ranges read only listed mailboxes after a sync, which unlists a foreign key
  account first; UIDBATCHES checks before its cache read anyway. METADATA reads mailbox
  annotations, not message content.

#### Deferred, by owner

**Done after ruling 54**
- `managesieve/src/restored_tests.rs` (Task 2's restored module): 1.0's `Token::Argument`
  holds a `SmallVec<[u8; 22]>`, so its 13 `b"...".to_vec()` arguments are now
  `b"...".as_slice().into()`, a call-shape change with no assertion change.
  `cargo check -p managesieve --tests` passes and `cargo test -p managesieve --lib` passes
  (1 test).

**Task 6**
- Nothing from `imap/src/op/search.rs`: the refusals need no index, the visible keys read
  the cache, and `query_emails` receives only set filters and cache comparators for a key
  account. What the search index holds for a key account's message (arrival time and size,
  part 5a's stub) is the index worker's, as part 5a's deferral says.

**Part 5c**
- The za IMAP and POP3 integration tests (`tests/src/za/mail/{login,reads,gates,search,
  traces}.rs`, `tests/src/imap/za_variants.rs`) are not compiled until Task 7; they need no
  API from 5b beyond 0.16's protocol behaviour, which 5b keeps.

### Part 5c: JMAP and the HTTP upload gate

Part 5c resolved the fifteen Task 5 conflicts in `jmap` (cells in the conflict ledger),
ported their hooks onto 1.0's readers, classified every JMAP method for key accounts, and
re-read the hooks that merged clean (`jmap/src/api/request.rs`, `changes/query.rs`,
`sieve/set.rs`, `vacation/set.rs`, `registry/mapping/action.rs`, `email/za.rs`,
`http/src/request.rs`). Two files outside the conflict list changed:
- `jmap/src/file/writer.rs`: 1.0 moved every FileNode blob read (create, update and copy)
  into `FileNodeWriter::resolve_blob`, so the three `za_refuse_raw_source` hooks of 0.16's
  `file/{copy,set}.rs` (inventory `file/copy.rs` row 2, `file/set.rs` rows 2 and 3) are one
  call there;
- `email/src/message/za/view.rs` (Task 5's): `za_has_attachment`, below.

`jmap` and `http` compile at Task 6 (ruling 21). The changed jmap files are rustfmt-clean
and were checked against v1.0.1's signatures by reading. `cargo check -p email --tests
--features test_mode` passes with no warnings, and `cargo test -p email --lib --features
email/test_mode` passes 8 tests (one new: `the_opened_row_answers_has_attachment_as_upstream`).

#### The readers at 1.0

- **Batch readers** (`Email/get`, `SearchSnippet/get`). 1.0 reads metadata through
  `read_metadata_batch` into a `DocumentBatch<MetadataValue>`. A key account's stub is read
  as `MetadataForm::Row` (its structure alone names only the sealed blob) and opened by
  reference with `za_open_metadata_row`. The opened row replaces the stored value for every
  reader below it: render, cost estimate, `raw_message`. The sealed blob is opened whole
  with `za_open_message_blob` into a `Zeroizing` buffer, held with the stored bytes' memory
  reservation. An unreadable stub or blob is not found (`za_readable`). An ordinary
  account's path is upstream's: its form, its blob and no extra read.
- **`Email/get`** takes the keys once (`za_mail_keys`). Only `id`, `threadId`, `blobId`,
  `mailboxIds`, `keywords`, `size` and `receivedAt` are served without opening the stub;
  `blobId` comes from the stub's structure, whose hash is the sealed blob's and equals the
  opened row's (5a binds them). 1.0 serves `hasAttachment` from the cache's keyword bit,
  which a key account's message never has (ruling 55). For a key account it reads the
  opened row instead: `za_has_attachment` applies upstream's ingest rule
  (`MessageMetadata::build`'s `has_attachments`) to the stored view, so the property forces
  the metadata read. `sentAt` and every header property come from the opened row's
  envelope, so 5a's zero `sent_at` offset is never read here.
- **Single readers** (`/jmap/download` and every `blob_download` caller, EmailSubmission).
  These go through `za_metadata_view` over the stored `MetadataRow` (`ZaMetadataRow
  { row, opened }`), then `za_open_message_blob(opened, ..)`.
- **`decode_section`** now mirrors `fetch_blob_section`: the blob store's range read of the
  section's outermost range (`SectionDecode::fetch_range`, cut at the end from offset 0,
  empty out of range otherwise), then upstream's own `SectionDecode::decode_fetched`. That
  decodes 1.0's nested sections (containers, then the part) exactly as a stored read does.
  Section offsets are the ones `Email/get` rendered from the opened row, in the plaintext
  blob's coordinates, which is what the opened blob holds.
- **A whole message** of a key account answers as upstream's `BlobContents::Message`:
  the opened row's header section, then `opened[blob_body_offset..]`, when the two differ;
  otherwise the opened bytes. Both are wrapped in an account `FetchedBlob` because they
  leave in the HTTP body.

#### Route map (`jmap` and `http`)

| Route | Entry | Sealing, unsealing or refusal boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `Email/get` (visible properties) | `jmap/src/email/get.rs` `email_get` | Dispatcher `za_assert_mail_keys`. `needs_content` false: the stored structure or the cache, nothing opened. |
| `Email/get` (headers, body, `hasAttachment`, `sentAt`, `preview`, parts, `bodyValues`) | same | Keys once (`:210`). Row form, `za_open_metadata_row` (`:305`), `za_open_message_blob` (`:365`) when the body is read. `hasAttachment` from `za_has_attachment` (`:334`). An unreadable message is `notFound`. |
| `Email/query`, `Email/queryChanges` | `email/query.rs:118`, `changes/query.rs:71` | Dispatcher (or `changes`) `za_assert_mail_route`, then `za_refuse_content_query`: a filter or sort needing content is refused before the cache, the object-metadata evaluation or the search store are read (classification below). |
| `SearchSnippet/get` | `email/snippet.rs:61` | Dispatcher keys; `za_refuse_snippet_terms` refuses any `text`, `subject` or `body` condition. Defence in depth: with terms, keys once (`:167`), row form, stub and blob opened (`:236`, `:285`); unreachable for a key account. |
| `Email/set` create | `email/set.rs:166` → `GroupedIngest` → `email_ingest_into` | Keys asserted at the create (`za_assert_mail_keys`); sealing in `email_ingest_into` (5a). An attachment `blobId` that is another account's sealed source is refused per create (`:707`, R31, `forbidden` `ZA_MAIL_KEYLESS`); its own sealed upload is read through `blob_download`, opened with the keys. |
| `Email/set` update, destroy | `email/set.rs` | Dispatcher route only; keywords and mailboxes. Training goes through `add_account_spam_samples` (5a: a key account is skipped, every chunk fenced). Object-metadata patches: Task 6. |
| `Email/import` | `email/import.rs:111` → `GroupedIngest` | Dispatcher keys; every `blobId` that is another account's sealed source refuses the call before the first import (R31). The blob is read by `blob_download_with_extra` (opened for the account's own sealed upload or message). The key branch of `email_ingest_into` ignores the reused `blob_hash` and seals a new blob (5a). No thread recheck for a key account (5a: `thread_recheck: None`). |
| `Email/copy` | `email/copy.rs:84` → `email::copy_message` | Dispatcher keys on both accounts; `za_assert_mail_copy` refuses when either is a key account, before any read; `copy_message` refuses again at use time (5a). R55: 1.0's loop collects `SpamSamples` and commits them through `add_account_spam_samples`, which fences every chunk (5a), so JMAP, like IMAP, has no fence of its own before a `commit_point()`. |
| `Email/parse` | `email/parse.rs` | Dispatcher keys; `blob_download_with_extra` opens a sealed source with the request's keys. |
| `Blob/get` | `blob/get.rs` → `blob_download` | Dispatcher keys; opened bytes for a sealed source. |
| `Blob/upload` | `blob/upload.rs:61` | Keys once (refused without them); every literal source that is a sealed source refuses the call before the first write (`:77`), and each source is checked again at its turn (`:116`, including a reference to this call's upload). A key account's upload is sealed (`za_put_sealed_upload`, `:235`). |
| `Blob/copy` | `blob/copy.rs:51` | Refused when either account is a key account, and for any sealed-source id, before the first link. |
| `Blob/lookup` | dispatcher | `za_assert_mail_route`; reads references, no content. |
| `/jmap/download` | `http/src/request.rs:221` → `blob_contents` (`blob/download.rs:175`) | A `Linked` Email blob of a key account is not found for any token but its owner's (`za_foreign_key_account` in `has_access_blob`), before that account's message cache is read (fix round 1, F9, ruling 60). A sealed source (`za_is_sealed_source`: a key account's `Reserved` upload, or `Linked`/`Embedded` Email blob) is answered only by `za_download_message` or `za_download_upload`, with `za_mail_keys` (keyless: R30's 403). Calendar data of a key account is denied in `has_access_blob` for `Linked` and, new at 1.0, `Embedded` blobs (`za_calendar_data`). |
| `/jmap/upload` | `http/src/request.rs:264` → `blob_upload` (`blob/upload.rs:262`) | The gate refuses a key account without its keys before the body is read (403); `blob_upload` asserts the keys again and seals. The body is read untraced for a key account's token. Re-read against 1.0's `blob_upload`: unchanged. |
| `EmailSubmission/set` | `submission/set.rs:630` | Dispatcher keys; `za_metadata_view`, then `za_open_message_blob` (`:701`); an unreadable message fails its create (`ZA_MAIL_UNREADABLE`). The recipients come from the opened row, the BCC strip runs on the opened bytes, and the plaintext goes only to the SMTP queue. |
| `Mailbox/get`, `/query`, `/changes`, `/queryChanges` | dispatcher, `changes/get.rs:248` | `za_assert_mail_route`. Object metadata: Task 6. |
| `Mailbox/set` | `mailbox/set.rs:1021` | Route; a grant added to or widened on a key account's mailbox is refused before upstream's `acl_validate` (ruling I3). |
| `Thread/get`, `Email/changes`, `Thread/changes`, `EmailSubmission/get`, `/query`, `/changes`, `/queryChanges` | dispatcher, `changes/get.rs:248` | `za_assert_mail_route`; cache and logs only. |
| `FileNode/set` create, update; `FileNode/copy` | `file/writer.rs:704` (`resolve_blob`) | `za_refuse_raw_source` before `has_access_blob` and any read of the blob. |
| `SieveScript/set` | `sieve/set.rs:720` | A key account's message or section never becomes a script (R32); an upload is read through `blob_download`, opened with the keys. |
| `SieveScript/validate`, `ContactCard/parse` | `blob_download` | A sealed upload is opened with the token's keys or refused. |
| `x:SpamTrainingSample/set` | `registry/mapping/spam_sample.rs:87` | `za_refuse_raw_source` before the download (R32). |
| `VacationResponse/set` | `vacation/set.rs` | The compile error names its position only (every account). |
| Push (EventSource, WebSocket, PushSubscription) | `services` | State changes only (`StateChange` with data types); no content. The services side is Task 6's. |

#### Dispatcher gate (`jmap/src/api/request.rs`)

`RequestMethod` and its six sub-enums, `MethodObject`, have the same variants at 1.0.1 as
at 0.16.27, and part 4b ported every 0.16 gate onto its arm. So no new arm was added. Two
functions newly answer at 1.0 inside existing arms: `ParticipantIdentity/changes` and
`Quota/changes` (0.16 answered `cannotCalculateChanges`). Several arms also changed
behaviour. Classes:
- **keys:** `za_assert_mail_keys`, the token must hold the target account's keys;
- **route:** `za_assert_mail_route`, a key account's mail is reached only by its own
  sessions;
- **refused:** `za_assert_calendar_allowed`, `accountNotSupportedByMethod` for a key
  account;
- **ordinary:** upstream's checks only.

| Method | Class | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `Email/get` | keys | |
| `Email/query`, `Email/queryChanges` | route | plus `za_refuse_content_query` |
| `Email/set` | route | keys at the create's site |
| `Email/copy` | keys (both accounts) | plus `za_assert_mail_copy` |
| `Email/import`, `Email/parse` | keys | |
| `Email/changes` | route | in `changes` |
| `Mailbox/get`, `/query`, `/set`, `/changes`, `/queryChanges` | route | I3 in `/set` |
| `Thread/get`, `Thread/changes` | route | |
| `EmailSubmission/get`, `/query`, `/changes`, `/queryChanges` | route | |
| `EmailSubmission/set` | keys | |
| `SearchSnippet/get` | keys | plus term refusal |
| `Blob/get`, `Blob/upload` | keys | |
| `Blob/copy` | keys (target) | `blob_copy` refuses either key account |
| `Blob/lookup` | route | |
| `Identity/get`, `/set`, `/changes` | ordinary | no content |
| `PushSubscription/get`, `/set` | ordinary | the token's own; payloads are state only |
| `SieveScript/get`, `/query`, `/set`, `/validate` | ordinary | R32 in `/set`; uploads opened through `blob_download` |
| `VacationResponse/get`, `/set` | ordinary | compile reason redacted |
| `Principal/get`, `/query`, `/queryChanges` | ordinary | capability filtering is Task 6's (`principal/get.rs`) |
| `Principal/getAvailability` | ordinary | 4b's handoff. At 1.0 the arm resolves `accountId`; the rule is per principal in `principal/availability.rs` (Task 6's file, inventory row 1): a key account contributes no busy periods, and a key account's own request answers an empty list (the existing `za_variants::calendars` and `za::gating` expectations) rather than a refusal. Ruling 51 covers grantees. |
| `Quota/get`, `/query`, `/queryChanges` | ordinary | |
| `Quota/changes` | ordinary | new at 1.0: member check, then `cannotCalculateChanges` |
| `AddressBook/*`, `ContactCard/*` (get, query, set, changes, queryChanges, copy, parse) | ordinary | `ContactCard/parse` opens a sealed upload or refuses |
| `FileNode/*` (get, query, set, changes, queryChanges, copy) | ordinary | raw-source refusal in `resolve_blob` |
| `Calendar/*`, `CalendarEvent/*` (including copy, parse, queryChanges), `CalendarEventNotification/*` | refused | 1.0 widened the notification arms to `assert_has_access(CalendarEvent)`; the refusal still names the target account |

| `ParticipantIdentity/changes` | refused | new at 1.0; added in `changes/get.rs:223`. As for `/get` and `/set`, it names the request's account, while upstream reads the token's own identities. |
| `ShareNotification/get`, `/query`, `/set`, `/changes`, `/queryChanges` | ordinary | 4b's handoff. The recipient's own records (`assert_is_member`): object ids, rights and the actor. The writer leaves `name` empty (`common/src/storage/index/build.rs`), so no content. A key account's mailbox gains no grant (I3), so it emits no grant notification. |
| `x:*` registry methods | ordinary | registry refusals (Task 3) |
| `Core/echo` | ordinary | |

**Object metadata, for Task 6.** The object-metadata surface (`jmap/src/api/metadata/`) is
not a method of its own. It is properties, patches and filters inside existing methods, and
it is Task 6's. For key accounts:
- 5a refuses metadata at ingest (`za_metadata_refusal`);
- 5c refuses `Email/query`'s metadata filters (below);
- these stay unported: `MetadataGet` in `Email/get` and `Mailbox/get`, `MetadataWriter`
  patches in `Email/set` update, `Email/copy` and `Mailbox/set`, `MetadataPreload`, and
  metadata `/changes` (`MetadataChanges` in `changes/get.rs`).

#### Filter and sort classification

1.0's `EmailFilter` and `EmailComparator` have the same variants as 0.16's, so
`filter_needs_content` and `sort_needs_content` keep their verdicts and stay exhaustive:
- content: the filters `text`, `from`, `to`, `cc`, `bcc`, `subject`, `body`, `header`,
  `hasAttachment`, `sentBefore` and `sentAfter`, and the sorts `from`, `to`, `cc`,
  `subject` and `sentAt`; since fix round 1 (F2, ruling 55), also every keyword filter
  (`hasKeyword`, `notKeyword` and the three thread forms) and keyword sort (`hasKeyword`
  and the two thread forms) naming `$hasattachment` or `$hasnoattachment` in any case;
- visible: everything else, including `before` and `after` (the arrival time), the sizes,
  the other keywords, mailboxes, threads and ids.

New at 1.0, `Email/query`'s filter type is `EmailQueryFilter { Email(EmailFilter),
Metadata(MetadataFilter) }`. The new exhaustive match is
`query_filter_needs_content(&EmailQueryFilter)` (`jmap/src/email/za.rs`). It refuses every
`Metadata` condition (`metadataExists`, `metadataTextContains`, `metadataTextEquals` and
their private forms) for a key account. Its message holds no object metadata (5a refuses
it at ingest), so the filter could only answer silently, as the attachment keywords would
(ruling 55). The refusal's detail names the filter only (`MetadataFilter::as_str`), never
its path or value. `SearchSnippet/get`'s filter is still `Filter<EmailFilter>`.

Unit tests: `email::za::tests::{filter_table, sort_table, query_filter_table,
first_refused_walks_the_filter_tree, ...}`. They run at Task 6's first compile.

  - a key account's `hasAttachment` from the opened row (true and false);
  - the metadata-filter refusal;
  - `ParticipantIdentity/changes` refused for a key account;
  - a nested section (a part inside a base64 `message/rfc822`) downloaded from a key
    account's message;

  - a sealed source named by a FileNode create, update and copy;
  - `Email/get` of `blobId` alone not opening the stub (the stub's hash);

**Task 6**

- Search-index work behind `email/query.rs`. For a key account, 1.0's `QueryFilter`,

**Recorded, no owner action**
- `za_has_attachment` reads the stored view. A part beyond `MAX_PART_ENTRIES` is not stored,
  so a message whose only attachment lies there reads `false` where upstream's keyword says
  `true`.
- `x:SpamTrainingSample/set` with a key account's `accountId` and an ordinary blob still
  creates a sample under that account, as at 0.16. R52's provisioning refusal sees it.
- `ParticipantIdentity/changes` names the request's account in its refusal, as `/get` and
  `/set` do; upstream reads the token's own identities whatever account the request names.

### Part 5d: the mail integration tests

Part 5d added Task 5's four new cases and the `plant_plain_message` helper, and adapted the
za mail modules and the key-mode IMAP and JMAP variants to 1.0's APIs and part 5a's reader
API. The `tests` crate compiles first at Task 7: every changed file is rustfmt-clean
(`rustfmt --edition 2024`) and was checked against the 1.0 signatures by reading; none of
it has run. No test hook was added.

#### The new cases

| Case | Module (`tests/src/za/mail/`) | Registered after | Negative control |
|---|---|---|---|
| `grouped_ingest_provisioning_wins_before_flush`: an `IngestGroup` of two threaded messages classified as ordinary (keyless token); `setup-token` and setup complete before the flush, which fails its assertion (`is_assertion_failure`) and commits no message, metadata row (`metadata_rows`, the immutable class scanned directly) or sample; the group's staged plaintext stays under temporary links until the purge removes them; the keyed retry stores both sealed (`WRAP_MK`, own threads, the opened bytes equal) and no other row | `fence` | `reissue_repairs_an_aborted_issuance` | the same group for `plainmail24` flushes in the clear, each message threaded, the reply in its parent's pending thread |
| `grouped_rejection_discards_uncommitted_plain_metadata`: a group holds an ordinary-classified message; the account is provisioned; the next ingest with the same keyless token is refused (`JmapEvent::Forbidden`, `ZA_MAIL_KEYLESS`, not a rejection) and the group discards its batch, so the flush commits nothing; the keyed retry stores both messages sealed, and the account's metadata rows are exactly the retry's | `fence` | the case above | `plainmail25`: an unparsed message is a rejection; the group keeps its batch, the rejected message adds no row; its parsed retry adds one message and one row |
| `grouped_ingest_mixed_key_and_plain`: one group interleaving `plainmail23`'s and `keymail28`'s copies of a message and its reply (the plain message records the thread key first); one flush; each result names its own account and document; the key messages are sealed under `mk`, unthreaded, each its own thread, opened bytes equal; their opened row equals the plain account's stored row for the same bytes (header section, body offset, size, preview, parts, subject) but for the blob hash; no sample for the key account | `delivery` | `restore_is_refused` | the plain messages are plaintext, threaded, the reply joined to its parent's pending thread |
| `assigned_ids_preserve_sealed_read_offsets`: three extended fixtures (a base64 `message/rfc822` holding its own base64 attachment, and an outer binary attachment) and a message without an attachment, grouped into the key account (keyed) and the plain account; by the flush's assigned ids and UIDs: owner binding (`BlobClass::Linked` of the account, the stub refuses another account's associated data, `blobId` is the sealed hash); twelve `UID FETCH` attributes (nested sections included) equal the plain account's; `Email/get` (with `sentAt`, `hasAttachment`, `bodyStructure`) equal; the outer attachment, the nested message and, through `Email/parse` of the nested section, its own attachment downloaded byte for byte; another account's download and a keyless Bearer download of the key account's sections refused; `hasAttachment` false and `sentAt` equal for the attachment-less message; a corrupted stub and a corrupted blob fail their own `UID FETCH` (`NO [SERVERBUG]`) and `Email/get` (not found) while the middle message reads | `reads` | `rf_5_large_header_block` | the plain account's answers are the comparison |

#### Finding for the primary

`Server::za_has_property` (`common/src/auth/vault.rs:704`) iterates the property class
(`ValueKey::property`). At 1.0 `EmailField::Metadata` lives in the immutable class
(`store/src/write/mod.rs:1741`), so ruling R46's refusal in `za_assert_no_data`
(`http/src/api/vault.rs:914`) never finds a destroyed message's row. `fence.rs`'s R46 case
would fail on it (it expects the 409), and two Task 6 assertions in `fence.rs`
(`za_has_property(.., EmailField::Metadata)`) read the same wrong class. Owners: Task 3's
helper, Task 6's tests. Resolved after part 5d: the helper reads the class 1.0 stores a field in, so these
call sites need no change (see Task 3's "Follow-up: R46's class").

#### Assumptions Task 7 must confirm at runtime

1. A fixture-sized `IngestGroup` batch commits as one transaction: ingest adds no commit
   point, so `FORCE_COMMIT_SPLIT` cannot split it, and a failed assertion commits nothing.
2. `setup-token`'s emptiness check and recheck pass while a group holds the account's
   messages in memory and its staged blobs under temporary links.
3. Setup invalidates the account cache, so the next `email_ingest_into` reads a key
   account; the keyless refusal there is `JmapEvent::Forbidden` with `ZA_MAIL_KEYLESS`.
4. `AccessToken::from_id_maybe_invalid(..).with_session_keys(..)` ingests as a keyed
   session (only `za_keys_for` is read).
5. An unparsed message is rejected before any batch operation (`MessageIngest(Error)`).
6. The JMAP metadata refusal reads `metadataExists: <ZA_MAIL_SEARCH_REFUSED>`, and the
   ordinary account answers `Email/query` for `{"metadataExists": "za-search/key"}`.
7. Each refused IMAP `KEYWORD $HasAttachment` search is one `ImapEvent::Error`.
8. `ParticipantIdentity/changes` on a key account answers `accountNotSupportedByMethod`.
9. A key account's `Email/parse` of a nested section blob answers its attachments with
   section blob ids that download the decoded bytes; another account's download of them
   is not 200, and a keyless Bearer download is 403 with `ZA_MAIL_KEYLESS` (R30).
10. `UID FETCH` of a key account's nested sections answers what the ordinary account
    answers for the same bytes, the UID and sequence number aside.
11. `copy_message`'s refusal is still `JmapEvent::Forbidden` that `should_write_err`, with
    the unlimited tenant budget the test passes.
12. The temporary-link key layout (`hash || account || until`) is unchanged, so `fence`'s
    and `uploads`' link scans keep finding the staged blobs.
13. The `sentAt` of the attachment-less message renders a 2026-10-20 date for both accounts.
14. Harness items from Tasks 2 and 7 the modules use unchanged: `read_queued_messages`,
    `ImapConnection`, `Account` helpers, `HttpAuthCache::clear`, `create_passwordless_user_account`.

### Task 5 rulings

52. **Task 5 ran in dependency order,** as 5a (`email`, `spam-filter`, `smtp`), 5b (`imap`,
    `pop3`, `managesieve`), then 5c (the JMAP mail files, the dispatcher gate and the HTTP
    upload gate) and 5d (tests), the last two split by ruling 56. Its review ran on Fable at
    xhigh. Jay later asked for no further Fable reviews until the PR stage. From Task 6 on,
    task reviews run on Opus.
53. **The restored DKIM tests are gated off `enterprise`.** `queue::Message` gained the
    SEL-gated field `tenant_ids` and has no `Default` or constructor, and fork code never
    names SEL fields. So the seven tests run only in a build without `smtp/enterprise`, and
    Task 8 adds a CI step for them.
54. **`managesieve`'s restored tests are adapted** (13 call shapes, `.as_slice().into()`), as
    Task 2's restore rule allows.
55. **Key accounts get no `$HasAttachment` or `$HasNoAttachment` keyword,** and searching,
    filtering or sorting on either is refused in IMAP and JMAP, in any letter case. The
    refusal also covers the two thread-keyword sorts, since a key account's message is its
    own thread. Both keywords are new in 1.0 and derived from content. 0.16 set neither, and
    the refusal matches JMAP's `hasAttachment` refusal (mail spec 5.2). BODYSTRUCTURE still
    shows attachments. Cost if wrong: IMAP clients show no attachment marker from flags for
    key accounts.
56. **5c and 5d were split** for context size.
57. **`Email/query`'s object-metadata filters are refused for key accounts.** They could only
    answer silently wrong, and sealed values have no index.
58. **A mail test passes `DiskQuotaBudget::unlimited()`** for the enterprise-only tenant-budget
    argument. The tests crate always enables `email/enterprise`, and the type is AGPL.
59. **`IngestGroup` never records a key account's message under a thread key** (one fork
    call, `za_group_thread`). Key accounts have no threading, and a key message in a group
    would otherwise split an ordinary reply from its thread.
60. **A key account's message blob answers not-found to every token but the owner's,** with
    no cache read (`has_access_blob`). That is v1.0.1's and 0.16.27's answer without a
    grant, and gives no existence oracle. One answer changes: a pre-provisioning grantee's
    403 becomes 404, which has a ledger row.

Limits recorded in Task 5:
- `hasAttachment` is read from the stored view, so it can say `false` when a message's only
  attachment lies beyond 16,384 parts.
- In enterprise builds, a key account's message counts toward no tenant's quota.

## Task 6

### Part 6a: the index fences and the background gates

Part 6a ported the fork's index fences onto v1.0.1's index queue (`services/src/index_queue/`)
and the store's index pass, and gated the background tasks that would touch a key account's
data. It resolved the two `services` conflicts (`task_manager/index.rs`, deleted as v1.0.1
did; `task_manager/manager.rs`) and filled the ledger cells of `store/src/search/index.rs`,
`store/src/write/batch.rs` and `store/src/write/mod.rs`. Lines are the worktree's after this
part. No background path obtains session keys, indexes decrypted content, or rewrites a key
account's sealed calendar data: the builders never read a key account's blob or event
content, and the share-revocation cleanup stages nothing of a key-account owner.

#### The index queue's ordering (ruling 19), from source

One worker runs a partition, and it runs that partition's entries in order:
- **One worker per partition on a node.** The manager dispatches a partition only if
  `state.claimed.insert(partition)` succeeds (`index_queue/manager.rs:245`), and removes it
  from `claimed` only when the worker's result arrives (`:66`).
- **Across nodes,** a worker holds the partition's lock (`try_lock_search_index`,
  `index_queue/index.rs:67`, `LOCK_EXPIRY` 10 minutes). It renews the lock only at the top of
  each batch, once less than `LOCK_MARGIN` (60 s) is left (`drain_partition`, `:107-131`).
- **In order within a worker.** `read_queue` (`:687`) iterates the partition's
  `SearchIndexClass::Queue` keys in ascending order: account, document, `created_at`.
  `pending_items` (`:801`) collapses one document's entries, and any unindex among them makes
  the document a deletion. `process_items` (`:191`) then:
  - builds the documents;
  - writes them, with the deletions, in one `Sink::flush` (`:283`), which for the internal
    store is one `index_account_documents` call, one `account_batch` and its chunks in
    sequence;
  - only then clears the exact queue keys it read (`:296`).

  An unindex queued after the read keeps its own key, so the next batch of the same loop
  runs it.

So on one node, an unindex of a document cannot run between two chunks of that document's
index write. The window the design names remains: a worker reads a row, the message is
deleted, and the worker writes a stale document. The next batch's unindex then removes it,
since `stored_documents` reads the stored document first. R54's scan sees the queue entries
throughout, because they are cleared only after the flush.

**Decision:** R62's per-chunk guard and R63's confirmation read (with `take_clears`) are
retired. This is provisional until Task 7's runtime reproduction (ruling 63). R62's term
scans (`has_account_search_terms`, `za_has_{email,calendar}_search_terms`) are kept.
`INDEX_GUARD_FAIL_ONCE` is retired with them: removed from `store::za_test`, and its
re-export dropped from `common::za::test_hooks`.

Two residuals, neither reproduced (both Task 7's):
- **A lost cluster lease.** One batch, its build and flush, can outlast the lease's
  remainder, and then another node runs the partition. R58 in every chunk stops a superseded
  tail once the account is provisioned.
- **Account destruction runs outside the partition lock.** `destroy_account` calls
  `unindex_account` and `clear_search_index_queue` directly
  (`task_manager/tasks/destroy_account.rs:235`, `:245`). So a pass in flight for the destroyed
  account can commit chunks after the range delete, and orphan terms under the destroyed id.
  They matter only to an account that reuses the id, and R62's kept term scans refuse its
  setup with the existing 409. The remedy is the usual one: recreate the account.

#### R54: the queue scan, the status key and `ShareRevokeCleanup`

Part 3a had already ported ruling 22's queue scan: any `SearchIndexClass::Queue` entry of the
account in the Email, Calendar, Contacts or File index (`common/src/auth/vault.rs:800`).
Part 6a adds the rest:
- **The partition's status key.** For each of the four indexes, the scan also reads
  `SearchIndexClass::QueueStatus { index, partition: account_id }` (`:827-846`). A failed pass
  sets it, and the next completed pass clears it (`update_partition_status`,
  `manager.rs:322-386`). A status key left with no entries is stale, and the partition's
  next pass clears it, so it blocks setup only until then.
- **`ShareRevokeCleanup` counts** for its `account_id`, the owner (`:877`), as the design
  says. This replaces part 3a's "no" in its R54 table. The record holds only the owner, and
  the task's grantee-side writes are container counters. The task stays exhaustive over
  `Task`, and no other variant is open.
- **The fence order is unchanged.** `za_has_tasks` stays where part 3b put it in
  `za_assert_no_data`, before R51's scan.

**No terminal failure.** A failing partition retries after `retry_delay(attempts)`:
30 s × 2^min(attempts, 8), capped at 30 minutes (`manager.rs:316-320`, `mod.rs:38-39`). It never
reaches a failed state, so its entries block provisioning until they drain. Part 6a adds the
fault hook for the brief's test: `services::index_queue::za::test_hooks::INDEX_BUILD_FAIL_ONCE`,
which fails the named account's next Email or Calendar build once. The test itself is 6c's.

#### R58 in every chunk (ruling 18)

Three pieces carry the fence:
- **The carrier is attached at the builders' plain returns.** `build_email_document` wraps
  both returns, the inline one and the `spawn_blocking` one, and `build_calendar_document`
  wraps its one return, all in `za_index_fence` (`index_queue/document.rs`).
- **`account_batch` places it** (`store/src/search/index.rs`). `za_index_fences` collects the
  pass's distinct absent keys before the documents are consumed. After `build_batch`, one call
  to `BatchBuilder::za_assert_absent_in_every_chunk` opens every non-empty commit chunk with
  `AccountId`, `Collection::Principal`, `DocumentId(0)` and `AssertValue(None)` on field 150,
  and shifts the chunk offsets to match.
- **The method refuses any other kind of batch.** It errors, and leaves the batch unchanged,
  when the batch holds pending changes or counters, or any operation other than a
  `ValueClass::SearchIndex` value. Those operations name their own keys, so the selection the
  assertion leaves behind does not matter to them.

The cost is ruling 18's: one key read per chunk on an ordinary account's pass. A pass holds
one partition, which is one account, so all of its fenced documents share one key.

Tests: the store unit tests run without a hook, since a document of 2,500 distinct terms
splits its pass by itself. `search::index::za_tests` covers:
- every chunk is fenced;
- an unfenced pass carries no assertion;
- the method refuses a batch that is not a search pass;
- on an ephemeral store, a fenced pass for a provisioned account writes nothing, while
  ordinary and unfenced controls are written.

`za_test::tests::a_vault_written_mid_pass_stops_the_later_chunks` (`test_mode`) parks the pass
after chunk 0 with `INDEX_CHUNK_PAUSE` and writes the vault record. The pass then fails, and
no `Document` key lands.

Mutation: fencing chunk 0 only fails two of these tests, the structural test and the
mid-pass test.

#### The F4 builders

- **Email** (`index_queue/za.rs`, `za_email_document`, called first in
  `build_email_document`). For a key account, or for a stub (format byte) whose account no
  longer exists, the builder returns a document without reading the blob. A stub therefore
  never reaches `index_document`, its attachment extraction or `spawn_blocking`. An existing
  ordinary account keeps upstream's document (R47).
  - **Premise mismatch:** the brief and the design say this document holds `ReceivedAt` and
    `Size`, but 1.0's Email index has no field for either. `EmailSearchField` holds text
    fields only, and arrival time and size are served from the message cache. So the
    document holds only the account and document ids, and no term derives from the message.
    6c's leak rule for `SearchDocument` should read "ids only, no term" for key accounts.
- **Calendar.** A key account gets no document (`za_skips_calendar`), and neither does a
  sealed archive (`za_is_sealed_event`, over 1.0's `CalendarEventField::Content`). Every other
  event is fenced.
- **Reindex.** `reindex_account` only queues entries (`index_queue/reindex.rs:102-178`), so
  rebuilt documents go through the same builders. No reindex path builds a document itself.
- **The un-index path.** In `delete_email_metadata`, the arm that reads `raw_headers` now
  calls `za_thread_subject_in`, which answers an empty subject for a header section that
  starts with the stub's format byte.
  - A stub's structure has no header entry, so `thread_subject()` already resolves it to `""`
    without reading the section. Unit tests `index_queue::za::tests` show both.
  - The SEL `ArchivedItem` snippet after it reads the envelope, which is empty for a stub. It
    is untouched.
  - The destroyed-stub un-index integration test is 6c's.
- **File and Contacts** keep upstream's builders, and are not fenced (see the questions).

#### The share-revocation cleanup

`stage_calendar`, `stage_event` and `stage_notification` (`task_manager/tasks/grantee_cleanup/stage.rs`)
return `Ok(false)` first when `za_skips_owner` holds. That check is a fork method in
`grantee_cleanup/za.rs` over `za_is_key_account(owner_id)`: an unknown owner is not a key
account, and a lookup error is returned so the task retries. Staging would deserialize a key
account's event content and rewrite it with `update_full`, without keys. The fork's keyed
revoke handles the grantee's entries. `rebuild_shares` runs unchanged: it writes only the
`acl_grant` and `acl_revoke` index keys derived from object ACLs.

#### The move route's input bytes

`BatchBuilder::accounted_input_bytes(&mut self)` (`store/src/write/batch.rs`) re-derives 0.16's
count over 1.0's `Operation`:
- **First,** it materializes the pending change logs and counters, as `commit_points()` does.
- **Per operation,** it counts 64 bytes plus the key hint, then by kind:
  - fixed and patched values count their length (a patch overwrites in place);
  - serializable values count their size hint (only `PendingMessageData` and `PendingTask`
    use them);
  - counters count 16;
  - index keys, cleared ranges and serialized logs count their lengths;
  - a log with pending ids counts `Changes::za_serialized_size_bound` (`write/log.rs`): two
    bytes, plus at most 10 per list count and 10 per id.
- **A merge counts no input.** 1.0's `MergeFnc` is a boxed closure, which has no countable
  inputs. So the count is a bound only for batches whose merges emit at most the 64-byte
  allowance. The move route's merges are the metadata viewer states (12 bytes,
  `merge_viewer_state`) and `clear_if_equals` (none).

#### Mechanical fixes (ruling 62)

Tasks 3, 4 and 5's `jmap`, `http` and `managesieve` code needed one fix:
- `http/src/api/shares.rs:15` (now `:22`): `use base64::{Engine,
  engine::general_purpose::STANDARD}` became `use encodify::base64::STANDARD`. v1.0.1 removed

#### The conflicts

- **`jmap/src/api/session.rs`.** v1.0.1's `jmap_account` builds each account object. The fork
  trims it afterwards (`za_trim_account`) and filters `primaryAccounts` with the same rule
  (`za_hidden_capability`). The key-account status comes from the account already read.
- **`jmap/src/principal/get.rs`.** v1.0.1's `Accounts` property comes from cached capability
  templates (`AccountObjects`), shared between principals. The fork filters each principal's
  copy of the template. Its `Capabilities` property keeps v1.0.1's calendar object, filtered
  for a key principal. The key-account status comes from `visible_principal`'s account, so the
  fork's second `account()` read is gone.
- Both now hide `calendars:parse` too: v1.0.1's account objects carry it, and 0.16's
  principal list didn't.

#### Object metadata

The rule: object metadata is refused when the request's account **or** its caller is a key
account (`za_metadata_refused`, `jmap/src/api/metadata/za.rs`; both accounts are read with
`account()`, which fails closed). A key account's own objects never hold it. A key account's
private metadata on an ordinary account's object would be its own data in the clear.
Requests between ordinary accounts take upstream's paths, and the presence flags stay
upstream's.

| Surface | Site | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| Patches a client names (`*/set` create and update, `Email/import`, a copy's patches) | `MetadataWriter::prepare_for`, `prepare_new`, `prepare_ingest` (`writer.rs`) | `forbidden` per object, before validation, so a no-op patch is refused too |
| Every non-empty update, including a copy's source containers | `MetadataUpdate::prepare`, `prepare_ingest` (`write.rs`), the only paths to a batch or to `IngestMetadata` | `forbidden` per object |
| Preloads for a set or a copy | `ObjectMetadata::preload` (`object.rs`); `MetadataPreload` itself is pure | no container is read |
| Reads (`*/get`) | `ObjectMetadata::load` (`object.rs`), the only caller of `MetadataGet::load` | no container is read; the objects carry no metadata property, which is upstream's answer where metadata is unsupported |
| Filters (`*/query`) | `ObjectMetadata::evaluate` (`object.rs`), the only caller of `MetadataQuery::evaluate` | `unsupportedFilter` with `ZA_METADATA_REFUSED`; `Email/query`'s ruling 57 refusal still runs first for a key account |
| `*/changes` | `changes/get.rs` | `MetadataChanges::Unsupported`: no private change log is read, and no metadata-only change is reported |
| Session | `api/session.rs` | no `metadata` capability on a key account's object, nor on any account in a key account's session; upstream's rule then drops the session-level capability when no account offers it |
| `Principal/get` | `principal/get.rs` | the same rule on each account object |
| IMAP `GETMETADATA`, `SETMETADATA`, `LIST … RETURN (METADATA …)` | `imap/src/op/metadata/{get,set}.rs`, `op/list.rs`, through `za_assert_metadata_allowed` (`op/metadata/za.rs`) | `NO [NOPERM]` in a key account's session, before any read; also for a key account's mailbox named by another session (`metadata_mailbox`), which ruling R34 already keeps out of that session's list |
| IMAP `CAPABILITY` | `imap/src/op/capability.rs` | no `METADATA` or `LIST-METADATA` in a key account's session (`za_key_session`) |

`apply.rs`, `copy.rs` and `filter.rs` are pure: they hold no store access, and their output
reaches the store only through the sites above.

#### Decisions recorded for the primary

- **The rule covers the caller too.** A key account reading or writing an ordinary account's
  objects gets no object metadata, shared or private. The alternative would refuse only
  private metadata for a key caller.
- **Reads answer as unsupported, not with an error.** A metadata-aware client's default
  `Mailbox/get` selects every metadata property, so a method error would break a key
  account's ordinary gets. The answer is what an account without `JmapMetadataGet` sees, and
  the session no longer advertises the capability.
- **Copies.** A copy out of a key account carries none of its source's containers, since the
  preload reads none. A copy into a key account whose source has containers is refused per
  object, even when the client named no metadata. This covers `ContactCard/copy` and
  `FileNode/copy` into a key account's shared address book or file tree, and the DAV dead
  properties those sources may hold. DAV keeps a key account's contact and file dead
  properties in the clear (design, "Other object kinds"), so the primary may relax this to
  carry DAV-only containers.
- **IMAP's advertisement uses the session's keys.** `CAPABILITY` is synchronous, so it checks
  `za_key_session()`. A session that logged in before its account was provisioned holds no
  keys and still advertises `METADATA`, but its commands are refused (the refusal reads the
  account).

#### Deferred, by owner

**Part 6c (tests)**
- `Mailbox/set` with metadata properties on a key account answers `forbidden`, and the same
  call without them succeeds; `Email/set` update and `Email/import` likewise.
- `Mailbox/get` and `Email/get` with metadata properties answer objects without them;
  `Mailbox/query` with a metadata filter answers `unsupportedFilter`.
- A key account's session object and `Principal/get` carry no `urn:ietf:params:jmap:metadata`;
  an ordinary account's do (the control).
- IMAP: `GETMETADATA`, `SETMETADATA` and `LIST "" "*" RETURN (METADATA (/shared/comment))`
  answer `NO [NOPERM]` for a key account; `CAPABILITY` lacks `METADATA` and `LIST-METADATA`;
  an ordinary session answers all of them (the control).

**Task 7**
