  session object. At 1.0 it gains v1.0.1's new capabilities, minus `metadata` at both levels
  for a key account.

### Part 6c: cache snapshots

Part 6c traced every input of 1.0.1's cache swap tier and wrote `cache_swap_tests`
(`tests/src/za/cache_swap.rs`). It adds **no production code**: upstream builds every
snapshot from stored rows only, and every restore is revalidated before it is served. This
confirms the interface sheet's frozen point 7 ("Snapshots need no fork code"). No test hook
was added. Lines are the worktree's.

#### What the tier can persist

The tier persists exactly two kinds of entry. `Snapshot` has two variants,
`Messages(Arc<MessageStoreCache>)` and `Resources(Arc<GroupwareResources>)`
(`common/src/cache/swap/writer.rs:92`), and `SwapCached` is implemented for those two types
only (`swap/evict.rs:20`, `:26`). The only caller of `SwapTier::store` in production code is
the writer's `write` (`writer.rs:471`), which stores `Snapshot::encode` (`:119`). Only the
five caches built with `swap.eviction(..)` (`config/inner.rs:148-180`) and the two large tiers
(`cache/large.rs`) feed it. Every other cache uses `DefaultLifecycle` and stays in memory.

| Cache | Swappable | Fed by | What an entry holds | A key account's entry | Fence on restore |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `messages`, `messages_large` | yes, `MessagesCache` only (`swap/messages.rs:64`) | `email/src/cache/mod.rs`: `full_cache_build`, `revalidate_messages`, `apply_keyword_writes`; all through `publish_messages` or `publish_loaded_messages` | per message: document id, change id, arrival time, sent time, size, keyword bits, thread id, mailbox UIDs; custom keyword names | read from the stored `MessageData` (`message_datas`). A key account's `sent_at` is never stored (`email/src/message/ingest.rs:377`), and a copy into or out of a key account is refused (`copy.rs:103`). `apply_keyword_writes` patches only the keyword bits just committed. Nothing comes from a stub's sealed blob. | Restored with `verification: Default` (`email/src/cache/mod.rs:616`), which is never fresh (`cache/mod.rs:94`). So `revalidate_messages` (`:203`) applies the change log from the snapshot's change id, or rebuilds if the log was truncated. Mailboxes are rebuilt from the store on every restore (`:599`). |
| `events`, `contacts`, `files`, `scheduling`, `resources_large` | yes, `GroupwareResources` (`swap/resources.rs:441`) | `groupware/src/cache/mod.rs`: `build_full_cache`, `process_changes`, `fetch_container`; every record comes from a stored archive (`push_from_archive`) | calendar: slug, ACLs, `(account, time zone, flags)` per preference, metadata kinds, ETag. Event: DAV names, UID, start, duration, created and modified times, flags, ETag. Inbox item: names, event id, `changed_by`, principals, flags. Contact card: names, UID, times. File: name, size, parent, ACLs, flags. Plus the base path and the path index. | the stored metadata row, which upstream derives from the sealed tree (`refresh_from_content`, sheet item 4). The display name, the calendar's preference names (where the owner's ring lives) and any content are never cached. Contacts and files are cached as their store holds them: plaintext for every account (part 6a's open question). | Restored with `verification: Default` (`swap/resources.rs:778`), and a stale base path is discarded (`groupware/src/cache/mod.rs:844`). `fetch_groupware_resources_at` (`:158`) then applies the change log before it returns: `is_fresh` fails (`:177`). ACL changes are logged: `ObjectIndexBuilder::build` logs a container update for every archive update (`common/src/storage/index/mod.rs:191`), and the fork's grant mutations write the calendar through `Calendar::update` (`sharing/mutations.rs:433`). |
| `access_tokens` (`SpillCache`) | no | `Server::access_token` | `AccessTokenInner` | in memory only | `account_epoch` |
| `http_auth` | no | `http/src/auth/authenticate.rs` | token hash, then account, token, expiry | in memory only. Served only with resident keys of the current generation (part 3b). | revocation epoch, generation fence |
| `za_keys` (`vault::cache::KeyCache`) | no | logins | `Arc<SessionKeys>` | in memory only | `remove_account` on Account and AccessToken invalidation |
| `accounts` | no | `Server::account` | `AccountCache` with `za_generation` in `AccountStorage` | in memory only | `account_epoch` |
| refresh slots (`RefreshSlots`) | handoff only | `notify_refresh_*`, `SwapEviction` | an `Arc` to one of the two swappable entries until it is written | as the entries above | not read back |
| `metadata_viewers`, `grant_revocations`, address, domain, role, list, DKIM, DNS and spam caches | no | various | no key-account content | in memory only | n/a |

`SessionKeys` cannot reach a snapshot. Neither swappable type has a field that holds an
`AccessToken`, a `SessionKeys` value or a `Secret`, and the writer reads only those two caches
(`writer.rs`, `peek` and `take_snapshot`). The caches that do hold keys or authority (the
access-token, HTTP and key caches and the account cache) are never swapped. So
`account_epoch` and `za_generation` have nothing to fence on load: a snapshot carries no
authority. Access comes from the token's ACL list and the cache's ACLs. The token is
rebuilt in memory; the ACLs are revalidated against the change log before any request sees
them.

#### Invalidation and removal

- `CacheInvalidation::GroupwareResources` drops the resident entries and deletes the
  account's four resource snapshots (`invalidate.rs:583-590`).
- `CacheInvalidation::MessageCache` drops the resident entry and the writer's pending state
  (`forget`), but it leaves the persisted message snapshot in place (`:591-594`). The next
  restore revalidates it.
- Account destruction forgets the account and removes all five snapshots
  (`services/src/task_manager/tasks/destroy_account.rs:261-272`).
- A grant or revoke invalidates the grantees' access tokens only. The owner's resource
  snapshot is corrected by revalidation on the next load.

#### Guards

None. Every entry that can be swapped is a projection of stored rows. So a key account's
snapshot holds only what its stored rows hold in the clear, and the leak scanner already
rules on those rows. The serialization and restore paths are backend-independent:
`Snapshot::encode` and `from_snapshot` sit above `SwapBackend`, so the proof covers Redis too.
Redis itself is not run.

#### Premise corrections

- **The blob-store tier cannot use the filesystem blob store.** `BlobStore::build_cache_swap`
  refuses `BlobSwapStore::FileSystem`. It also refuses `BlobSwapStore::Default` when the
  configured blob store is the filesystem one (`store/src/build/swap.rs:10`, `:25`, `:52`):
  that store skips writes whose length has not changed. With `BLOB_STORE=FileSystem` the
  boot records a build error, and the harness's `assert_no_errors` panics. So the suite's
  `BlobStore` server uses the default blob store, which is the data store when `BLOB_STORE`
  is unset. The suite refuses `BLOB_STORE=FileSystem` with a message. The brief and the design
  ("`BlobStore` on a filesystem blob store") need this correction. The CI step must not set
  the variable.
- **Framing is not a privacy control.** The frame's xxh3 checksum
  (`swap/frame.rs`) is framing only. Snapshots are stored uncompressed: the file tier writes
  the frame as is, and the blob tier stores it with `CompressionAlgo::None`. The suite scans
  both the stored bytes and the decoded frames.

#### Observations (no fork action)

- **The writer does not rewrite a snapshot it believes current.** `write_if_changed` skips a
  target whose `persisted_change_id` equals the cache's change id (`writer.rs`). So a file
  replaced behind the writer stays until the account's next change. That is harmless,
  because every restore revalidates. The suite's late-writer rounds depend on that
  revalidation, not on the rewrite.
- **Ordinary-era metadata can outlive its messages.** An ordinary account's message
  snapshot can still hold the ids, times, sizes, keyword bits and custom keyword names of
  messages that were deleted before the account was provisioned, until its next write. It
  holds no content, and provisioning still requires the account to hold no mail.

#### The suite

`za::cache_swap::cache_swap_tests` is its own `#[tokio::test(flavor = "multi_thread")]`
function, outside `za_tests`. For each tier it boots one `TestServer` through Task 2's
`with_cache_swap`. The tiers are `LocalFile` under `<temp>/cache_swap_file/swap`, then
`BlobStore { store: Default }`. Each server uses ruling 20's settings: `min_account_size: 0`,
`flush_changes: 1` and `flush_interval: 0`. The suite asserts that the tier is enabled and
uses that cadence. Every flush goes through `SwapTier::flush()`, and the first case asserts
that `snapshots_written()` grew. Each tier then runs both cases. After that, the suite stops
the writer, shuts the server down and waits until the listener ports refuse connections.

Each restore is checked by the `CacheEvent::SwapHit` event for that account and collection
(`za::mail::Traced`), so no case passes on a rebuild from the store. Every scan reads:

- the stored bytes without the server: every file under the tier's directory, or each part's
  pointer and both slots in the blob store;
- the frames decoded from those bytes by the shared codecs alone
  (`MessagesCache::from_snapshot`, `GroupwareResources::from_snapshot`): their `Debug` form,
  plus the string arenas of the resource chunks and the path index.

The scans assert that at least one frame of each kind decoded. The needles are:

- ten protected text canaries: `X-WR-CALNAME`, `SUMMARY`, `DESCRIPTION`, `LOCATION`,
  `COMMENT`, an `X-` property, the calendar display name, and the mail subject, sender name
  and body;
- each account's password and its Basic credential;
- each key account's `mk`, `ewk` and `mwk`, unlocked as a login does (`za_unlock`), each raw
  and in standard base64 (padded and unpadded), URL-safe base64 and hex;
- the vault marker `$za$`, which the owner's ring name carries.

**`swap_roundtrip_contains_no_protected_canary`** (accounts `swap-key`, `swap-plain` and
`swap-pending`):

- *Provisioning race.* `swap-pending` is created with no password. Its calendar cache is
  built while the account is ordinary (the default calendar), and the snapshot is flushed and
  kept. `setup-token` is parked at `SETUP_TOKEN_PAUSE`, after its first emptiness check.
  Meanwhile the account's calendar is evicted and restored from that snapshot (`SwapHit`).
  Then the token and setup complete.
- *Round trip.* Each account receives a PUT of `swap-visible-res.ics` with every event
  canary, and LMTP delivers one message with the mail canaries. Each account's caches are
  then built and flushed. The scan finds no needle in any stored or decoded snapshot. The
  positive control: each account's calendar snapshot holds the resource name, both raw and
  decoded, and resolves `default/swap-visible-res.ics`; each message snapshot decodes to one
  message. The ordinary account is the differential: upstream's caches carry no content for
  any account.
- *Eviction and fresh load.* After `remove_resources` and `remove_messages`, the key
  account's GET restores the calendar (`SwapHit`) and serves the sealed event's summary with
  the owner's keys. The message cache restores with one message.
- *Stale snapshots.* For `swap-key`, the snapshot taken before a later event and message is
  stored again after them. For `swap-pending`, the ordinary-era calendar snapshot is stored
  again. In both cases a late writer is modelled. The fresh load restores it and serves both
  events and both messages. A final scan finds no needle.

**`swap_restore_after_revoke_cannot_restore_access`** (`swap-owner`, `swap-reader` and
`swap-never`, all key accounts):

- *Setup.* The owner makes calendar `swapshare` (its display name is a canary) and PUTs an
  event. `swap-never`'s GET is the oracle for refusal. The owner pins the reader and grants
  `view`, and the reader reads the event. The owner's snapshot is flushed and kept, and it
  is asserted to hold the reader's grant.
- *Restore racing the revoke.* `shares/revoke` is parked at `SHARE_COMMIT_PAUSE`. Meanwhile
  the owner's calendars are evicted and restored (`SwapHit`). The restored cache holds the
  grant, and the reader still reads the event. Then the revoke commits, and the reader gets
  the oracle's answer. The snapshot written after a revalidation holds no grant.
- *Late writer, two rounds.* In each round the pre-revoke snapshot is stored again, the
  owner's and the reader's resource caches are evicted, and the owner's calendars are
  restored (`SwapHit`). The restored cache holds no grant, and the reader gets the oracle's
  answer. The stale snapshot is stored again, the owner's cache evicted, and the reader's
  request is refused again. The owner still reads the event. A final scan finds no needle.

**Runtime assumptions:**

- The suite runs alone: `STORE=RocksDb RUST_MIN_STACK=16777216 cargo test -p tests
  cache_swap_tests -- --nocapture`. Its two servers bind the default listener ports one after
  the other, and those ports are `SO_REUSEPORT`.
- `BLOB_STORE` is unset, or names a store other than the filesystem one.
- The default calendar created by the ordinary account's first cache build does not count as
  calendar data for `setup-token`. It has no shared metadata container (part 3b's check).
- A grantee's GET while the revoke is parked is not blocked by the parked mutation. The
  harness's DAV client times out after 500 ms.
- `HookParked`, `za_post`, `za_post_as`, `za_setup`, `za_unlock`, `pinning::pin`,
  `mail::{Traced, deliver}` and `TestServerBuilder::with_cache_swap` behave as their 1.0
  ports in Tasks 2 to 5 describe. The suite was checked against them by reading only, and
  formatted with rustfmt. It compiles first at Task 7.
- The suite calls neither `assert_is_empty` nor any cleanup. Each tier's store is discarded
  with the suite's temporary directory. The blob tier leaves temporary `BlobLink` keys,
  which expire after the retention period.

#### Deferred, by owner

  - R58 per chunk through HTTP;
  - the destroyed-stub un-index test;
  - the reindex and attachment-indexer tests;
  - the mid-pass provisioning and deletion tests;

  - the object-metadata and IMAP METADATA tests.

### Part 6d: the remaining Task 6 tests and the leak scanner at 1.0

Part 6d wrote the Task 6 tests that parts 6a to 6c left open, ported the
za tests that still used 0.16's index tasks and storage (`leak.rs`,
`index_task.rs`, `mail/fence.rs`'s index cases, `mail/search.rs`'s scan),
and extended the leak scanner to 1.0's spaces. It changes no crate code and
adds no hook: ruling 63's reproduction uses the existing
`INDEX_WRITE_PAUSE`, and a partition is held through its status key (below).
Commits: `34f704f37` (the scanner and the helpers), `e40fa2c10` (the
metadata refusals), `3ca6e4941` (the index cases).

**Checks.** `cargo check -p tests --tests` reaches type checking: the
crate's other files still fail (152 errors, Task 7's), and none is in a file
this part touched, which also carry no warning. Borrow checking of these
files is not confirmed, because the crate does not finish compiling.
rustfmt is clean on every touched file.

#### Holding a partition

0.16's tests held the task consumer, which does not hold 1.0's index queue.
`utils::za::hold_index_partition` writes the partition's status key as a
failed pass writes it, with its retry an hour away, so the manager skips
the partition (`scan_index_queue`) while its entries stay queued.
`release_index_partition` clears the key and wakes the manager, as
`x:IndexQueueStatus/set` with a `Running` status does. A partition must be
idle when it is held, and `wait_for_tasks` waits for a held partition.
Beside them: `queue_entries`, `partition_status`, `wait_for_index_queue`
(bounded, it does not wait out a retry delay), `search_keys` (a document's
id and value, and the term keys with their verbatim bytes),
`index_search_documents`, `unindex_search_documents`,
`unindex_search_account` and `delete_event_records`. 1.0's
`SearchStore::index` and `unindex` are unreachable on the internal store, so
the tests go through `index_account_documents` and `unindex_account`.

#### The cases and where they live

| Case | Module | What fails it |
|---|---|---|
| `reindex_key_account_stays_visible_fields_only` | `index_task::test_reindex` | A key account's rebuilt Email document that holds a term or more than its ids, or a threading key, non-empty sort keys or a leak-scan violation; the ordinary control must hold the subject, body and attachment tokens. The records are first removed with the account-wide range delete, so the reindex task must rebuild them. |
| `attachment_indexer_never_reads_sealed_blob` | `index_task::test_attachment_indexer` | The key account's sealed blob is deleted and its entry queued again: a blob read fails the pass ("Blob not found"), so the partition must drain within 10 s. The control proves the deletion is detected: the ordinary account's same pass enters its retry state, and its unindex then clears it. |
| The index-queue fault test | `index_task::test_index_retry` | `INDEX_BUILD_FAIL_ONCE` fails a calendar build. The partition keeps its entry, and `x:IndexQueueStatus/get` reports it failed. The reset (`x:IndexQueueStatus/set`) makes the retry run within 10 s, well inside the 30 s delay, and it indexes the event. A second failure with the events deleted refuses setup with R54's "account has unfinished tasks". After the reset, the queue drains and setup proceeds. |
| Provisioning mid-pass | `index_task::test_mid_pass_provisioning`; `fence::superseded_index_write_loses_to_provisioning` and `superseded_index_tail_loses_to_provisioning` | `index_task` uses `FORCE_COMMIT_SPLIT` and a two-message pass: chunk 0 holds every term merge and the first `Document` key, and chunk 1 the second. On one node, R46 refuses setup while the pass is parked. A superseding node B is simulated (`emulate_superseding_node`), and its deletions leave the second message's postings, which R62's term scan refuses. After the account-wide unindex, setup completes. The resumed chunk 1 must fail its assertion: nothing lands, the partition enters its retry state, and one reset drains it. Fencing chunk 0 only fails it. `fence`'s two cases do the same with a whole small pass and with a large body's natural split. |
| Deletion mid-pass | `index_task::test_mid_pass_deletion`, run last in the suite | The account is destroyed while its pass is parked between chunks, and no orphan may be left under its id. Part 6a expects this to fail: destruction runs outside the partition lock. |
| The ordering reproduction (ruling 63) | `fence::setup_refuses_an_in_flight_index_write` and its calendar twin | The import's index write is parked at `INDEX_WRITE_PAUSE`, and the message's unindex is queued before it runs. After the release there must be no document or term, and setup must pass with no cleanup. If the late write orphans the document, this case fails. |
| The destroyed-stub unindex | `index_task::test_destroyed_stub` | A key account's destroyed stub must leave no row, sort keys, blob link or search document, and its queue must drain without a failure. The format-byte arm itself is unit-tested by 6a (`services::index_queue::za::tests`), because a stub's thread subject resolves without reading its header section. |
| Metadata refusals | `gating::test_metadata` | Covered on the key account: `Mailbox/set` create and update with metadata (`forbidden`, `ZA_METADATA_REFUSED`), the same create without metadata (it succeeds), `Mailbox/get` (metadata omitted), `Mailbox/query` with `metadataExists` (`unsupportedFilter`) and the session capability. Over IMAP: `GETMETADATA`, `SETMETADATA` and `LIST … RETURN (METADATA …)` answer `NO [NOPERM]`, and `CAPABILITY` lists neither `METADATA` nor `LIST-METADATA`. An ordinary account's identical requests succeed. |
| The leak enumeration | `leak::{scan, test_index_control, test_mail, test_shared_structure}` | Below. |

The `fence.rs` index cases at 1.0:
- **R46** holds the Email partition.
- **R51** plants its document through `index_account_documents`. 1.0 has no `ReceivedAt` field.
- **R54's `account_tasks`** reads only the `MergeThreads`, `CalendarItipMessage` and `ShareRevokeCleanup` task records. Index work is read as queue entries.
- **R62's term scans** are kept. 0.16's "`Index` key alone" case is dropped, because every 1.0 field is a term.
- **R62's two between-chunks cases and R63's merge case are flipped.** The unindex waits behind the parked pass on the same partition: setup is refused meanwhile, and nothing is left afterwards.
- **R63's two other cases are retired** with the guard: `failed_guard_with_its_document_stored_defers_the_task` and `stopped_document_leaves_its_batch_written`.
- **`multi_chunk_calendar_merge_is_written_whole`** is ported unchanged.
- **The jmap variant's `wait_for_tasks`** (PR #66, "An unindex queued before the import's index write can orphan the document") stays until Task 7 runs the reproduction.

#### The leak rules per space

`scan` walks `Subspace::ALL` and keys every rule on the `Subspace` enum,
never on bytes. `SearchTerm` and `SearchDocument` are read from the
internal search store, and every other subspace from the data store. Each
record is also searched for the canaries, both raw and decoded.

| Space | A key account may hold in the clear | Source and reason | Planted violation, and the permitted record beside it |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Property` (`p`), `Collection::Metadata` | No `Private` container. A calendar's or event's `Shared` container is a sealed wrapper (`is_sealed_container`). A mailbox's, message's or Sieve script's `Shared` container is flagged. An address book's, card's or file's container is permitted: DAV keeps their dead properties in the clear. `Viewer` and `Owner` counters are 12 bytes. | Ruling 66. Rulings 16, 23 and 29. The design's "Other object kinds". `ViewerState`. | A `Private` container and a 5-byte `Owner` counter, beside a 12-byte `Viewer` counter (`test_index_control`). An unsealed container on a shared calendar and on its event, written by upstream's keyless writer (`test_shared_structure`). |
| `Property` (`p`), records | Events are checked as row and content pairs (`ZaEvent`): no display name, no per-account preference, a sealed tree, and the envelope matches the domain. Inbox items are checked as row and content pairs: `Itip` with no snapshot, and a sealed message. A record without its pair is flagged. | Task 4 (part 4c), ruling 13. | The existing controls: a planted plaintext event, now planted with `plant_event`; a plaintext item; the shared-calendar header and envelope mutations, now split between the row and the content. |
| `Immutable` (`c`) | A message row that is a stub byte-equal to `seal_stub`'s (`stub_structure` over its bundle and blob), and empty sort keys (`MessageSortKeys::default`). Nothing else of the account. | Mail spec 5.2; `za_ingest`. | Content-bearing sort keys over empty ones, and a stub rewritten with plaintext headers (`test_mail`). |
| `IndexProperty` (`v`) | No threading hash. A mailbox name index is permitted (visible by structure; mail spec 5.6). So are Sieve seen-id hashes and a submission's integer indexes. Anything else is unclassified and flagged. | Mail spec 5.4. By source, the only writers are `email` and `merge_threads`. | A threading hash, beside a mailbox-name hash. |
| `SearchTerm` (`z`) | No Email or Calendar term of the account. Contacts and File terms are permitted. | Calendar spec 9; mail spec 5.5; ruling 65. | Calendar and Email terms, beside a Contacts term. |
| `SearchDocument` (`m`) | No Calendar document. Every Email document holds its ids only (`IDS_ONLY_DOCUMENT`, `[0, 0, 0]`). | Ruling 64. | A Calendar document and an Email document that lists a term, beside an ids-only Email document and another account's Calendar document. |
| `SearchQueue` (`y`) | Entries keyed by index, account, document and creation id, whose value is one action byte. An empty queued-work marker. A status key that decodes as a `SearchIndexStatus`; its reason is searched for canaries. | `queue_document_index` and `queue_document_unindex`; `update_partition_status`. | An entry whose value carries more than its action, beside a valid entry and the hold's status key. |
| `System` (`u`) | The schema version (`0x00`), the node-id leases (`0x01`) and the UID leases (`ZA_UID_LEASE_PREFIX`, 37 bytes). Anything else is flagged. | `SystemKind`; ruling 24. | An unknown kind. |
| `BlobLink`, `Blobs`, `SpamSamples` | As at 0.16. | | The existing controls. |

**Ordinary-account positive control** (`test_mail`). A message delivered to
`plainmail26` must trip every mail rule against 1.0's real writes:
- "plaintext metadata row";
- "sort keys with content";
- "threading key of the account";
- "search term of the account";
- "search document with terms";
- "plaintext message blob".

The existing scanner controls and corruption controls are kept. 0.16's
plaintext-dead-property control becomes the container control. The token
search returns `Subspace`, and the visible reply token is now in the item's
content record (`CalendarNotificationField::Content`).

#### Fixture names

| Name | Used by |
|---|---|
| `plainmail26` | `leak::test_mail`'s ordinary control |
| `keymail30`, `plainmail27` | `test_reindex` |
| `keymail31`, `plainmail28` | `test_attachment_indexer` |
| `keymail32` | `test_destroyed_stub` |
| `plainmail29` | `test_index_retry` |
| `plainmail30`, `plainmail31` | `test_mid_pass_provisioning` and its control |
| `plainmail32` | `test_mid_pass_deletion` |
| `keymail33`, `plainmail33` | `gating::test_metadata` |

`plainmail19`, `21` and `22` (the retired R63 cases) are unallocated. The
next free names are `keymail34` and `plainmail34`.

The suite's order (`za/mod.rs`):
- `gating::test_metadata` runs after `gating::test_scheduling`.
- After `index_task::test_sealed_archive`, five cases run in turn: `test_destroyed_stub`, `test_reindex`, `test_attachment_indexer`, `test_index_retry` and `test_mid_pass_provisioning`.
- `index_task::test_mid_pass_deletion` runs after `mail::traces`, before the key accounts are destroyed.

#### Runtime assumptions (Task 7)

- **Indexing.** The za server indexes Email and Calendar, as 0.16's fence
  cases required. `build_calendar_document` returns before the fault hook
  when Calendar is not indexed.
- **Index passes.** A pass reads both of two queued messages
  (`index_batch_size` ≥ 2). Under `FORCE_COMMIT_SPLIT`, the two-document pass
  commits as described (6a's store unit test asserts 2 commit points).
- **Partition status.** A status key whose retry is in the future makes the
  manager skip the partition. A failed R58 assertion fails the pass, and the
  partition then enters its retry state.
- **`x:IndexQueueStatus/set`** accepts `{"status": {"@type": "Running"}}`.
  `x:IndexQueueStatus/get` serializes a failed status with
  `"@type": "Failed"`.
- **Stub byte equality.** `MetadataRow::encode(stub_structure(..), bundle)`
  is deterministic, and the stored stub equals it byte for byte. Otherwise
  every scan of a key account reports "stub whose structure is not the
  sealed stub's".
- **No other writers.** No upstream path writes another `IndexProperty`,
  `Immutable` or `System` record for a key account. Otherwise the scanner's
  "unclassified" rules flag it, and the rule needs a ruling.
- **Default permissions.** The default user role carries
  `JmapMetadataGet`/`Set` and `ImapMetadataGet`/`Set`.
- **Metadata defaults.** Vendor namespaces are on (the default).
- **`using`.** A key account's request may name
  `urn:ietf:params:jmap:metadata` in `using`, although its session does not
  advertise it.
- **Attachment extraction.** The text attachment is indexed in the
  `Attachment` field. The RTF part makes the row's attachment extractable.
- **Destruction.** A destroyed account's DestroyAccount task completes while
  another account's pass is parked.

  takes the partition's lock, or sweeps again after the pass) or accept the

- If the in-flight reproduction leaves nothing, remove the
  `jmap::za_variants` wait (PR #66) and record the evidence.
- `TestServer::fetch_email` (`utils/server.rs`) still reads 0.16's metadata
  archive. The scanner no longer uses it.

  - the next free names (`keymail34`, `plainmail34`);
  - the retired `INDEX_GUARD_FAIL_ONCE` in the hook list;

    only";

### Fix round 1: the Task 6 review's findings

The review (`task-6-review.md` in the plan workspace) raised F1-F11. This round fixes F1-F8
and F10, and applies ruling 69 to F9. F11 (CLAUDE.md's silent dependencies) is Task 9's.

| Finding | Change | Behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| F1, ruling 66 | `za_assert_no_data` (`http/src/api/vault.rs`) gains a term right after R54's: `Server::za_has_object_metadata` (`common/src/za/metadata.rs`), one pass over the account's `Collection::Metadata` records. It refuses any `Shared` container except an address book's, a card's or a file's (DAV keeps those in the clear for every account): a mailbox's, message's, Sieve script's or calendar's container, a server-level IMAP annotation (`Collection::Principal`), or one left on a destroyed document. It also refuses any `Private` container on the account's objects, and any `Owner` counter that counts containers (the account's own private metadata on another account's objects). A counter that counts none is checked against that owner's `Private` range too. The term sits after R54's for R54's reason: a task that wrote a container and cleared its queue entry between the two scans would otherwise pass both. The writers get the ordinary-writer assertion: `ZaMetadataFence` (from `Server::za_metadata_fence`, `None` on PostgreSQL or MySQL) asserts that the owner, and for a private container the viewer, hold no vault record. It is placed immediately before the container's operations, so it lands in each commit chunk that holds them, and the caller's selection is restored. Its carriers: JMAP's `PreparedMetadata` (set in `MetadataUpdate::prepare`, applied in `PreparedMetadata::build`), IMAP `SETMETADATA` (mailbox, private and server annotations, `imap/src/op/metadata/set.rs`), and the private viewer of the containers that `email_ingest_into` (plain path) and `copy_message` carry, whose owner the provisioning fence already covers. `za_provisioning_fence` now calls the same assertion (`za_assert_no_vault`). | Issuance answers 409 "account already holds object metadata" while the account holds such a container. A container write by a writer that read both accounts as ordinary fails once a vault record exists: JMAP reports the commit error, and IMAP answers `NO` ("Metadata was modified by another process."). An ordinary account pays one key read per container write. DAV's dead-property writer gets its fence by ruling 70 (below). |
| F2 | The leak scanner decodes each `Viewer` and `Owner` counter: an 11-byte key naming a real collection, and a 12-byte value that decodes as a `ViewerState`. A counter that counts containers is flagged ("metadata counter of private containers"). The walk also reads every owner's `Private` records whose viewer is the scanned account ("private metadata container of a key account on another account's object"). Every `Shared` container other than a calendar's or event's (sealed) and an address book's, card's or file's (permitted) is flagged. Before, only a mailbox's, message's or Sieve script's was flagged; a `Principal` annotation passed. | Stronger. Planted controls in `test_index_control`: a counting `Owner` counter, a mailbox's and a server-level `Shared` container, a permitted address book container, and a foreign `Private` container. The functional caller-rule case is in `gating::metadata_beyond_mailboxes`. |
| F3, ruling 66 | `MetadataWriter` records the copy sources whose shared containers the preload did not read, because the caller or the source account is a key account (`za_unread_sources`). `prepare_new` and `prepare_ingest` refuse such a copy (`za_drops_containers`) when the destination or the caller is a key account (`za_refused`). | A key account's `ContactCard/copy` or `FileNode/copy` from a source with containers answers `forbidden` (`ZA_METADATA_REFUSED`) per object, where it created the copy without them. A copy out of a key account by an ordinary caller into an ordinary account still carries none (6b's decision). For `Email/copy` into a key account, the per-object refusal now comes before `copy_message`'s. |
| F4, rulings R29 and R47 | `za_thread_subject_in(za_key, ..)` answers the stub's empty subject only when the account is a key account and the header section starts with the format byte. The classification is read lazily (`ZaUnindexAccount::for_row`): at most once per pass, and only for a row whose thread subject is in its header section. A real stub resolves without its header section. | An ordinary row whose header section starts with `0x01` takes upstream's `thread_subject_in` again, so its un-index clears the threading key its ingest wrote. Unit test: `index_queue::za::tests::an_ordinary_row_starting_with_the_format_byte_keeps_its_subject`. The review's fixture needs the empty `Subject` last to reach that arm: the envelope keeps the last one. |
| F5 | `za_index_fence`'s doc says that `account_batch` asserts in every chunk, that the `Document` keys follow the term merges, and that R62's guard and R63's read are retired. | None. |
| F6 | `gating::metadata_beyond_mailboxes` covers `Email/set` create and update with metadata, `Principal/get`'s account objects (the key account's own, and the control's as the key account reads it), the caller rule on the control's shared mailbox (`privateMetadata` and `metadata`), `Mailbox/changes` (a metadata-only change reported to the owner and not to the key account, which still sees the rename), and `ContactCard/copy` into the key account (F3). `mail::gates::rf_2_stale_grant` adds an ordinary session's `GETMETADATA` and `SETMETADATA` on a key account's mailbox: OK before provisioning, `NO` after, nothing stored. `mail::fence` adds `setup_refuses_object_metadata` (four refused containers and a permitted address book container) and `metadata_writes_lose_to_provisioning` (the fence over JMAP, IMAP and a server annotation, with controls). | Premise correction: at 1.0 `Email/import` takes no metadata (its request has no metadata property), so no import case exists. `FileNode/copy` takes the same writer path as `ContactCard/copy` and is not tested separately. |
| F7 | `test_reindex` delivers a larger message a second before a smaller one. It checks each sort's order against the `receivedAt` and `size` values that `Email/get` reports, and checks that the two orders differ. | Stronger. |
| F8 | See F2: a planted `Shared` container on a mailbox and one on `Collection::Principal`. | Stronger. |
| F9, ruling 69 | `index_task::test_mid_pass_deletion` asserts the orphan: the resumed chunk's document under the destroyed id, and nothing else. It then asserts the orphan is inert: no account holds the id, no queue entry is left, and an account created afterwards (`plainmail36`) gets a greater id. It then removes the orphan for the suite's final emptiness check. | The residual is accepted as retention, not access. The orphan's `Document` value lists its terms, and a term of up to 16 bytes is stored verbatim, so a destroyed account's plaintext tokens can persist under an id that is never reused (for a key account, Contacts and File terms only, ruling 65). |
| F10 | Real on one node. The writer handles `Forget` in turn, after a write it is already encoding, or after the rest of a flush pass. `forget` uses `try_send`, which drops the signal when the channel is full. So a snapshot could be stored after `remove_account`. `destroy_account` now awaits `SwapTier::flush()` between `forget` and `remove_account` (`services/src/task_manager/tasks/destroy_account.rs`). The flush is queued behind the signal and writes whatever is still pending, so no write of the account is in flight on this node when the snapshots are removed. | Account destruction waits for one flush of the swap writer. Residual: on a cluster with a shared tier (blob or Redis), another node's writer can still store a snapshot of the destroyed account after the removal. It holds the destroyed account's ids, times, names and UIDs (no content), and no account restores it, since ids are not reused (ruling 69). |

Fixture names: `keymail34` and `plainmail34` (`setup_refuses_object_metadata`), `plainmail35`
(`metadata_writes_lose_to_provisioning`) and `plainmail36` (`test_mid_pass_deletion`'s probe).
`gating::metadata_beyond_mailboxes` reuses `keymail33` and `plainmail33`. The next free names
are `keymail35` and `plainmail37`.

Ruling 70 closed the round's open item, the unfenced DAV dead-property writer:

| Finding | Change | Behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| Ruling 70 (F1's DAV writer) | One fork call, `za_dead_fence(server, account_id, collection, sealed)` (`dav/src/common/za.rs`), fences only calendar and calendar-event containers (`za_dead_fenced`). Address books, cards and files are excluded, because key accounts keep their dead properties in the clear. The fence is returned only when the writer took the plaintext path. `DeadPatch::prepare` passes `Some(za.is_some())`, and `DeadWrite` carries the fence (`za_fence`). The calendar sites apply it before they build the write, in the same commit chunk: `calendar/mkcol.rs`, and `calendar/proppatch.rs`'s calendar update, metadata-only update and event update. The copy path (`ContainerWrites::build`, `common/dead/containers.rs`) passes `None`, so the destination's classification is read with `account()`. The key account's sealed retry path builds no fence. Unit test: `common::za::dead_fence_tests::only_calendar_containers_are_fenced`. | A plaintext calendar or event container write, by a writer that read the account as ordinary, fails once a vault record exists. That covers PROPPATCH, MKCALENDAR and the calendar COPY and MOVE routes. A sealed write and every contact or file container write are unchanged. On an ordinary account, each such write costs one key read, and each calendar container copy costs one cached account read. |

For Task 9 (CLAUDE.md): `za_assert_no_data`'s new term ("account already holds object
metadata", after R54's), the metadata fences (JMAP, IMAP, ingest and copy; DAV's calendar
containers by ruling 70) beside the mail writers' ordinary-writer assertion, and the next free
names.


### Task 6 rulings

61. **Task 6 ran as four parts:** 6a (the `services` index queue and fences), 6b (the first
    `jmap`, `http` and `managesieve` compile, and the object-metadata refusals), 6c (cache
    snapshots and the swap suite) and 6d (the remaining tests and the leak rules). Its
    review ran on Opus at xhigh.
62. **Mechanical fixes during the first compile were made in place,** and listed. Behaviour
    changes would have gone back to their owners. In the event there was one mechanical fix
    (`http/src/api/shares.rs`'s base64 import).
63. **The index queue's ordering reproduction runs at Task 7,** since it needs the `tests`
    crate. R62's guard and R63's read are retired on source evidence, provisionally until
    then.
64. **A key account's Email search document holds ids only.** 1.0's Email index has no
    arrival-time or size field, so this is more private than 0.16. The `SearchDocument` leak
    rule is "ids only".
65. **A key account's File and Contacts indexes keep upstream's builders,** because contacts,
    address books and files aren't sealed for key accounts, as at 0.16.
66. **Object-metadata refusals apply when the account or the caller is a key account.**
    - Gets omit metadata rather than fail.
    - `/changes` reports no metadata changes.
    - IMAP answers `NO [NOPERM]`, and CAPABILITY drops `METADATA` and `LIST-METADATA`.
    - A copy out of a key account carries no metadata, and a copy into one, its own copies
      included, is refused when the source carries any.
    - Setup refuses an account that holds object metadata (409 "account already holds
      object metadata", placed after R54's term).
    - The metadata writers assert that no vault record exists in every commit chunk.
67. **The rest of Task 6 was split** into 6c and 6d for size.
68. **The swap suite's `BlobStore` tier runs on the default blob store.** 1.0.1 refuses a
    filesystem blob store for that tier.
69. **The destroy window is accepted.** `destroy_account` runs `unindex_account` outside the
    partition lock, so an index pass in flight can leave terms under the destroyed id. 1.0
    never reuses account ids (`OBJ_SEQ_ID`, a counter that only grows), so they are inert for
    access. Cost if wrong: a destroyed ordinary account's terms linger in the index.
70. **Calendar dead-property writes are fenced.** A write to a calendar or calendar-event
    dead-property container, by a writer that classified the account as ordinary, carries
    the vault-absence assertion in every commit chunk (`za_dead_fence`). Address-book,
    contact and file containers are excluded.

Open for Task 9: the new 409 reason goes into CLAUDE.md and into the Circulo contract
(`docs/api.md` in `circulo-account`).

#### The five held spots and their rulings

Part 7a held five files for the primary under ruling 72. The rulings (Decision 1's
dispositions) and what `ce4ee7c9a` does:

73. **`system::task`: subject deleted.** 1.0 removed `TaskManager.max_attempts`,
    `total_deadline` and `manager::next_retry_time` (a transient failure retries until it
    succeeds; `alert_after` marks it `Stalled`, with different semantics, so the cases are
    not re-expressed through it). `retry_time_boundaries`, the attempt-limit case and the
    terminal-deadline case are removed, with the helper and constant only they used; the
    rest of the module stays.
74. **`system::oidc`: ported as an equivalent test of the same flows;** the assertions on
    the four removed `OidcProvider` settings (`auth_code_max_attempts`,
    `refresh_token_renewal`, `anonymous_client_registration`,
    `require_client_registration`) are subject deleted. Written from 1.0's public API and
    RFC 6749, 7636, 8707 and 9207, not from SEL-only code (this is the dual-licensed
    `tests/src/system/oidc.rs`). The authorization request is built as the authorization
    endpoint's query string (`response_type=code` and the 0.16 test's parameters, S256),
    sent as `LoginRequest::AuthCode { query, consent: Some(true) }`; the code and `iss` are
    read from `LoginResponse::Authenticated { location }`. A refused authorization is "not
    an `authenticated` answer", since 1.0 answers every login with 200 and a typed body.
    `OAuthResponse.scope` is a `String`. Removed: the settings from the fixture, the
    `> REFRESH_TOKEN_RENEWAL` lower bound on the refresh token's `expires_in`, and the
    renewal-window checks (no new refresh token early, a new one inside the window); 1.0
    rotates the refresh token on every refresh. Kept: the refresh itself, the access
    token's one-second lifetime, the original refresh token's rejection at its expiry,
    single-use codes, and the anonymous confidential-registration refusal, which 1.0 still
    enforces (`registration.rs` authenticates every non-public registration).
75. **`store::lookup`: subject deleted** for the branches 1.0 cannot reach: the four
    `store_assert_is_empty` checks and the initial `store_destroy` under
    `if let InMemoryStore::Store` (1.0 has no in-memory store in the data store), and the
    `purge_in_memory_store` calls (a local store expires entries when read). `counter_incr`
    becomes `key_incr`; `counter_get`, which 1.0 removed, is read as an increment by zero,
    which 1.0 returns; `is_rate_allowed` loses its fourth argument. Everything else stays.
76. **`store::query`: internals replaced, with an equivalent test** (the search store is a
    fork hotspot, R58 and R62). Over 1.0's filter API (`Text`, `Integer`, `KeyValue`,
    `DocumentSet`): the text, keyword, phrase, key-value and boolean-group cases are
    unchanged; the dataset's integer columns (year, width, height) and the text range
    filters on the acquisition year, which 1.0's Email index cannot hold (ruling 64), become
    `DocumentSet` filters computed from the dataset, as 1.0's callers pass cache-side
    predicates; every sort is a `SortedSet` comparator ranked from the same columns (1.0's
    search stores sort only through set comparators); 0.16's PostgreSQL-only tie order
    goes, since 1.0 sorts locally for every store. Integer filtering moves to the Tracing
    index, the one index with integer fields: `EventType` equality, a `QueueId` range and an
    OR group are added. The internal store is written and unindexed through the index
    worker's `index_account_documents` and `index_trace` (`SearchStore::index` and
    `unindex` serve external stores only), and its trace deletion is `unindex_traces`, by
    id bucket: the six trace ids sit in three buckets of 2^48 ids, and the deletion removes
    the first two, where 0.16 deleted ids below 3 of 0..=5. The expected results are
    0.16's.
77. **`smtp::inbound::antispam`: expressed through a public path.** mail-auth 0.14 keeps
    `DkimOutput`'s per-result constructors and `ArcOutput::with_result` crate-private but
    exposes `From<DkimResult> for DkimOutput` and `From<ArcResult> for ArcOutput`
    (`ArcResult` is `DkimResult`), so the 24 `dkim.result` and `arc.result` fixture lines
    are built through them, with the same results. No expected tag changes.

#### Expectations for part 7c

Runtime facts this part relies on and did not run:

- `smtp::lookup::expressions` evaluates `key_*` and `counter_*` against an `sql` lookup
  store, which 1.0's `InMemoryStore::Sql` refuses (`NotSupported`); expect it to fail and
  need a ruling.
- `system::oidc`: the dynamically registered public client's login succeeds with
  `consent: Some(true)` in the same request, and a refused login answers 200 with a
  non-`authenticated` type.
- `store::query`: 0.16's indexed-text sort order for To, From and Cc equals the
  lexicographic order of the lowercased values, and its text "greater than" on the
  acquisition year equals string comparison; `KeyValueMatch::Contains` matches as 0.16's
  key-value `Contains` did.
- `store::lookup`: a local store's `key_incr` by zero returns the counter's value, and 0
  after it expired.

- `all_queued_messages` returns every due event of every queue, sorted by due time, where
  0.16's spool listed them through a fresh `Queue`; the SMTP queue tests that loop on it
  (`lmtp`, `smtp`, `retry`, `manager`) assume the same set.
- `CacheEvent::{Hit, Miss}` carry the looked-up key in `Key`, as 0.16's store events did,
  so `za::tracing`'s and `za::mail::traces`' controls and canaries keep their meaning.
- `smtp/inbound/{dkim2,dmarc,sign}.rs`: updating only `MtaStageData.dkim_sign_domain` after
  `mta_add_all_headers` keeps the headers it set.
- `store::ops`: the batch assigns the change id the `ChangeIdBe` patch writes when the set
  precedes `log_container_insert`.
- `webdav::cal_itip` and `za::sharing`: the recipient policies reproduce 0.16's messages
  (one per recipient group with every attendee visible).
- `imap::body_structure`: the fixtures still match 1.0's writer; a mismatch writes
  `*.imap_failed` and is a 1.0 output change for the expectation ledger.
- `webdav::{prop,lock}`: the large-property cases now probe `metadata.max_entry_size`.

### Part 7b: the two gates and the dispositions

Part 7b wrote the C10 gates and the dispositions file. It ran no cargo and changed no Rust
file. The self-tests that need disposable changes and the candidate's real exports belong to
part 7d.

#### The tools

All four are in `scripts/tests/`, and `README.md` there gives their inputs and the commands
that produce them. They build on `tests/qualification/tools/` and reimplement neither the
strict rule nor the cohort figures.

- **`check_test_inventory.py`.** It takes exactly the brief's arguments: `--baseline`,
  `--dispositions`, `--listing`, `--ignored-listing` and `--executed`.
  - **Listings.** Each listing is a capture of `cargo test ... -- --list` output, one file per
    configuration, named as in the baseline's `libtest.configurations`.
  - **Executed.** `--executed` is the output of `strict_rule.py functions`, run on the
    candidate's step exports and sources. So "executed" is the strict rule, recomputed.
  - **Failure classes.** It exits 1 on each brief condition, under its own name:
    - `missing`, `unselected` and `newly_ignored`;
    - `empty_selector` and `capture_missing`;
    - `status_change` and `missing_from`;
    - `call_not_executed` and `loop_not_executed`;
    - `helper_not_executed` and `tail_not_executed`.

    Each failure names the test, the step and the count.
  - **Granularity.** It reads step by step, so a loss in one step fails.
  - **Required configurations.** A capture is required for every CI step and every ignored-only
    configuration of the baseline. A selection move's target is required where a moved test is
    no longer in its old step.
- **`check_coverage.py`.** Its arguments are `--baseline`, `--dispositions`, `--merged`,
  `--sources` and an optional `--out`.
  - **The cohort.** It maps the 40 files across the moves, adds the file's additions and computes
    both figures with `cohort.py`.
  - **Failures.** It fails when either figure falls below 17,821/18,490 or 10,646/11,246, as
    exact integer comparisons, or when a cohort file is missing.
  - **Reported only.** These groups never count: `restored_tests`, the port's fork test files,
    the `tests` crate, and the upstream product, with per-crate deltas against the baseline's
    `per_crate`.
- **`gate_common.py`.** It loads and validates the dispositions file, merges in the baseline's
  own 34 dispositions, and parses the captures.
- **`test_gates.py`.** The self-tests, run with `python3 -I`.

**Unconfirmed entries.** An entry marked `"unconfirmed": true` does not excuse anything. A
requirement that only such an entry would satisfy fails as `unconfirmed`,
`rename_unconfirmed` or `move_unconfirmed`, and the failure names the entry. The primary
confirms an entry by setting the flag to `false`.

**Renames.** The gates match renames only through exact `{from, to}` pairs. They accept the old
identifier while the candidate still has it, so the unchanged baseline passes with the
committed file too.

#### The dispositions file

`tests/qualification/dispositions.json` holds entries from four places: the tasks' sections,
the expectation ledger, the rulings and the source. Entries by kind:

| Kind | Entries | Subjects |
|---|---:|---|
| `internals_replaced_equivalent` | 11 | 8 `tests/src` functions, each with its 1.0 replacement as the equivalent, and 0.16's three `groupware::tests` path tests, with `common::storage::dav::tests::*` as theirs |
| `subject_deleted` | 3 | 5 functions: `system::task`'s two retry helpers (ruling 73), `store_lookup_expire_all` (Task 2's harness), and the two retired R63 cases in `fence.rs` (part 6d, rulings 19 and 63) |
| `sel_only` | 1 | the `telemetry_tests` calls `metrics::test` and `tracing::test` (ruling 33) |

The other sections:
- **Renames.** Eight libtest pairs:
  - `calendar::sequence::tests::*` to `calendar::sequence_floor::tests::*`, five tests
    (ruling 25);
  - the two tests Task 2 moved to `utils::outbound::tests`;
  - `seal::tests::a_plaintext_archive_is_not_a_stub` to `a_plaintext_row_is_not_a_stub`.

  Four function pairs: `store_assert_is_empty_except_vaults` to
  `store_assert_is_empty_except_account_state` (commit `cee76c781`), and `fence.rs`'s
  `search_keys`, `SearchKeys::holds_term` and `SearchKeys::is_empty`, which moved to
  `utils/za.rs` (part 6d).
- **Selection moves.** Two:
  - ruling 53's seven DKIM tests, to a step built without `smtp/enterprise`;
  - `http`'s two `za_test` tests, to a step of their own with `test_mode`. `http/test_mode`
    can't be listed in the workspace step ("`UNIT_TEST_FEATURES` for 1.0").
- **Cohort moves.** One: `sequence.rs` to `sequence_floor.rs` (ruling 25).
- **Cohort additions.** Eight fork runtime files the port added:
  - `common/src/za/metadata.rs`;
  - `dav/src/common/propfind/za.rs`;
  - `groupware/src/calendar/seal/container.rs`;

**The source census.** 20 baseline `tests/src` functions that executed at 0.16.27 have no
identity in the current tree.
- 16 are covered by the entries above.
- The other 4 are covered by the baseline's own SEL-only file dispositions: `system/archiving.rs`
  and `system/tenant.rs`.

The only baseline libtest identifiers absent from the tree are the renamed ones and the moved
or replaced modules above. The removed sub-test calls are the SEL-only and ruling-33 ones.

**Unconfirmed, 8 entries.** Each one waits for the primary:
- **Replacements inferred from the source.** The note names no replacement for these five:
  - `leak.rs`'s `check_email_search` to `check_search`;
  - `leak.rs`'s `metadata_key` to `metadata_row_key`;
  - `leak.rs`'s `write_metadata` to `write_metadata_row`;
  - `fence.rs`'s `delete_event` to `utils::za::delete_event_records`;
  - `fence.rs`'s `wait_for_the_unindex` to `utils::za::wait_for_index_queue`.
- **One rename.** The `seal` test's rename is inferred from the diff.
- **Two selection moves.** Task 8 hasn't named either step. The configuration names
  `test.yml__unit-tests__smtp-unit-tests-without-enterprise` and
  `test.yml__unit-tests__http-unit-tests` are proposals, and each move's `to_command` is the
  command that produces its capture.

#### Self-tests

`python3 -I scripts/tests/test_gates.py`: 28 offline tests pass in about 4 seconds.

- **The strict rule.** Synthetic exports of a synthetic `tests/src` file go through
  `strict_rule.py functions`. Each of these fails with its step and count:
  - a body that ran but skipped its last statement;
  - a future built but never awaited;
  - an omitted synchronous call;
  - a loop with no iteration;
  - a helper's early return;
  - a tail block that was never polled;
  - a loss in one step only.

  The unchanged case passes. Renames and dispositions excuse a failure only when they are
  confirmed and their equivalents hold.
- **The listings.** A candidate built from the committed baseline's own records passes, with an
  empty dispositions file and with the committed one. It covers 795 tests, 27 configurations,
  272 call-steps, 3,833 helper-steps, 24 tail-steps and 14 loop-steps. Each of these fails:
  - a missing test;
  - a test dropped from one CI step;
  - a newly ignored test;
  - an empty selector;
  - a missing capture;
  - a dropped restored module (`common::auth::oauth::token::tests`, all 10 tests);
  - an ignored test that now runs;
  - `za::setup::test` lost in `za-tests-fs-blob` only;
  - an unrun `imap::body_structure::test` or `webdav::cal_itip::test`;
  - an unconfirmed or uncaptured selection move.

  The `sequence_floor` renames pass only with the dispositions file.
- **The coverage gate.** The baseline's exact fractions pass. One line below the full
  threshold fails, and so does one line below the runtime-only threshold, each while a
  restored test file adds 50,000 covered lines. A missing cohort file and an uncovered
  addition also fail, and excluded files don't count.

With `--real DIR`, two more tests feed both gates the 0.16.27 raw inputs as the candidate:
- the 105 captures;
- `strict_rule.py functions` over the 19 step exports;
- `merged.json` and the 5b914ddb5 tree.

Both pass. The coverage gate prints 17821/18490 and 10646/11246, with every per-crate delta
at zero.

#### For part 7d

- **Capture the listings.** Capture every CI step and every ignored-only configuration under
  its baseline name. Use the 1.0 commands, plus the two move targets' `to_command`.
  Then run `strict_rule.py functions` on the 19 step exports and feed both gates.
- **Confirm or correct the 8 unconfirmed entries.** Until then, the gate fails on them by
  design.
- **Expect more dispositions.** The candidate's real exports may show helpers that no longer
  run because a ported test lost its caller, such as `system::task`'s remaining helpers or
  `telemetry` helpers. The gate names each one, and it then needs an entry.

## Conflict ledger

**The real merge, 2026-10-30.** In the worktree, on `stalwart-1-upgrade` at `9601038de`,
`git merge --no-commit --no-ff 23b69755c` (v1.0.1) reported 134 conflicted paths, as
predicted.

**Restored.** Right after it, `git checkout HEAD -- tests/ .github/workflows/test.yml`
restored 542 paths from the fork side, and `git diff HEAD -- tests/ .github/workflows/test.yml`
is empty afterwards. The 542 are:
- 520 files the merge deleted under `tests/` without a conflict;
- the 21 modify/delete conflicts under `tests/`;
- `.github/workflows/test.yml`, which Task 8 rewrites.

`tests/` now holds 604 files: the 599 at `9a7574203`, plus `tests/qualification/`'s five.

**Prediction check.** Outside `tests/`, the workflow and `docs/`, the working tree matches
the dry merge's tree `df7eab1ff` except for conflict-marker labels
(`git diff df7eab1ff -I '<marker lines>'` is empty). So the inventory's deleted-path list
and its inline-test-module list stand as written.

**Workspace members.** The merge also removes `tests` from the root workspace's `members`
(see "Merge prediction"). Task 2 adds it back.

**Unresolved.** 112 conflicts remain: 1 add/add, 106 content, 5 modify/delete. Each is listed below with its owner from the hook
inventory's file groups. Resolving each is the owning task's port work, recorded here as it
happens.

**Committed as the raw merge** (Jay, 2026-10-30: "Commit now and after every task"; this
replaces ruling 4's uncommitted state). The merge commit holds every content and add/add
conflict with its markers in place. The five modify/delete paths outside `tests/` are kept
as the fork's file, because each holds hooks its owner ports to the replacement; the owner
deletes the file once they're ported. So the branch doesn't compile until the owners
resolve their files (Task 7's gate). Every later task ends with a commit.

| Path | Conflict | Owner | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Cargo.lock` | content | Task 2 (regenerated) | resolved (Task 2a): regenerated from v1.0.1's lockfile (`git show 23b69755c:Cargo.lock`) with `cargo metadata` once the manifests resolved. No v1.0.1 package changed version; `stalwart` is now `0.1.1-beta.1`; 72 packages were added: `vault`, `tests` and 70 crates that only `tests`' dev-dependencies pull (`jmap-client`, `testcontainers`, `rust-s3`, `ece`, `biscuit`, `serial_test`, `csv` and their dependencies). `cargo tree -d --workspace -e normal,build` lists the same 157 entries as v1.0.1's tree. With dev edges, the extra duplicates are internal to test-only clients: `quick-xml` 0.41, `reqwest` 0.12 and `hashbrown` 0.14 under `rust-s3`; `tungstenite` 0.29, `rand` 0.9 and `sha1` 0.10 under `jmap-client`; `darling` 0.24 under `testcontainers`. None sits at a server crate's API. |

| `crates/store/src/dispatch/lookup.rs` | content | Task 2 (lease home: Task 3/Task 4) | resolved (Task 2a): v1.0.1's file as supplied (both blocks). Row 2's purge skip is moot, not deferred. Evidence (paths in `v1.0.1-src/crates/`): v1.0.1 has no `purge_in_memory_store`, and `InMemoryStore` (`store/src/lib.rs:426-439`) has no data-store variant: it is `Sql`, `Local`, `Redis`, `Http`, `Static` and the SEL `Sharded`, and `Sql` answers `NotSupported` to key writes (`store/src/dispatch/lookup.rs:61` and the arms after it). So nothing sweeps expired keys out of the data store. Its other bulk deletions of data-store keys are: `Store::purge_store`, which only clears zero counters in `Shape::Counter` subspaces (`store/src/backend/{rocksdb,sqlite,foundationdb}/write.rs`); `danger_destroy_account` (`store/src/dispatch/store.rs:415-476`: the account's `Counter` kinds, its `Logs`, `Indexes`, `Property`, `Immutable` and `IndexProperty` prefixes, and its ACLs); the change-log truncation (`delete_changes` and `truncate_change_log`, `common/src/storage/transaction.rs:116-235`, `Logs`); the private-metadata cleanup (`common/src/storage/metadata/cleanup.rs:228-265`, an owner's private metadata and private logs); the telemetry purges (`TelemetrySpan`, `TelemetryMetric`); the reindex and search-queue clears (search subspaces); and the console's operator-driven range delete. Ruling 24's guarantee (only the asserting sweep clears a lease) therefore rests on Task 3's choice of a `Shape::Value` subspace and key range for `ValueClass::Any` that none of these reaches. `ZA_UID_LEASE_PREFIX` stays in `store/src/lib.rs` as the first byte of the lease key in it. |

## Expectation ledger

Filled from Task 2 onward: every changed assertion, early return, `cfg` skip or new
`#[ignore]` in a ported test, with the 1.0 change behind it.

| Test | Change | 1.0 change behind it |
|---|---|---|
| `utils::cheeky_hash::tests::test_cheeky_hash_all` | Its first six checks are rewritten for 1.0's layout: length, key bytes and boundary through `len`, `as_key` and `key_len`, and a `from_key_bytes` round trip in place of the `AsRef` check. The other seven are unchanged. | `utils/src/cheeky_hash.rs`: `CheekyHash([u8; HASH_SIZE + 1])` keeps the length byte last and stores up to 16 bytes directly; `as_bytes`, `payload`, `deserialize` and `AsRef<[u8]>` are gone. |
| `types::id::tests::parse_jmap_id` | `Id::from_str("p333333333333p333333333333")` is asserted to fail, where 0.16 asserted it parses. | `types/src/id.rs`: `from_str` is `STALWART.decode_u64`, which refuses a numeral longer than 13 symbols (`Error::Overflow`); 0.16 shifted the excess bits out. |
| `imap_proto::protocol::fetch::tests::serialize_fetch_data_item` | Its six ENVELOPE, BODY and BODYSTRUCTURE cases are removed; the BINARY, BODY[section], FLAGS and INTERNALDATE cases stay. Their equivalent goes to the imap crate (Task 5 handoff). | `imap-proto/src/protocol/fetch.rs`: `DataItem::{Envelope, Body, BodyStructure}`, `Envelope` and `BodyPart` are gone; `imap/src/op/fetch/structure.rs` writes those items from the message metadata. |
| `imap_proto::protocol::list::tests::serialize_list_item` | The rev2 expectations of the two non-ASCII names lose their `OLDNAME` tag. | `imap-proto/src/protocol/list.rs:263-266`: a rev2 or UTF8=ACCEPT client gets the name as stored; OLDNAME is kept for RENAME (RFC 9051 5.1, 6.3.9.7). |
| `imap_proto::protocol::select::tests::serialize_select` | The rev2 LIST line of the second case loses its `OLDNAME` tag. | The same change. |
| `imap_proto::parser::select::tests::parse_select_objectid` | The object id `abc` becomes `bcd` in the inputs and expectations of three cases, which keep testing that MAILBOXID and ACCOUNTID values are parsed. 0.16's three inputs stay as cases of their own with 1.0's outcome: MAILBOXID `abc` is dropped (ACCOUNTID `xyz` kept). | `imap-proto/src/protocol/mod.rs:80-85`: `ObjectId::decode_id` refuses an id that starts with the zero symbol `a` followed by more symbols (a non-canonical encoding); the parser then drops the MAILBOXID. |
| `imap_proto::parser::fetch::tests::parse_fetch` | `BODY[MIME]` becomes `BODY[1.MIME]`, with `Section::Part { num: 1 }` before `Section::Mime`. 0.16's input stays as a case of its own, asserted to be refused (`parse_fetch` returns an error). | `imap-proto/src/parser/fetch.rs:374-377`: MIME needs a part number before it, as RFC 3501's section-spec says. |
| `imap_proto::parser::search::tests::parse_search` | Two expectations with `NOT (…)` gain an `And` after `Not` and an `End` closing it. | `imap-proto/src/parser/search.rs` (`parse_filters`): a parenthesised group merges into its parent only when the parent is AND, so NOT applies to the whole conjunction. |
| `imap_proto::utf7::tests::utf7_decode` | Decodes with `IMAP.lenient()`. With the strict decoder, five of the eleven cases fail. | 0.16's in-tree codec moved to `encodify::utf7::IMAP`, whose default decoder refuses non-canonical input; 1.0 decodes mailbox names with `IMAP.lenient()` (`imap-proto/src/receiver.rs:752-763`), as 0.16's codec did. |
| `dav_proto::parser::tokenizer::tests::test_tokenizer` | A `Token::Content` arm panics. | `dav-proto/src/parser/mod.rs`: the new `Token::Content`, returned only in raw-content mode, which the test never turns on. |
| `common::storage::dav::tests::*` (0.16's `groupware::tests`) | Written against `canonical_uri_path` and `canonical_path_segment`. The `is_uri_segment` assertions are gone, the stable case checks `dav_file_name` too, and `a/b.txt` is asserted to have no URI path (`InvalidCharacter`). | `common/src/storage/dav/mod.rs:143-149`, `:234-245`: 1.0 always encodes a stored name and canonicalises a request path by decoding it; a segment that decodes to `/` is refused (`decode_segment`). |
| `telemetry::telemetry_tests` | The `metrics::test` and `tracing::test` calls are removed; both modules keep compiling. | Ruling 33: without a licence 1.0 refuses `Metric` and `Trace` (`assert_enterprise_object`, `jmap/src/registry/mod.rs:18-42`), the only objects those two assert on; no test licence (`core.enterprise` is `None`). |
| `telemetry::alerts::test` | Processing the alerts, with its email and event assertions, is removed. Creating the alerts, the in-memory metric checks (OSS) and the cleanup stay. | Ruling 33: `process_alerts` (`common/src/enterprise/alerts.rs:33-34`) returns nothing while `core.enterprise` is `None`. |
| `system::authorization::test` | The masked email is no longer created, so the visibility, update, destroy and admin-query checks run on `PublicKey` only. | Ruling 33: `MaskedEmail` is refused without a licence (`assert_enterprise_object`). |
| `system::directory::test` | The masked email's creation, its `account_id_from_email` and `rcpt_resolve` checks and its destroy are removed. | The same. |
| `system::delivery::test` | The masked emails' creation, their domain and quota refusals, their two VRFY checks and their destroy are removed. The two spam cases that were delivered to them (GTUBE to Junk, the CardDAV `card-exists` override) are delivered to john's alias `john.doe@example.org` instead. | The same. The spam filtering is OSS, so those cases stay. |
| `smtp::inbound::antispam::antispam` | The `llm` case is removed from its case list. | Ruling 33: the LLM analysis needs a licence (AI models are off while `core.enterprise` is `None`). |
| `email::message::za::seal::tests::*` | Rewritten for 1.0's metadata row: the stub is a `MetadataRow` whose structure holds one binary root part at offsets (0, bundle, bundle + sealed) instead of 0.16's empty part at zero offsets; there is no `rcvd_attach`, so the arrival-time and attachment-bit checks are gone; the moved, tampered, bad-policy and unknown-wrap cases re-encode the bundle under a new row instead of editing `raw_headers`; round trips compare the opened row's structure and header section with the full row. A new case, `a_forged_bundle_does_not_open_as_a_row`, covers the checked decode. | `email/src/message/metadata/{mod,row}.rs`: `MessageMetadata` is a flat structure with no `raw_headers`, `rcvd_attach` or `contents`; the header block lives in the row's header section; the arrival time and the attachment keywords live in `MessageData`. |

## Task 1 check

Part B's answer to the brief's check, item by item:

- **Every original runtime hook has an owner and a disposition.**
  - Yes for all 485 hooks under `crates/` (483 diff hunks plus ruling 28's two C3 hooks
    in `http/src/auth/authenticate.rs`, owned by Task 3) and the 3 in the root
    `Cargo.toml`. Each sits in one file row with an owner, and each (b), (c) and (d) hook
    has its own row with a v1.0.1 site. The 42 (a) hooks merge clean.
  - The pending-item flag in `groupware/src/calendar/mod.rs` collides with 1.0.1's
    destroy flag; ruling 30 moves it (Task 4).
  - The auto-merged Docker tag change in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` goes to Task 9 (C11).
  - `Cargo.lock` is regenerated by Task 2.
  - The llm hook's disposition is "SEL-only, not ported".
  - The disposition is not final for the hooks named in "Findings for the primary": the
    UID lease's storage, the SEQUENCE module clash, DAV sync paging, the fork-written SEL
    snippet and R62's scans. Each has an owner but needs a decision first.
- **Every removed index, metadata or test-support path is accounted for.**
  - Index: `services/src/task_manager/index.rs`, `common/src/storage/index.rs`, and
    `store/src/search/bm_u32.rs` and `bm_u64.rs`.
  - Metadata: `email/src/message/metadata.rs` and `types/src/dead_property.rs`.
  - Test support: the 541 `tests/` files and `.github/workflows/test.yml` (restored), the
    114 dav-proto fixtures (Task 2 restores them with their tests), and the root
    workspace's `tests` member (Task 2 re-adds it).
  - Test hooks: the 144 upstream `test_mode` sites and the 3 non-module `cfg(test)` items
    that 1.0.1 regated each have a 1.0.1 counterpart and a proposed disposition in
    "Upstream test hooks (for Task 2)".
  - The design does not name the dav-proto fixtures or the workspace member; both are new
    here.
- **Every removed test is accounted for.**
  - The 541 `tests/` files are restored by the fork-side checkout.
  - All 132 upstream inline test modules are listed above with their 1.0 counterparts.
    That is 282 active tests outside the SEL crates, plus the SEL-only `scim` and
    `scim-proto` modules, whose 183 tests are dispositioned.
  - The fork's 7 unit modules in upstream files (10 tests) survive in the merge result.
    They must be kept through the conflict resolution, under their current names: their
    libtest identifiers are in the baseline.
- **No data migration task appears in the scope.** Correct.
  - The plan's Tasks 1 to 9 hold none, and Task 2 accepts the removal of
    `crates/migration` "without inventing a fork migration replacement".
  - v1.0.1 ships `resources/scripts/migrate_v016.py`. It is out of scope under "fresh
    stores only".
